## 2026-09-23 (desk): F315, telling same-magnitude weapons apart by IR cell

The S56 resolver matched on magnitude alone, so any two magnitude-9 weapons in one loadout read "A / B". The latch
already held the protocol and subtype, and most magnitude-9 weapons sit on different cells: the USP-S on (0,3), the
Breacher on (5,0). The roster and catalogue now carry each weapon's cells, and the phone matches cell + magnitude,
then cell, then magnitude, each on the loadout and then the catalogue. Only the Assault Rifle and Energy Rifle share
(0,0) at 9; MC's `--distinct-weapon-cells` flag (off) moves the Energy Rifle to (0,2), the one free proto-0 cell,
with a fn-1 `$SIR` row. No bench has sent a (0,2) word; `bench-2026-09-24.md` 4.10 does, and gates the default.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): BLE reliability tools for F297, F269 and F270

Built three bench tools on `feat/ble-reliability`; no hardware was touched, so there is no measurement yet.
`connect-metrics` (F297) connects from cold N times and logs link time, first-attempt success and any headset or
BLE drop inside 30 s. It uses the laptop's radio, so its numbers describe the gun and headset, not the phone.
`raw-bytes` (F269) writes exact segments or a zero-gap stream under one writer lock, logs each write after it
completes, and counts replies from the first write on (a review fix: the first version missed a `$PONG` that
arrived mid-stream). F270's write-with-response flag ships off in `brxlink.js` and `ble.py`. A two-round polish
loop then fixed: a response chunk now waits for its answer; `raw-bytes` refuses bytes outside a frame and ends with a
liveness `$PING`; a failed send on a dropped link counts as a drop; and the deny check now catches `$FACTORY*` and
`$ DPLAY,*`, in `protocol.py` and in the phone's `frameCommand`, which both had the gap.

pool in live, lobby, briefing, and Night Ops HUDs. A scanner config with one team-scoped station derives

The session SQLite envelopes show station respawns followed by camper deaths 2.7–4.2 seconds later, beyond the
configured 2-second station protection window. The phone bundle writes `$TMP` protection, maps the trigger

while typed or remembered URLs remain the fallback.

## 2026-09-18 (day, desk) — the second emitter is real, and the weapons catalogue stopped lying in four places

Weapons session. No hardware of my own; every measurement below came from the playtest and brx-ltm lanes and is
credited to them. What I own is the pricing, the guards, and four counting faults that were mine.

**The finding the day turned on.** `$WEAP` **t1 is `WeaponIRSource`**, and at 2 the shot leaves the gun emitter AND
the shooter's headset, with **t12 carrying the second word's damage**. Measured: a Callsign capture (cap30) put a
Shotgun pull at 45 then 70, 88 ms apart on the same sensor, a kill. Then measured again on a victim held at 250
armour, reading the pool BETWEEN the two words: Shotgun 250 → 205 → 135, Plasma Sniper 126 → 101 → 21. So each word
carries its own magnitude and they stack. Three weapons do it: Shotgun, Plasma Sniper, Rocket Launcher.

**What that meant for us.** `resolve()` emits each weapon's captured frame and writes only the tokens we balance,
and t12 was never one of them. So we priced t5 and shipped Battle Company's t12 untouched. The Plasma Sniper was
the worst of it: we cut its t5 from 80 to 25 deliberately, while it kept throwing an 80 nobody counted. t12 is now a
declared `wire.headset_dmg`, and a captured t12 with no declared price is a **refusal**, not a default.

**Armour Piercing was smuggling the second word.** The perk writes its priced damage into t5 and `_rekey` points the
WHOLE frame at the AP cell, so both words land on fn 2, straight past armour and shields. An AP Shotgun delivered
15 + 20 = 35 to bare health for the price of 15. No test covered a weapon declaring both fields. AP now zeroes the
second word.

**Four counting faults, one root cause: counting rows in a file rather than weapons in a game.**
The site advertised **25 weapons** (it is 15) and offered a MELEE class nobody can pick. The phone's perk step
demanded **7** perks against a build shipping 5. The arsenal pools said **11 OF 13** when the primary pool is 11 of
15 and the secondary 13 of 15, which are genuinely different because a weapon that cannot kill is a legal secondary
and an illegal primary. And the home page still said **22 weapons, eleven pickable**. All four now derive from the
shipped artefact rather than from a typed number.

**`support` had become a dumping ground**, holding a .50 anti-materiel rifle beside a smoke launcher. Four weapons
moved to the role they play. The follow-on caught by Tony reading the live page: their `tags` did not move with
their `role`, and **tags are what loadout policy filters on**, so a host excluding support weapons would silently
have banned the AMR, Suppressor, Energy Rifle and Charge Rifle. ⚠️ My first guard for that was useless: it checked
that a weapon carries its role's tag, and the AMR's fault was an EXTRA tag, so it passed with the bug planted back
in. The rule that works is semantic: **`support` means the weapon cannot kill**, so a lethal weapon wearing it is
the bug by definition.

**Crits, proven and then used.** `$WEAP` t6 is a crit chance the GUN rolls: t6 = 20 gave 9 in 64 (14.1%), t6 = 50
gave 54 in 119 (45.4%), a crit is the magnitude **x1.5 truncated**, and **`$HIR` token 6 flips**, so the victim's
node can see the proc. Nothing used it. Three weapons now do, paid for in damage so the average holds: Burst Rifle
40% at 10 damage, AMR 30% at 21, Toxin Rifle 15% at 8 where the crit is the **poison proc** rather than a buff.

⚠️ **Hits-to-kill stays the GUARANTEED number and crits only ever beat it**, because the compiler and the public page
both rest on "a weapon is a number of hits". A test asserts `htk == ceil(pool / damage_per_pull())` for every weapon
so nobody folds an expected value into the published one.

**Tony's question found a flaw in my design.** Asked how many trigger PULLS the Burst Rifle takes to kill, the answer
was four, and at my first crit chance of 25% it was still four **95% of the time**. Hits-to-kill is the wrong unit
for a weapon firing three rounds a pull; a player would never have noticed it crits. At 40% it is three pulls about
a quarter of the time. The AMR has no such problem: one pull is one round, and four normal hits plus one crit is
84 + 31 = 115 exactly, so five hits kill 83% of the time and six when unlucky.

**F276, filed and closed the same hour, and worth keeping for the negative.** The V4_30/V4_31 receive path drops an
IR word identical to one taken within 159 ms. Our Shotgun shipped t5 20 / t12 20, same key, no crit, 88 ms apart:
exactly that shape, which would have made it a 6-pull weapon against a catalogue publishing 3. **Benched: five
single pulls, two `$HIR` of 20 every time, 60 to 75 ms apart, and on one pull both landed on sensor 0 as
byte-identical frames and BOTH registered.** The block does not apply. The insurance split (21/19) was reverted and
the equal-pricing guard DELETED, because a guard enforcing a disproven rule is a trap for whoever believes it. ⚠️
Nothing we owned could have caught the risk either way: every prior measurement was of UNEQUAL words.

**The self-expiring exemption expired, which is the only time I have watched one work.** `KNOWN_DOMINANCE` briefly
held `(amr, shotgun)`: repairing the Shotgun's range cost it the only thing separating it from a slow rifle. Rather
than buy a lead with a magazine buff nobody believed in, the pair was named with an assertion that fails when it
stops applying. The AMR's nerf to pay for its crits stopped it dominating, the assertion failed on cue, and the
entry is gone.

**Corrections I was given, both right.** A minimum write interval is NOT the cure for the recoil write budget, it is
the CAUSE of F259: a deferred write lands composed against stale counts and its `$AMMO` hands a round back. And a
bench result describes the frame you SENT: a lane armed Callsign's captured 45/70 and reported it as our Shotgun,
which ships 20/20. The rule is now written down: arm from `resolve()`, never from a capture.

**Shipped besides:** the public `/arsenal` page, generated from the catalogue so it cannot rot, linked from the home
page, with art for every weapon including the hidden ones. And all 25 descriptions rewritten as player copy, with
the balance changelog moved to a `notes` field the UI never receives, after Tony read the Suppressor's card quoting
a dated magazine change and a dominance argument at somebody choosing a gun.

## 2026-09-17 (night, desk) — the doc-rot apply, two code reviews, and the CI guard nobody could satisfy

**Why.** Tony asked whether the issues, bench sheets, followups and handoff were accurate, then for a
maintainability and DRY review. Six lanes ran: four applying a doc-rot review, two reviewing the code.

**The one public-facing error.** The site published each weapon's Reserve column from `$WEAP` t17. The
player carries **t40**, which is exactly half of it (F207, proven on four weapons on 2026-09-16), so
every weapon on the public site advertised twice the spare rounds it has. `site/lib/data.mjs` and its
test now read t40, and the manual's per-weapon numbers are halved with it: the Energy Launcher carries
three spare rounds, not six. The manual used to call the question unsettled, which was true when it was written and is not
something a reader of a facts page should have to carry.

**Retractions that had reached one file of a pair.** `protocol/brx-protocol.md` leads and the manual
follows, and four corrections had only landed in the manual or in neither: t37/t38 are not an "enable
pair" (t38 alone switches overheat on, t37 is a charge weapon's tap damage, F229); t2 is
`gunRangeOutdoor` and t41 is a measured null outdoors (F231, F234); t40 is the player's reserve; and
`$GSET` t7 compiles as 0, not the capture's 50.

**Six sheets whose work ran are archived**, and one that the review called dead is not: the flash-control
sheet still has open rungs (L10-L14) and four living files cite its appendix. A lane that re-verifies
before it obeys is worth more than a lane that obeys.

**What the code reviews found.** No Critical on either. The real ones: the node's "the writer stands
down" set was re-typed at eight sites with no two agreeing, and is now one table whose equivalence was
proven over all 8192 input states rather than argued; two overheat readings with different windows were
both shipped in `state()`, so the HUD's bar could sit hot for 19 s after the word cleared; Mission
Control decided "is this match in play" in twelve places; and the console's mock shipped an FFA frag
limit of 15 against the server's 25, so `?mock`, the surface UI work is verified against, demonstrated a
cap the server never sends.

**A finding the review got half wrong.** The recap's local `mmss` was called a drifted copy of
`fmtClock`. It is not: a countdown and a duration are read differently ("01:05 left" against "1:36
held"), and the KOTH gate caught the change the moment a possession line read "01:36". The fix is two
named helpers, `fmtClock` and `fmtDuration`, not one.

**CI.** 15 of the last 19 runs on main failed on `test_site_shots`. The manifest records the git tree
hash of `webapp/mc/src` and `app/src`, so any commit that moves a UI leaves the shots stale, and the
guard then fails **every later commit** until a person runs the capture. No CI job could fix it: the
capture needs a built `app/www`, a built `webapp/mc/dist` and a real browser, and the run had none of
the three. Tony's decision: CI regenerates and commits them itself. The new `site-shots` job asks the
manifest the same question the test asks, builds both UIs, captures with the Playwright build the
lockfile pins, and pushes as `github-actions[bot]`. It never forces: if a UI moved while it ran it goes
red and the run queued behind it produces the right shots. Its push step was proven against a sandbox
remote in four scenarios before it went near the repo. The staleness pair skips inside Actions only, so
the job that can fix the problem is the one that reports it.

## 2026-09-17 (night, desk) — the arsenal merge, one overheat reading, and the ammo gauge's real question

**The merge.** `origin/main` `f4e7e263` (the brx-weapons arsenal rework, A48) went into
`fix/playtest-2026-09-13` as `6d6a8d64`. Four files conflicted: `app/src/engine.js`,
`mcp/tests/test_stage_mirror.py`, `docs/FOLLOWUPS.md` and `docs/HANDOFF.md`.

**The one real conflict was heat, and both sides were right about different things.** Main added
`overheated()`, which reads one gun-wide number (`gunHeat`, `$ALCD` token 5) against `OVERHEAT_HEAT`.
This branch already tracked heat per slot with a staleness window (`_overheating()`, `heatBySlot`,
`_heatAt`). The per-slot reading wins for one measured reason: **a locked gun stops sending `$ALCD`**, so
a gun-wide 99 sits above the line forever and the accuracy writer stays stood down for the rest of the
life. `_overheating()` treats a reading older than `HEAT_STALE_MS` as untrustworthy and hands the case
to `poolStale`. So `overheated()`, `gunHeat` and `OVERHEAT_HEAT` are deleted, main's accuracy-writer test
now drives `_overheating()`, and the `overheated` pin left `KNOWN_UNMIRRORED` because the stage already
mirrors the per-slot rule. The test keeps driving a real `$ALCD` heat frame, not a flag: that is the only
shape that catches the original dead-guard bug (`overheatLocked`, which nothing ever set).

**F248, and the question the gauge actually asks.** A48 gave the catalogue `weapon_class`, and the HUD
picked its ammo gauge from `weapon_class === "energy"`. That class is wider than the old id match, so the
Rail Gun (class energy, magazine 2, one round per shot) read **"50%"** on a two-round magazine. The gauge
asks a different question than the wording does: *does one full charge cost more than one round?* The HUD
now draws the cell gauge and the reserve pills from `rounds_per_charge > 1` (`usesCellGauge()`), and
`weapon_class` keeps only the RELOAD-against-RECHARGE wording. The Energy Rifle's 300-round magazine
moves to the big-magazine bar with the same rule, which is the rule working, not scope creep.
`app/tools/screens.mjs` asserts both classes and the pre-A48 fallback; all three steps were proven to
fail against the old `hud.js` first.

**Found while fixing it (F250):** the night skin hides discrete pips (`[data-env="night"] .pips > i`) and
styles a thin bar in their place, but a pip weapon has no bar, so a Rail Gun or a sniper rifle shows the
number alone at night. It is older than the night-ops work and it is the same on every pip weapon.

**Bookkeeping.** F234 carried two unrelated items; the `connect()` alias bug is now **F249** and the row
is closed. Next free F id is **F254** by the end of the night (F251 and F252 from the DRY review, F253 from the polish review). `npm run test:all` at the merge: 1970 passed, one failure, which is the expected
`test_published_build` (the published APK reads 0.2.1 against the app's 0.3.0).

## 2026-09-17 (afternoon, garden, MacBook) — the range test: `t41` is inert outdoors and **`$WEAP` t2 `gunRangeOutdoor` is the emitted-power control**

> ⚠️ **MECHANISM SUPERSEDED 2026-09-18** — every measurement below stands; the reading of "emitted
> power" does not. The V4_31 disassembly shows `t2` sets the emitter's CARRIER FREQUENCY,
> `38000 - 125 * (100 - range)` Hz, while emitter power never moves with it. A low value detunes the
> word out of the receiver's band-pass near 38 kHz instead of shortening the beam, so the flat shelf
> is the pass-band and the knee near 31 belongs to the receiver. `t41` is inert outdoors because the
> firmware reads it only indoors and only when it is itself non-zero. Source of record:
> `protocol/brx-protocol.md`, the `$WEAP` row headed "range is a CARRIER FREQUENCY".

**Why.** Step 3 of [`../archive/bench-weapons-2026-09-17.md`](../archive/bench-weapons-2026-09-17.md), run off the MacBook per
the MacBook victim-gun handoff (spent, deleted in this commit). Shooter
`Tactix-E20D` (the advert prefix is not `Tactix`; only the four-character tail identifies a gun), victim `Tactix-3D4F` at 999 HP on the mark, Mac holding
the victim link. Bright sun throughout; both guns in **physical outdoor mode**. `$GSET` t2 = 0 (F162) in every head.
Capture: `~/.brx-mcp/captures/range-t41-2026-09-17-victim-b.jsonl` (2220 lines).

**⚠ CONDITIONS, and the biggest caveat on every number below.** About an hour outdoors, **very hot**, and **the sun
moved through the session**. The victim's headset — the receiving dome — **sat in direct sun the entire time**, while
the shooter's gun moved in and out of direct sun. F162 already says outdoor IR reception is light-sensitive, so this is
a first-order confound, not a footnote: every threshold measured below is for a receiver in the worst lighting it will
ever see, and the session's own baseline drifted as the sun moved.

The tell is in the miss pattern. Tony: *"sometimes id miss 5-6 shots in a row and then land 3-4 in a row."* Streaks
that long are not per-shot randomness — something slowly varying was moving the threshold under us (sun angle on the
dome, heat, the gun's own shading). **Tony's read is that range is clearly affected**, and the streakiness is why the
counted percentages below understate the dial: a run that lands in a bad streak scores near zero at a setting that
works fine a minute later. Treat every sub-30 number as a lower bound, and re-run the ladder with the dome shaded
before anything ships.

**Method.** Two `$WEAP` slots differing only in the token under test, each with its own damage so the victim's `$HIR`
magnitude names the slot (1 vs 21), and its own fire sound so the shooter can hear which is live. Hit counts are `$HIR`
events; shots fired are read from the mag walk when the shooter stayed inside BLE range. **A reload TOPS UP the mag
without discarding the partial** (`$BUT,2` took 28 → 32 and drew exactly 4 from reserve), so
`fired = start + reserve_drawn − current` is exact.

### Part 1 — `t41` (`gunRangeIndoor`) does nothing in outdoor mode

Slot 0: t41 = 5, damage 1, `G03`. Slot 1: t41 = 75, damage 21, `R01`. Everything else identical, `t21`/`t22` both 100
so the F230 accuracy walk on `E20D` could not contaminate it. 5 stock / 5 low / 5 stock at each mark.

| Distance | stock (first) | low | stock (last) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 m | 6 hits / 5 pulls | **5/5** | 6 hits / 5 pulls |
| 10 m | 6/6 | **5/5** | 5/5 |
| 20 m | 5/5 | **5/5** | 5/5 |
| 40 m | 5/5 | **5/5** | 5/5 |
| ~200 ft | 6 hits / 5 pulls | **7 hits / 5 pulls** | 6/6 |

Mag arithmetic afterwards, which is the measurement that matters: the low slot never reloaded (reserve sat at 384) and
went 32 → 5, so **27 low rounds fired, 27 low hits — zero misses from 3 m to 200 ft**. The stock slot fired 57 and hit
55. **Q15's t41 half is a null: t41 = 5 behaves exactly like t41 = 75 at every distance, and if anything slightly
cleaner.** Every hit in the whole run read sensor **0** (front dome) — not one gun-body hit, even at 2 m, which is
cleaner than F228 predicts.

**F170 gets its missing measurement for free:** that head was **hosted** (written over BLE from the Mac) with `$GSET`
t2 = 0, outdoor mode, bright sun, and the stock slot landed **12 hits at ~200 ft**. That is the hosted t2=0 far-mark
result the 2026-09-13 handoff never recorded. → **F170 closed.**

### Part 2 — the token that does work: **t2 = `gunRangeOutdoor`**

Found by reading the APK field order (`callsign-extract/protocol-classes.md`): `slotType, iRSource, gunRangeOutdoor,
primaryDamageType, primaryPowerType, primaryDamage, …`. Aligned against the bench-proven anchors (t5 = damage,
t14 = fire rate) that puts `gunRangeOutdoor` at **t2** — the position `weapmap.py` labels `(scale/const)` precisely
because it never moved in the two frames the map was derived from. It is a live field in the shipped catalogue:
**every gun reads t2 = 100, melee reads 90** (t41 is 75/20). **We spent the whole first run varying the INDOOR token
while the guns ran in OUTDOOR mode, with the live token pinned at 100 on both slots.**

Ladder, all outdoor mode, bright sun, same two guns (counted where noted, otherwise Tony's field read):

| t2 | result |
|---|---|
| **5** | **0 hits from 38 shots** — 15 at 3 m and 23 at point-blank, muzzle on the dome. Counted. Dead everywhere |
| **13** | 3/8 at 15 m, **precise aim**. Genuine attenuation, not aim error |
| **18** | 1/8 at 25 m; ~2/8 at 10 m |
| **20 / 22** | 22: 3/8 at 25 m, ~2/8 at 10 m |
| **25** | 3 m: 16/32 then **0/32** on the next mag. 10 m: 15/32. Counted |
| **26** | landing consistently at 15 m |
| **31** | lands, "more inconsistent, inconsistency starts earlier" |
| **32** | 10 m: 15/32 (47%). Counted. Worse at 20 m |
| **35** | too weak at 10 m |
| **37 / 41** | indistinguishable from each other; both land at 40 m on good aim |
| **40** | "mostly fine, over 50%" at 10 and 20 m |
| **42** | reliable at 30 m, still hits at 40 m; **cone at 30 m barely different from the t2=100 control** |
| **45** | lands consistently at 40 m on good aim; some dead-on shots still miss |
| **50** | 3 m 11/11 · 10 m 21/23 · 20 m 100% · 30 m 90%+ · 40 m ~33%. Counted at 3 and 10 m |
| **100** | ~100% everywhere to 200 ft |

**Shape.** A floor below which nothing arrives at any distance (5 is dead against the dome), a real transition roughly
**13 → 26**, and then a long flat shelf: everything from ~31 to 100 behaves the same at every distance we can pace out,
because the native envelope is far longer than the garden. **The afternoon was spent sweeping 31→50, which is the flat
part** — hence the early and wrong reading that t2 was a near-binary gate.

**Does it fence a weapon to a distance? Not proven either way, and the session could not answer it.** At 42 the cone
at 30 m was barely different from the 100 control, and 37/41/42/45 all land at 40 m with careful aim — which argues the
shelf is flat and t2 reads as aim tolerance up there. Below the transition it is genuine attenuation (13 gave 3/8 at
15 m with *precise* aim). But the whole ladder was shot into a dome in direct sun with the light changing, and the
miss streaks say the threshold was moving during the run. **Tony's field judgement is that range is clearly affected.**
The honest position: t2 is unambiguously an emitted-power control with a floor and a transition band; whether it can be
tuned to fence a weapon at a chosen distance is UNRESOLVED and needs the shaded re-run. What it is already good for is
a venue-wide "super indoor" master: turn every gun down together and the whole game tightens.

**Method limit reached.** At 18 and 22 the numbers stopped being monotonic — 22 gave 3/8 at 25 m and 2/8 at 10 m. With
8-shot groups and the first-two-shots effect below, values under ~26 cannot be separated. The shotgun value is between
22 and 31 and needs a better run sheet, not more volleys.

### Side findings

- **THE FIRST TWO TRIGGER PULLS ARE WEAKER.** Tony, repeatedly and unprompted: "first two trigger pulls will usually
  not have the power and then 3+ have better chance of hitting." Every group today was 5 or 10 rounds, so the first two
  of every group were handicapped — which inflates the miss rate at every setting and hits the low values hardest
  (a weak shot still clears threshold at t2=100, not at 25). This is the likely explanation for "16/32 then 0/32 at the
  same mark". **Every short-group number in the ladder above is suspect by roughly this much.** → F232.
- **The gun holds at least FOUR `$WEAP` slots.** `$WEAP,0..3` all acked with their own `$ALCD` and independent `$AMMO`.
  **ALT only cycles two**, so slots 2-3 are configured but unreachable without a `$BMAP` change. → F233.
- **`$HIR` t4 carried the shooter's team correctly** — `$TID,1` read back as `shooter_team 1` on every one of several
  hundred hits, with `$TID` sent BEFORE `$SPAWN` and `$GSET` t1 (friendly fire) = 0, cross-team. That is the opposite
  of the playtest's 38 hits that all read team 0, and it does not support F206's leading suspect (`$SPAWN` wiping
  `$TID`). Not chased — noted for whoever takes F206.
- **Double-fire at 100 ms.** Four extra rounds landed 106-120 ms behind their pull, one `t14` interval: at a 100 ms
  cycle a normal-feeling pull sometimes puts two rounds out. Feeds S28.
- **BLE range, Mac to gun: about 20 m.** The shooter dropped the link at 20 m every time, which is why shot counts
  exist for the close marks only.
- **The victim died mid-volley once** (983 → 0 on 47 rifle hits) and every later shot silently registered nothing.
  Reset HP between rows; a dead victim reads exactly like a range failure.
- **`connect()` refuses to re-open an alias whose link has dropped** ("already connected", then "Service Discovery has
  not been performed yet" on the next write) — `disconnect` then `connect` every time. Cost several minutes across the
  session. → F234.

**Teardown.** Victim: `$CLEAR` then `$SIR,0,0,,1,0,0,1,,*`, left armed and hittable. **The shooter went out of range
before its teardown landed** — it still carries the t2 18/22 test slots. Its `$SIR` table was never cleared so it is
not in the F11 state, but it needs a re-arm before real use.

---

## 2026-09-12 (bench procedure, NOT YET RUN) — B6/F162: does `$WEAP` t41 buy outdoor range, and by how much

**Why.** Tonight's field session (`bug-dossier-2026-09-12.md` B6): Tony could not register a hit at
30-40 ft outside; point blank worked. MC ships `$WEAP` t41 (`gunRangeIndoor`) at 75 for **every**
weapon regardless of venue (`weapons.json` `rng:75`; `compile.py` never raised it) — the outdoor flag
today only changes `$VOL` and `$GSET` t2. This entry is the bench procedure to close FOLLOWUPS
**F162**, not a result — nobody has run it yet. (Extended 2026-09-13 with **Runs C, D and E** — `$GSET`
t3 `gunLaserRegion`, the ALT-hold identity question, and `$IRTX` — and with the `DRIVE_IO_MODE` gate
in `mc/compile.py` that emits C's and E's frames. Runs A and B below are unchanged.)

**⚠ CRITICAL CONSTRAINT, read before touching a gun.** Whether `$WEAP` t41 changes emitted IR range
AT ALL is **UNTESTED** (`protocol/brx-protocol.md` ~L272, "t41 ... whether it changes emitted range
is untested"). Q15's one prior positive (`weapon-design.md` §5 U2, 2026-08-26: t41=100 killed at max
indoor distance, t41=5 zeroed — but at CONTAMINATED rig readings, so it does not count as a clean
result either way) is indoor and about REDUCING range, not extending it. **Do not ship a value out of
this sweep into `compile.py` on a hunch** — `mc/compile.py`'s `RANGE_ENV_OVERRIDE` is deliberately
staged as a no-op (see that file) until a number comes back from the steps below. A null result
("t41 does nothing to range") is a completely acceptable, useful answer — it means the fix is
elsewhere (gunLaserRegion, an aperture, or an honest "outdoor teams only" venue note).

### Setup

Two guns (headsets powered and linked), a MEASURED outdoor lane with 40+ ft of clear line of sight
(a park path, a long driveway — anywhere Tony can tape or pace out four marks). No MC, no phone: this
is raw BLE frames straight to both guns, same tool class as `bench-super-indoor-2026-09-07.md`.

1. Power-cycle both guns and headsets (screamers after ~a day powered, `FOLLOWUPS.md` §9 preflight).
2. Tape or pace four marks from the TARGET gun's position: **10 ft, 20 ft, 30 ft, 40 ft**. Keep the
   target gun's aim and height fixed for the whole session — only the shooter moves.
3. Connect to both over BLE (brx MCP tools; `alias` is your choice, used below):
   - `mcp__brx__connect(address=<shooter's BLE address>, alias="shooter")`
   - `mcp__brx__connect(address=<target's BLE address>, alias="target")`

### Arm the TARGET (once per `$GSET` t2 setting — see the matrix; otherwise leave it alone)

The target just needs to be alive, on a different team than the shooter, and able to interpret the
shooter's AR shot (proto 0 / subtype 0 — the same cell `tutorial_frames()` uses for exactly this
reason). `mcp__brx__send_batch(alias="target", gap_ms=150, commands=[...])`:

```
$CLEAR,*
$START,*
$VOL,30,0,*
$GSET,0,0,1,0,1,0,50,1,*
$PSET,1,0,45,70,70,50,,H44,JAD,V33,V3I,V3C,V3G,V3E,V37,H06,H55,H13,H21,H02,U15,W71,A10,*
$TID,3,*
$SIR,0,0,,1,0,0,1,,*
$SPAWN,,*
$BMAP,0,0,,,,,*
```

(Target's own `$GSET` t2 does not need to move — outdoor mode is a per-emitter setting on the
*shooter*, per `compile.py`'s `outdoor=config["environment"] == "outdoor"` wiring. Leave the target at
indoor/`0` for the whole session unless a control specifically calls for changing it.)

### Arm the SHOOTER with one t41 value, fire, read the target

The shooter's `$WEAP` frame below is the real captured Assault Rifle frame
(`weapons.json` → `assault_rifle.capture.frame`) with **only the last numeric field (t41, currently
`75`) edited** to the value under test — everything else, including the `<T41>` position, must stay
byte-identical so nothing else moves between rungs. `mcp__brx__send_batch(alias="shooter", gap_ms=150,
commands=[...])`, `<OUT>` = the `$GSET` t2 value under test (0 indoor / 1 outdoor), `<T41>` = the
range value under test:

```
$CLEAR,*
$START,*
$VOL,30,0,*
$GSET,0,<OUT>,1,0,1,0,50,1,*
$PSET,0,0,45,70,70,50,,H44,JAD,V33,V3I,V3C,V3G,V3E,V37,H06,H55,H13,H21,H02,U15,W71,A10,*
$TID,1,*
$WEAP,0,,100,0,0,9,0,,,,,,,,100,850,32,384,1400,0,0,100,100,,0,,,R01,,,,D04,D03,D02,D18,,,,,32,192,<T41>,*
$SPAWN,,*
$AMMO,0,200,999,1,*
```

Then, standing at the marked distance and aimed at the target:

1. Pull the trigger **5 times**, one aimed shot at a time (do not hold full-auto — each pull must be
   separable in the log).
2. After each pull, `mcp__brx__wait_for(alias="target", prefix="$HIR", timeout_s=5)` — a reply is a
   HIT, a timeout is a MISS. (`$HIR` is emitted by the gun that GOT hit, never the shooter — connect
   `wait_for` to `"target"`, not `"shooter"`.)
3. Record hits/5 for that (distance, t41, outdoor) cell.
4. Re-arm the shooter with the next `<T41>` (repeat the whole `send_batch` above — `$CLEAR` first is
   required, `$AMMO` refills the mag) and repeat from step 1.

### Sweep matrix

**Run A — does t41 move range at all (indoor $GSET, isolate the one variable):**

| distance | t41 = 50 | t41 = 75 (stock) | t41 = 100 | t41 = 125 | t41 = 150 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| point blank (~2 ft, sanity control) | | | | | |
| 10 ft | | | | | |
| 20 ft | | | | | |
| 30 ft | | | | | |
| 40 ft | | | | | |

Fill each cell `n/5`. Go past 150 only if the field allows and 40 ft is still failing at 150 — there
is no known ceiling on t41, so keep doubling until either 40 ft passes 5/5 or the gun stops accepting
the value (record which).

**Run B — does the outdoor `$GSET` t2 bit alone matter (t41 held at stock 75, the control the dossier
flagged as unverified)?**

| distance | t2 = 0 (indoor) | t2 = 1 (outdoor) |
|---|---|---|
| 20 ft | | |
| 30 ft | | |
| 40 ft | | |

### Runs C, D and E — the OTHER levers (added 2026-09-13, still NOT RUN)

Runs A and B ask "does `$WEAP` t41 / `$GSET` t2 move range". These three ask the questions that are
left if both come back null, and Run D asks the one that decides whether MC can drive any of it at
all. **Same lane, same marks, same two guns, same target arming as above** — only the shooter's head
changes. Compile-side, all of C and E is staged behind `mc/compile.py`'s `DRIVE_IO_MODE` gate
(`"off"` today, byte-identical head; `venue_mode_frames()` emits exactly the frames below), so the
values here and the values MC would ship are the same text.

**Run C — `$GSET` t3 `gunLaserRegion` in isolation.** The manual calls t3 "the one field that looks
like a direct power control ... the first thing to try" (`docs/manual/dev.md`). Every capture and
every head MC has ever pushed is t3 = 1; the APK's `GunLaserRegion` enum is USA / International, and
**which of those is 0 and which permits more power is not documented anywhere we hold** — so this run
measures the direction rather than assuming it.

Shooter head exactly as in "Arm the SHOOTER" above with `<T41>` left at the stock **75** and t2 left
at **0**, changing only t3:

```
$GSET,0,0,<T3>,0,1,0,50,1,*
```

| distance | t3 = 1 (shipped, the CONTROL) | t3 = 0 |
|---|---|---|
| point blank (~2 ft, sanity control) | | |
| 20 ft | | |
| 30 ft | | |
| 40 ft | | |

Run the control cell (t3 = 1) at each distance in the same visit as its t3 = 0 cell, not once at the
start: the lane, the light and the battery all drift. If t3 = 0 is *worse*, that is a result — it
means 1 is already the higher-power region and `GSET_T3_BY_ENV` in `compile.py` must keep 1 at both
venues. Also record whether t3 = 0 is even accepted (`$GSET` has never been rejected on the bench,
but nothing here has been flipped either).

**Run D — is the ALT-hold state the same bit as `$GSET` t2?** This is the run that decides whether
MC can drive the venue at all, and it needs no target and no shots.

1. Connect to ONE gun over BLE and start a passive capture (`native_capture.py`, receiver-first: we
   want the BLE wire, so leave the IR rig out of it).
2. With the gun **connected and idle**, hold ALT for 3 s so the gun toggles indoor/outdoor natively.
3. Record: does ANY frame appear on the BLE wire? A `$BUT`? A `$GSET` echo? Anything at all?
4. Repeat the hold to toggle back, then repeat the whole thing on a gun that is **spawned/live**
   (the `$PHONE` tap is open in play and reports buttons, so the two cases can differ).
5. Now the other direction: send `$GSET,0,1,1,0,1,0,50,1,*` (t2 = 1) to a gun whose on-gun setting is
   indoor, **power-cycle it**, and check whether the on-gun display/behaviour comes back outdoor.

What each outcome means:

- **A frame appears on the hold** → the gun tells us its venue, MC can read it back, and a readiness
  check ("this gun says INDOOR, tonight is OUTDOOR") becomes buildable. File it as a new row.
- **Nothing appears, and the `$GSET` write does NOT survive a power cycle** → `$GSET` t2 and the
  ALT-hold state are *different* bits, MC cannot see or set the persisted one, and the ALT-hold
  checklist in MC (`ui/VenueModeReminder`) is the permanent answer, not a stopgap.
- **Nothing appears, but the `$GSET` write DOES survive a power cycle** → they are the same bit and
  MC already owns it. Then `$GSET` t2 must be sent **authoritatively every game** (it already is, in
  every head) and the reminder can soften to "confirm", not "set".

**Run E — `$IRTX` in isolation.** ⚠️ **Read the `$IRTX` row in `protocol/brx-protocol.md` before
sending anything.** Two field lists exist for this command; the 4-field
`iRPower,soundOnHit,rangeOutdoor,rangeIndoor` one reads exactly like the venue control we want, and
it is the one that **already emitted zero IR** against a receiver control. The real shape is the
11-field one, and in it `$IRTX` **transmits a word** rather than setting a mode:

```
$IRTX,<Direction>,<BulletType>,<PlayerId>,<Team>,<Damage>,<IsCriticalShot>,<Power>,<IrRange>,<LoopFire>,<IrPulse>,<FlashLED>,*
```

Arm the shooter with the ordinary head (t41 = 75, t2 = 0, t3 = 1), then **without pulling the
trigger** send one `$IRTX` per rung and read the target exactly as in the main procedure
(`wait_for` `$HIR` on the target, 5 words per cell — `$IRTX` five times, not a trigger burst):

```
$IRTX,0,0,0,0,0,0,<POWER>,<RANGE>,0,0,0,*
```

`Damage` stays **0** and `PlayerId` stays **0** on purpose: a zero-damage word still produces a
`$HIR` on a target whose `$SIR` row matches, so hits are still countable, and nothing can be credited
or killed by a probe. `Team` stays **0**, which is not the target's `$TID,3` — keep it that way, or
`$GSET` t1 = 0 (friendly fire off) makes the target ignore every word and the whole run reads null. (If 5/5 cells come back empty at point blank, the word is not being emitted at
all — stop and record that, it is the same null the 4-field shape gave.)

| distance | (75, 75) = indoor pair | (100, 100) = outdoor pair | (100, 150) | (150, 150) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| point blank (~2 ft, the "does it emit at all" control) | | | | |
| 10 ft | | | | |
| 20 ft | | | | |
| 30 ft | | | | |
| 40 ft | | | | |

Then the question Run E exists for: **does it stick?** After the best-performing `$IRTX` rung, pull
the TRIGGER five times from 30 ft without sending anything else. If the trigger's own shots now reach
further than they did in Run A's stock cell, `Power`/`IrRange` are persistent settings and `$IRTX` is
a venue lever after all; if they do not, `$IRTX` is a transmit-only command and is closed as a range
control.

### What closes F162

- **If Run A shows a clean, monotonic hit-rate improvement with t41** at 30-40 ft: that value (plus
  headroom) is the outdoor number. Update `RANGE_ENV_OVERRIDE["outdoor"]` in `mc/compile.py` to it,
  update `test_gun_range_pct_is_a_noop_pending_bench_confirmation` in
  `mcp/tests/test_weapon_derivations.py` to assert the new outdoor value instead of the no-op, and
  fold the finding into `protocol/brx-protocol.md`'s t41 row (drop "untested").
- **If Run A shows no change at any t41** (30-40 ft fails identically at 50 and 200): t41 is refuted
  as a range lever outdoors. Record it as such in `protocol/brx-protocol.md` and close F162 with "t41
  confirmed NOT a range control"; `gunLaserRegion` (`$GSET` t3) and a physical fix become the next
  lever, not a new t41 sweep.
- **Run B is a pure control**, whichever way Run A goes: a positive Run B result (t2 alone extends
  range) would mean MC's outdoor flag already carries a range fix it never gets credit for, wired for
  volume only today (`play_volume()`); fold that into the same `compile.py` change.
- **Run C** decides `GSET_T3_BY_ENV` in `mc/compile.py`. A clean win for t3 = 0 at 30-40 ft: set
  `DRIVE_IO_MODE = "gset_t3"` and update `test_candidate_gset_t3_...` /
  `test_drive_io_mode_ships_off` in `mcp/tests/test_venue_mode.py` in the same commit. t3 = 0 equal
  or worse: leave the gate off, set both venues to 1 in that table, and record t3 as refuted in
  `protocol/brx-protocol.md`'s `$GSET` row.
- **Run D** decides whether MC can ever own the venue, and it is the one to run FIRST if bench time is
  short — a null on it means the `ui/VenueModeReminder` checklist is the permanent answer and both
  compile-side candidates are worth less. Its outcomes are spelled out above; all three are writable
  results.
- **Run E** either promotes `$IRTX` to a real lever (the "does it stick" half comes back positive →
  `DRIVE_IO_MODE = "irtx"` with the winning pair in `IRTX_BY_ENV`) or closes it: a transmit-only
  `$IRTX`, or a silent one, both mean the 11-field shape is not a venue control and
  `protocol/brx-protocol.md`'s `$IRTX` row gets the result folded in.
- **⚠️ One lever per run.** t41, `$GSET` t2, `$GSET` t3 and `$IRTX` are four separate hypotheses about
  the same symptom, and `compile.py` keeps them in four separate places (`RANGE_ENV_OVERRIDE`, the
  head's own `$GSET`, `GSET_T3_BY_ENV`, `IRTX_BY_ENV`) for exactly that reason. A rung that moves two
  of them measures neither.
- Either way, write the result into this file (a new dated entry, do not edit this one), strike F162
  in `FOLLOWUPS.md`, and update `HANDOFF.md`.

## 2026-09-11 (night) — first real game test on the Mac: 1v1, two taggers, 25 new ids

**Setup.** MacBook host (this is the match-day machine), MC `session 8b501cfd`, two real taggers.
iPhone `node-78fcf9754b` = TONY (`Tactix2-3D4F`) built and installed from **today's tree**; Android
`node-2ef0f76583` = ROCCO (`Tactix-E20D`) on **APK 0.1.8** (2026-09-10, `cfe2a8e`). tdm then ffa,
frag_limit 7, time_limit 300 s. Evidence kept: `~/mc-20260911-1927.log`,
`~/.brx-mcp/mc/session-8b501cfd.sqlite`.

**Full sheet, self-contained, written for an agent to work from:
[`../archive/game-test-2026-09-11.md`](../archive/game-test-2026-09-11.md).** 25 new ids: F110-F127 and S20-S26.
What follows is only what the log should preserve beyond that sheet.

### Getting there cost an hour, and none of it was the code
`devicectl` install over a **wireless** CoreDevice tunnel sat at "Acquired tunnel connection" for
three minutes and delivered nothing. I killed it mid-tunnel, which **wedged `remotepairingd`**
(`tunnelState: unavailable`, `pairingState: paired`). Tony's reboot cleared it and the install then
took **13 seconds**. Two lessons: do not kill a `devicectl` install mid-transfer, and
`system_profiler SPUSBDataType` **returns nothing at all under this sandbox** — I read its silence as
"the phone is not on USB" and sent Tony chasing a LAN problem that did not exist. `ioreg -p IOUSB`
shows the truth.

### The three things the desk could not have found
- **F121** is the one to internalise. Tony reported "you can take damage during the countdown, while
  team colors are still on headsets" — and those are *the same event*. `compile.py:934` ships
  `sir_rows` in the same head as the pregame team colour, and per F11 the `$SIR` table IS the arming
  of hit reception. **The team colour on the headset is the visible marker that you have become
  hittable.** He saw the correlation; the code confirms it is adjacency in one frame list.
- **F124**: the frag limit announces "next kill wins" and then nothing ends the match. The end exists
  in `modes/deathmatch.py:110` and was never ported to MC's scorer. Configured, announced, displayed,
  never enforced.
- **F125**: MC reported "END REACHED 2 OF 2 NODES" and the node's own log says
  `match ended: time-expiry` eighteen seconds later. Settled only because the Android's SHARE LOG
  upload worked.

### A pattern worth naming
Three separate bugs are **the HUD asserting something the hardware never confirmed**: the reload
animation is a blind timer that writes nothing to the gun (F123), the LED strip freezes because death
is deliberately hands-off (F113), and countdown damage is ignored by the software while the hardware
fully reacts (F121). `screens.mjs` asserts *presence*, never *agreement with the gun*.

### Refuted, so nobody re-derives them
- **The briefing clipping is NOT the `max-height:360px` media query.** The HUD renders into a fixed
  844x390 `#frame` that is then scaled, so that query keys off the device viewport and is irrelevant.
  Measured clipping at 891x411, 667x375 and 812x375 alike.
- **The wrong kill sound was NOT `low_health`.** All nine of Tony's kills show `hp=45 armor=70` in the
  store. Nor a bad kill-line id: the male kill pool (VAA/VA8/VA9/VAK/VAL) is clean.
- **The silent UNSTOPPABLE medal is NOT a missing medal path in 0.1.8** (that APK postdates the medal
  code by six days) and **NOT the wrong `$PLAY` slot** — the same voice-slot form plays kill lines all
  night. Narrowed to VX0U itself, the only medal sound that is `voice:game_mode`.
- **`tabular-nums` does not work in Saira Condensed.** Measured: "11" is 113px and "88" is 178px on the
  same element. MC makes the identical assumption about Oswald in `tokens.ts` and it is unmeasured.

### What worked, recorded as loudly as the bugs
Mid-match resync after the CAM crash (node.md §3.10, unplanned, on hardware). Early end reaching both
nodes with `game_over` and `victory` — that MC-to-node victory push had never been field-confirmed.
`next_kill_wins` at a cap of 7. The iPhone auto-discovered MC over mDNS with no QR and no typed URL.
The FFA feed computed FIRST BLOOD and STREAK tags correctly — which is also the proof that the medals
in **F116** are computed and then discarded.

### 2026-09-11 (night) — ⭐ CALLSIGN'S WHOLE CLOUD PROTOCOL, DECODED OFF THE WIRE (Mac + WireGuard, one last snoop)

Tony ran Callsign one last time on the MacBook and we captured it end to end. This is the record of the
method (which the runbook got half wrong) and the full protocol, lobby through game-over.

## Method — the HTTP proxy was the wrong tool; WireGuard is the right one

The runbook's Job 1 (system HTTP proxy + `mitm.it` cert) **does not work for Callsign**: it is a Unity /
IL2CPP app, and Unity's iOS networking **ignores the system proxy setting**. Login succeeded and *nothing*
reached the proxy — the trap looks exactly like a silent TLS failure but is not one. The fix that worked:
`mitmweb --mode regular --mode wireguard` on the Mac, scan the WireGuard QR into the WireGuard iOS app,
drop the Wi-Fi proxy. WireGuard captures at the network layer, so the app gets no vote. **Promoted into
`capture-runbook.md` Job 1** (done this session).

Two bonus facts from the tunnel: **the Callsign REST API is plain HTTP** (`http://ltp-prod-v4...`, no TLS,
so no pinning to fight), and **the game config does NOT ride REST** — which is why the runbook's target
endpoints (`settings`, `voice-profiles`, `arenas/games`) never fired even after login and pairing. They
are cached client-side; the live game data comes through the lobby (below).

## The API and auth (plain HTTP, bearer token)

- **Auth:** OAuth2 password grant → `POST /oauth/token`, **client_secret hardcoded in the app**
  (`client_id=callsignclient`), returns `{access_token, refresh_token, expires_in ~10195, scope:"read write
  trust", authorities:[ROLE_CallSign]}`.
- **Only REST calls seen the whole session:** `GET /api/v1/callsign/profile/<userId>` (self and each other
  player — the roster is polled per-player, there is no lobby-roster endpoint), `PUT .../profile`,
  `GET .../arenas/last-time-played-profile`. No weapon/voice/game endpoint fired.

## The lobby (this is what our LAN WebSocket replaces)

AWS **Cognito** (identity pool in **eu-west-1** — note the region split) issues temp creds → each client has
its **own GUID-named SQS queue** (only `GetQueueUrl`/`SetQueueAttributes`/`ReceiveMessage`/`SendMessage`/
`DeleteMessageBatch` were on the wire — `CreateQueue` was not captured, so "creates" is inferred from the
per-client queue + `MessageRetentionPeriod=60`), subscribed to one shared **SNS topic**
`LTP-BRX-Lobby-Topic_prod` (account `623577608736`, **us-east-1**), and **long-polls** (`WaitTimeSeconds=20`).
Hosting a game = **SNS Publish**; joining = ReceiveMessage. The **~1-min lobby delay is this**: long-poll +
SNS→SQS fan-out. Vendor middleware is **Melior Games** (`MeliorGames.Net.*`); the game layer is
`LaserTag.CallSign.*`.

## The game object — .NET BinaryFormatter, base64, published via SNS

A hosted game is `MeliorGames.Net.Providers.AmazonNet.Messages.RoomIsOpenMessage → MultiplayerRoom`. **The
room, its `MultiplayerTeam[]` and `MultiplayerParticipant` are Melior's (`MeliorGames.Net.*`, from
`MeliorGamesLib`), NOT `LaserTag.*`** — `LaserTag.CallSign.Domain.*` appears only inside the nested
GameSettings blob. `MultiplayerRoom` fields:
`GameSettings · SquadLeaderVoices · GameSkin · GameMode · ModuleVersion · Title · Description · Address ·
Location · ReadyPlayers · Uid`, plus `Teams[]` and `Participants[]`. `GameSettings` is a **nested
BinaryFormatter blob** → `LaserTag.CallSign.Domain.GameSettings.GameSettingsInfo` holding a
`List<GameSettings.WeaponSettings>`, each:
`{FireMode, <engine-mode>, <gender>, <primary WeaponType>, <secondary WeaponType>, GameSkin, "Scan", Id}`,
followed by a `List<LaserTag.CallSign.Domain.WeaponType>` (enum, int `value__` backing).

**Three** distinct room configs were published (grouped by GameSettings signature; two were played to
completion):

| field | game A (133×) | game B (54×) | game C (148×) |
|---|---|---|---|
| room `GameMode` (skin display) | `TeamSnipers` | `Slayer` | `Slayer` |
| engine mode (in GameSettingsInfo) | `FactionWarsOffline` | `FreeForAll` | `FreeForAll` |
| primary / secondary weapon | `SniperRifle` / `None` | `AssualtRifle` [sic] / `Shotgun` | `AMR` / `SmgSaw` |
| `GameSkin` | `Halo` / `Halo Classic` | `Halo` / `Halo Classic` | `Halo` / `Halo Classic` |
| Teams | Team 1, Team 2 | Team 1, Team 2 | Team 1, Team 2 (present even in FFA) |

So there are **two mode fields** — a skin display name over an engine name — and the confirmed `WeaponType`
enum members so far are `SniperRifle`, `AssualtRifle` (their typo), `Shotgun`, `None`, `AMR`, `SmgSaw` (the
full enum is larger; only picked weapons appear).

## The in-match protocol (captured across two full games with kills/deaths)

Two message layers over the SNS/SQS transport (counts = times seen in this capture; the list is what
appeared, not a closed set):

- **Melior transport (`MeliorGames.Net.*`):** `RoomIsOpenMessage` · `JoinToRoomMessage` /
  `JoinToRoomAnswerMessage` · `RoomUpdateMessage` (room-state sync) · `LeftRoomMessage` · `PingMessage` /
  `PongMessage` (keepalive, 178× each).
- **CallSign game logic (`LaserTag.CallSign.Net.Messages`):** **`HitMessage` (40×) and `PlayerKilledMessage`
  (9×) are the real hit/kill wire events** · `PlayerReadyMessage` (12×) · `StartGameMessage` (3×) ·
  `PlayerInfoChangedMessage` (154×) · `GunVolumeSettingsMessage` (3× — **relevant to our volume work**) ·
  `FullMargeGameStatisticsMessage` [sic] — live stats, wraps `UserUpdateInfoData[] {Id, PlayerGameStatistics}`
  · `BeginRestartGameMessage` (3×) · `GamePauseMessage` (1×) · `GetPlayerInfoMessage` (1×) ·
  `GameOverMessage → GameOverData {Reason, PlayerGameProfile, PlayerGameStatistics}`.
- **Game-over `Reason` enum** seen: `Normal` (15×), `NumberOfPlayersLessThanMinimum` (7×) — there are surely more.
- **Still undecoded:** the byte layout inside `HitMessage` / `PlayerKilledMessage` (who-hit-whom, weapon,
  damage) and `PlayerGameStatistics` — the message *types* are pinned, the field-level contents are not.

## ⚠️ The honest limits (what we did NOT get)

- **Numeric weapon STAT values are not in the payload.** `WeaponSettings` carries weapon *names/enum*, not
  the `$WEAP` accuracy/damage/timing numbers. P8's "server-side weapon stats" goal is **not** met; those are
  fetched elsewhere or are truly per-account server state. Not closing P8 on this.
- **`PlayerGameStatistics` field names are not recoverable** from these messages — the numeric members
  (kills/deaths/score) serialize as inline value-types whose names live only in a type-definition record that
  these back-referencing messages do not repeat. We have the *shape*, not the field labels.
- **P3 not answered.** `SquadLeaderVoices` was **empty** in both games (no squad-leader voice set). The field
  exists in `RoomIsOpenMessage`; to capture the voice-pack data, set a squad voice and re-host. P3 refined,
  not closed.
- **Possible LAN P2P after handoff:** a participant `Address` is a real LAN IP (`192.168.0.x`, RFC1918), so
  gameplay may coordinate phone-to-phone on the LAN; only the tunneled phone is visible, so the peer side is
  unproven (the host's own participant `Address` was the WireGuard tunnel IP, itself private).

## Handling (licensed content + secrets)

Raw capture is `~/Desktop/callsign-wg-1841.flows` (~48 MB), **kept off the repo**. It contains Tony's GPS
(`Location` = two lat/long doubles), both phones' IPs, a live bearer + refresh token, and the app's
hardcoded client secret. Facts promoted to `protocol/callsign-extract/protocol-classes.md`; **no raw asset
JSON, no tokens, no coordinates in git** (`RAW_ASSETS_NOTE.md`). Tear-down after: WireGuard tunnel removed,
Wi-Fi proxy off, mitmproxy cert profile deleted.

## The generalisation worth keeping

**To MITM a Unity/IL2CPP mobile app, do not use the system HTTP proxy — it is silently ignored. Use
mitmproxy's WireGuard mode** (network-layer, app-agnostic). And when an app's config "isn't in the API
capture," check whether it rides a *message bus* (here SNS/SQS) rather than REST before concluding the
capture failed.

### 2026-09-07 — Python DRY review: the duplication was not the problem, the guards were

A repo-wide review of all ~41k lines of Python, run as an agent team (four read-only reviewers, then
five implementers), asked for deduplication and mostly found something else. Recording the method and
the generalisation, because the generalisation is the reusable part.

## What the duplication actually looked like, measured

Token-shingle clone detection over the whole tree, rather than impression:

| area | lines | literal clone density |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp/tools/` (80 bench scripts) | 8,310 | **high** — 18+ cross-file clusters |
| `mcp/brx_mcp/` (production) | 18,010 | low — `modes/lms.py` ↔ `survival.py` the only real one |
| `mcp/tests/` | 13,991 | low |

So `tools/` looked like the target and was not one: reading every `deaf_*`, `range_*`, `native_*` and
`led_*` family in full showed they are distinct one-shot experiments whose value is the RECORD of what
was run. Merging them would have destroyed evidence. The production package's problem was never
copy-paste — it was god modules (`state.py` 1665, `__main__.py` 1478, `stage.py` 1319) and drift
between tables that were *supposed* to agree.

## Six bugs, and where they were hiding

None were found by looking for bugs. All six were found by asking "why are there two of these?"

1. Five bench tools ended every run on a bare `$CLEAR` with no `$SIR` restore (F11) — a gun that
   reports healthy and cannot be hit, handed to the next experiment.
2. `diag/runner.py` did the same in a `finally:` after EVERY diagnostic run.
3. `diag/cases.py` shipped 5 of the 10 `$SIR` rows — no rocket, no rail gun, no melee.
4. `alert` was missing from `MC_KINDS`, so every A11.4 alert MC sent was rejected at the node.
5. `cli.END_SEQUENCE` never received the 2026-08-25 revive fix.
6. `FakeCompiler.cues()["game_over"]` was byte-identical to the real compiler's VICTORY frame, and
   there was no `victory` key — so the fallback compiler played the win sting to everyone at the
   whistle.

## ⭐ The generalisation: four guards could not see the fault they were built for

This is the finding worth keeping.

- `test_bench_teardown.py` scanned **only `finally:` blocks**. All five tools put their teardown in
  the plain body of `main()`. Its docstring shows the narrowing was deliberate, to dodge 19 false
  positives — but those came from matching `$CLEAR` in COMMENTS and PRINTED PROSE, not from where it
  looked. The fix was to tighten what counts as a frame (AST literals), not to narrow where to look.
- `test_clear_safety.py` sweeps **only NAMED sequences**. The diag runner's teardown was an inline
  tuple, so it was never in the sweep — while `diag.END`, a constant nothing referenced, sat on that
  file's allowlist legitimising the shape. **The declared one was dead and the live one was
  invisible, and the allowlist entry is what made the file look considered.**
- `test_led_invariants.py` (written the same evening) walked `poolgauge` and `presentation` and never
  imported `mc.compile` — so a `$HLED,,6` from the compiler would not have tripped the effect-6 pin,
  the highest-value rule in the file, with the hole exactly where real bundles are assembled.
- The MC and app suites both passed while `alert` was dead, because MC asserts on `FakeNet.pushed`
  (no validation) and the app calls `engine.onMcMessage` directly. **Both sides were tested and the
  wire between them was not.**

None of these was carelessness. Each guard was correct for the place it looked. **A green result from
a narrow guard is indistinguishable from a green result from a complete one**, which is why a guard
has to state its own blind spot. Filed as F40.

## Method notes

- **The suite itself was not trustworthy.** `run_tests.py` aborted the whole run on one file's import
  error, so ~30 later files silently never executed while the totals stayed plausible. Fixed first,
  before anything else, because every later measurement depended on it.
- **Eleven test files hand-rolled `asyncio.get_event_loop()` helpers on a shared loop, and four had
  independently invented different defensive patches for the resulting order-dependence** ("Two wrong
  versions of this preceded it… seen as 607-pass and 604-pass"). Every patch defended the shared loop;
  the shared loop was the bug.
- **Three refusals to merge were worth more than most of the merges.** The rule that emerged, from the
  hit-audio lane: the test is not "do these look alike" but **"does the flag select between two answers
  to the SAME question"**. It stopped `plan()`/`plan_in_place()`, Deathmatch's team-winner vs LMS's
  player-winner, and `_sync_kit_open` being folded into `_resend` — the last of which would have
  double-pushed every config with a re-rolled voice bank, invisible to every test because both banks
  are individually valid.
- **A suspected bug that was not one** is also a result: the post-`start()` loadout resend was
  investigated, found correct (the node keeps its schedule and re-acks itself), and the chain pinned in
  `test_mc_loadout_after_start.py` rather than left as a docstring warning.
- **`git stash` in a shared tree reverted another agent's four files mid-edit.** A whole-tree backup
  taken before the run is what made that a near-miss instead of a loss.

Suite: 809 passed / 63 files at the start, **953 passed / 0 failed / 46 skipped / 77 files** at the
end, with ~115 new tests. Remaining backlog is F42.
