## 2026-09-12 (midday, desk) — the doc-rot review, and its apply pass, on the whole repo

**Setup.** WSL, no hardware. Three peer sessions live (brx-fixes on the game-test M1/M2 in the main tree; brx-backhaul on
its own worktree/PR; brx-dry starting a contract generator). Method written first as `.claude/skills/doc-rot-review/SKILL.md`.

**Review.** Eleven read-only lanes, disjoint file sets, judged against what each file cites (routes vs `api.py`, fields vs
`types.py`/`types.ts`, runbook flags vs argparse, retractions vs the log). 625 tracked files, ~250 findings before dedupe,
~200 after subtracting the peers' in-flight work; ~55 P1. Ledger: artifact "Open BRX Rot Ledger". The nine cross-lane
shapes: a retraction lands in one file of a pair (fn 24, `$GLED` gate 5, the shield pool, the F23 formula, the 2166-id
bank); status re-grown in nine non-status files, always behind the code; three "GENERATED" headers on hand-kept copies;
the typed UI boundary drifted on the newest work and the contract test pinned 8 of ~18 shapes; three rules engines all
maintained; the amendment index used as a design inbox; guards reading HEAD not the tree; first-contact files the most
rotten while the deep files were excellent; 1.4 MB of bounded dead weight. The Python package had 7 dead one-liners in ~900.

**Apply.** Eight lanes, each with an exclusive file list and the peers' dirty set off-limits, every lane forbidden to run
repo scripts or touch the index; the main session committed each with `git commit --only`: 5e4ced8 c551196 27ba42c e4073f8
d02f027 f91b847 3e2eb36 94ca492 f356c33 edeb0d3, then this close on top of brx-fixes' M2 (7f1d6fd). What the lanes found the
REVIEW had wrong: `platform.md`'s "objective time" claim is true (`engine.js` emits `possession` with `hold_ms`); the two
facts called exclusive to the reference guides were already in the manual; `f11_ab.py` was a library for 18 scripts, not a
one-off (hoisted before deletion); three "closed" scripts were still named by live sheets (kept); the `resume()` live-relink
finding was fixed by M2 before this close. **Tests at the last full run before M2:** `mcp` 1367 passed / 2 failed (both M2's,
since fixed there) / 58 skipped; `webapp/mc` 207/207; `app` 321/321; `cd site && npm test` 90/90. **Conclusion:** the repo's
guards only ever caught what had broken once; the new ones encode every class this pass found, and the skill carries the
method plus the apply-phase lessons (exclusive ownership per lane, the peer's dirty set is authoritative, `git add` before
`--only` for new files, grep imports before deleting a "one-off").

**Polish loop over the apply commits (afternoon).** Three review lenses (doc accuracy, code/tests, rot regression) on
Opus, three rounds, fixes on Sonnet lanes with exclusive files, ten more `--only` commits (f557381 .. 20877d8). Round 1:
no Critical; five High (perks still hand-copied thrice; a living bench sheet sending the reader into the archive; the
stage-coverage guard blind to getters; a dangling cross-reference to the deleted polarity caveat; the friendly-fire rule
stated flat while F49 is unexplained) and ~15 Medium. Round 2: the §7 rewrite had deleted the utility spec's one
hardware-verified line; dev.md still stated friendly-fire flat; the unknown-module scan skipped modes/ and diag/; the
engine-method slice took the last brace. Round 3: three wording Mediums and one High (the modes/diag hole), all fixed.
What the loop taught: a cleanup pass writes its own rot (dated breadcrumbs, "GENERATED" claims that outrun the generator,
a hedge repeated four times), and a guard added in a hurry needs a can-fail test the same day. Final: `mcp`
1469 passed / 0 failed, `webapp/mc` 287/287, `app` 381/381, `cd site && npm test` 90/90 (after brx-fixes fixed the
12j locator its REVIEW KIT change had broken). Deferred Lows are in the round reports, not rows: `test_site_shots`
carries two overlapping staleness checks; the wiki-link slug guard does not exempt fenced code; `_CITED_ID_ALLOW` keys on
basename; `if TYPE_CHECKING:` imports classify as eager; `pyproject` lists `packages = ["brx_mcp"]` only, which a wheel
would ship without subpackages; `backend.ts:500` filters `!k.hidden` over an already-filtered list.

## 2026-09-12 (keyboard) — A28 BACKHAUL: a phone with a data plan reaches MC off the field Wi-Fi, no per-phone setup (branch `backhaul-a28`, B30)

**Decision (Tony).** Not a requirement. A phone that has a data plan should keep getting game updates and kill
confirms when it wanders out of MC's Wi-Fi; a whole lobby on backhaul can unlock modes. Must work for anyone who
cloned the repo with **no custom setup on each phone** → cloudflared *quick tunnel* on the MC machine (no account,
no domain, no login), exposing the NODE SOCKET PORT only. Internet at the laptop is the one new field requirement,
and only when the host turns it on. Tony's step-0 idea became a REACH block on the Armory join card; the
"before any phones connect" constraint disappeared because MC pushes the public URL to already-joined phones.

**Contract:** contracts §5d + A28 index row (A26/A27 taken by uncommitted 2026-09-11 work; A29–A31 too, so A28 it is),
API.md (`lan.public`, `coverage`, `NodeView.reach`, `POST /api/tunnel`), FOLLOWUPS B30, ADR-0002 amendment note.
Key calls: the node **prefers backhaul** when offered (the only way coverage can be observed rather than asserted);
the join secret is enforced only on hellos that arrive through the tunnel or from a non-private peer; derived
coverage makes frag/survival ends authoritative but **never** lifts `time_limit_s`; `PARAMS.requires_coverage`
reserved, no catalog mode uses it.

**Build:** contract commit first, then three lanes on a worktree with disjoint files (server opus · MC UI sonnet ·
app transport sonnet), then a three-pass polish loop (correctness + security opus, UX sonnet with a real browser).
Passes closed **1 high + 13 medium**: the hello-based pub probe collided with the server's one-socket takeover
(now a reachability probe with no hello); app.js wiped the transport's persisted pub on every connect; pub/secret
were global, so a phone dialled the previous MC's tunnel with its key (now url- and session-scoped); the secret gate
keyed on a status that could flip to error while cloudflared still routed (now on the child being alive, plus an
orphan pid file, reap-with-escalation and a latch that keeps the gate armed when the kill fails); `reach` was
client-asserted (now stamped by MC from the socket path, and cleared through one `_drop_socket` exit); the 8 s
giveup armed only on `onopen` so a blackholed pub stuck the phone offline; `ws://` public URLs to public hosts
rejected. Deferred (B30): per-peer pre-hello cap, forged Cf header counting toward coverage, `origins=`.

**Verified (software only):** mcp 1349 (venv) / 1284+59 skipped (system python) · app 295+1 skip · MC UI tsc clean,
146 unit, backhaul e2e in headless Chromium at 1280/393 · site build + 90-step gate · shots regenerated. MC smoke-run
from the worktree with `--public-url` showed the two-URL QR, the public block and coverage in `/api/state`.
**Not yet:** a REAL cloudflared (every tunnel test drives a Python stand-in) and any hardware. Bench gate = one Pixel,
Wi-Fi off, data on, scan the QR, join, take a kill, see KILL CONFIRMED (B30).

**Also today:** `docs/architecture-topology.md` gained §2.1 and an accuracy pass (the "one phone, never a whole match"
claims were stale: two phones have played three whole matches); the `/platform` landing gained a Topology section
("Three links. Only one has to hold.") with the six design captions and the four plain costs.

**Idea parked (Tony's question):** a kill confirm over the BLE advert — the victim's advert already carries alive/seq,
three player bytes are spare; put `killed_by` in the value byte while dead and let the shooter's phone credit itself
locally. Presentation only, victim-authoritative scoring unchanged. Not on this branch.

## 2026-09-12 (session brx-dry) — contract DRY phase 1: one machine source for the node↔MC wire

**Built.** The wire tables (kinds, required fields, event types, size caps, timing constants) and 18 shared
TypedDict shapes lived three times — `envelope.py`, `envelope.js`, `types.ts` — hand-kept in step by nobody
checking. A mechanical diff found them already apart: `possession` was a persisted event on the Python side
and absent from the phone's whitelist (`envelope.js`); `result` demanded six fields on the MC side and only
`match_id` on the node side — deliberate (A24: a result must reach the engine even short a field), but until
today an unwritten asymmetry rather than a rule. `mcp/tools/gen_contract.py` now renders
`webapp/mc/src/api/contract.gen.ts` and `app/src/transport/contract.gen.js` from `mcp/brx_mcp/mc/types.py` +
`envelope.py`; `mcp/tests/test_contract_generated.py` (no skip path) fails CI on drift, importable
under bare system python. `envelope.js` imports the four tables from the generated file and re-exports every
name it exported before (no importer changed); `types.ts` re-exports the generated shapes and declares none
of them itself. `_REQUIRED`/`_EVENT_REQUIRED` go public as `REQUIRED`/`EVENT_REQUIRED`.

**The ACCEPT_MIN decision.** The rule lives once, in `envelope.py`: `ACCEPT_MIN: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] =
{"result": ("match_id",)}`. `validate(env, direction="mc")` (the node receiving from MC) checks
`ACCEPT_MIN.get(kind, REQUIRED[kind])`; `direction="node"` (MC receiving) always checks the full
`REQUIRED[kind]`. This makes A24's guarantee — a result reaches the engine even missing a field — an
invariant the generator and a test assert, rather than a fact that lived only in a comment on one side.

**The concurrency lesson.** Mid-lane, `types.py` picked up 139 lines that were not this lane's own work: a
`git commit --only <file>` from a peer session, sharing the one git index this repo's sessions all use, swept
the file's then-current state (someone else's app-0.2.0 `APP_MINOR` bump plus unrelated enrichments) into
`be1ce39` alongside the intended change. The content was correct — nothing was lost or wrong — but it landed
under a commit message that did not mention it. Lesson: when more than one session has the same file open,
diff what a `--only` commit is about to take, not just the path list, before trusting the message to describe
the diff.

**What stays hand-written (FOLLOWUPS F42.9–F42.13, filed today, none blocking):** the ~29 UI-only view types
(`State`, `LiveView`, `RecapView`, `NodeView`, `StationView`, `WeaponView`, …) have no Python TypedDict —
`api.py` builds them untyped, so there is nothing to generate from yet; pyright as a CI gate for `mcp/` is
unstarted (first manual run today: 249 errors, 81% of defs already annotated, the hot files are `state.py`
(79) and `stage/stage.py` (56)); `webapp/mc`'s `tsconfig` is not `strict` yet; `app/src/transport`
(`envelope.js`/`transport.js`) has no JSDoc + `checkJs` reading a generated `.d.ts`; `test_ui_contract.py`'s
DEFAULT_POLICY/voice test SKIPPED on an unparsable `gameSummary.ts` entry — a skip that had to be a parse
fix, not a permanent exemption (closed the same day by the polish pass below).

**Also found mid-session:** the working tree carried an unresolved merge (PR #3 `backhaul-a28` → `main`,
conflicts in `contracts.md`, `FOLLOWUPS.md`, this log, and several `mcp/brx_mcp/mc/*` + `app/src/*` files;
`types.py` briefly did not parse). It resolved (by another session) partway through this session's docs pass;
noted here because it blocked `python3 run_tests.py` for a stretch and is not a defect in the contract-DRY
work itself.

**Polish loop on `86a8c3a` (three read-only reviewers, then a fix pass, then two fresh reviewers):** no
Critical. One High: `test_ui_contract.py`'s DEFAULT_POLICY guard, the very test the commit message advertised,
ALWAYS skipped because its hand-rolled TS scanner could not read the `...over` spread in `gameSummary.ts`
(`_ts_object` now reads `...ident`; parser misses are AssertionErrors, never Skipped; 4/4, 0 skipped). Mediums,
all fixed: the generated file attached `PERSISTED_EVENT_TYPES`'s "this is a WHITELIST" comment to
`MAX_LOG_CHUNK_BYTES` (the comment rule is now ONLY "8+ spaces after `#` = continuation"; `FrameBundle.cues`
re-wrapped to that style); `envelope.py` still compared `shooter_num` against a literal 63 (now `MAX_PLAYERS`);
`_T_MIN_MS`/`_T_MAX_MS` were the last hand-mirrored pair (public, generated, imported by `envelope.js`; every
UPPER_CASE constant in `envelope.py` is now scanned generically); Literal aliases lost their comments. Lows
taken because they were one-liners in the same files: section dividers no longer become JSDoc, `STATION_SOURCE_IDS`
keeps source order, a non-`str` Literal member raises, the dead `MC_KINDS | {"apply"}` is gone, `Live.tsx`'s
hard-coded 8000 is `STALE_AFTER_MS`, the deleted `types.ts` nuance for `Loadout`/`LoadoutPool`/`LoadoutPolicy`
is back as `types.py` docstrings (comments travel). Found on the way: `test_site_shots.py`'s `_skip` called
`pytest.skip`, a BaseException `run_tests.py` does not catch, so under the venv a dirty UI tree ABORTED the
whole run with no summary line; it now raises the runner's Skipped unless `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` is set.

**Verified on the merged tree (`6925d32` + this pass):** `gen_contract.py --check` up to date; `run_tests.py`
1521 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped under the venv, 1457 / 0 / 65 under system python (the one skip is
`test_site_shots` on a dirty tree, which regenerates with `cd site && npm run shots`, run this session);
`webapp/mc`: `tsc -b` clean, vitest 266/266; `app`: 365/365.

**The pyright gate (F42.10), closed the same day:** `standard` mode over `brx_mcp/` (`stage/` excluded — it's
the raw-JSON bench stage that must keep mirroring the phone engine, filed as its own typing job, F42.14) went
from 191 errors to 0 across three lanes (247 counting `stage/`; 152 of the 191 were in the `mc` package
alone), with no `# type: ignore`, no `cast(Any)`, and no silencing `Any` used to get there.

**Real gaps the checker found, not just missing hints:** `diag/cases.py`'s `cases_for` referenced an
undefined name `Capability` (the module imports it as `Cap`), so the function could never run; `net.py:269`
built a websockets `Response` with `headers=None` where the library requires a `Headers` object — an
`AttributeError` waiting for the first request to hit the node socket on the wrong path; `compile.py` had two
`cues[...] = play_frame(...)` writes that could store `None` into a `dict[str, str]` wire field when a voice
pool and its family disagree (now skipped instead); `policy.py`'s `dropped_by` could call `_name` on `None`
given an empty weapons list; `modes/deathmatch.py`'s `_last_shot` was annotated as a 2-tuple while the code
stored a 3-tuple; `modes/objectives.py`'s `owner` (an `Optional[int]` where 0 is a real team, not falsy) was
used as a dict key after only a truthiness-adjacent check; `poolgauge.py`'s `display_colour(None)` worked
only by luck; `__main__.py`'s `_game_sim` was annotated `-> None` while a test captured its dict return.
`state.py` alone carried four real behaviour changes, named as such because pyright surfaced them, not the
review: `ev.pop("_seq") or ev.get("seq")` where nothing ever writes `_seq` (half-dead code); `PUT
/api/config {"led": null}` used to store the literal null while every other nullable key drops it (now
drops); `_merge_config` merged the respawn/scoring/health/stun bags wholesale, so an unknown key nested
inside one survived (now whitelisted like the top level already was); `_voice_ids`/`_check_loadout` built a
`set[str]` from unfiltered ids (non-strings now skipped). Five more `state.py` helpers dereferenced the
scorer/start-info `Optional` unguarded — unreachable today, but `_start_body` now raises `ConflictError`
rather than a bare `TypeError` if it ever is.

**Annotation-only changes worth naming:** `policy.py`'s public functions now take
`LoadoutPolicy`/`LoadoutPool`/`Loadout`; `compile.py`/`presentation.py` take `GameConfig`; `perks.py` returns
`PerkView`; `views.py` takes `Sequence[Weapon]`; `presets.py`'s config params are `Mapping[str, Any]` on
purpose (raw input, sanitized inside, not a hole); `ScoreRow` gained `after_end_kills`/`after_end_deaths` as
`NotRequired`, and the generated contract was regenerated for it.

**Verified:** `run_tests.py` 1534 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped under the venv (the skip is `test_site_shots`
on a dirty tree), including the pyright gate itself; 1468 / 0 / 67 under system python. CI's second
(with-extras) pass installs pyright so the gate runs there too.

**A two-lens polish pass on the pyright work (annotation honesty; behaviour changes) found no Critical, one
High and five Mediums, all fixed:** the rewritten `_merge_config` read `merged["win_by"]` unguarded, so a
config restored from a snapshot missing that key would have turned `PUT /api/config {"scoring": ...}` into a
KeyError 500 (now falls back to the mode default, pinned by a test); the pass's four behaviour changes
(the nested-bag whitelist, `led: null` dropping, the websockets 404 `Headers()` fix, `perks.view` forwarding
every `EFFECT_KEY`) each gained a test of its own; pyright is now pinned in CI to the venv's version, so an
upstream release can't redden an unrelated commit; the redundant `_alert_cb` wrapper is gone and the
`ReadinessRow` docstring matches its one-literal build. Deferred Low: `self.compiler` stays unannotated, so
the catalog-getter annotations are asserted rather than checked — a `Protocol` is the real fix, later.

The M2 loop had stopped with round 2 half-applied (two lanes hit the account session limit). Finished it: the phone-side
round-2 review ran on the committed range and found a **Critical** — the A26 pick debounce held ONE pending pick per node,
so tapping a rifle on PRIMARY then a row on SECONDARY inside 400 ms destroyed the first pick silently — and a **High** of
my own making: `resume()` had been changed to reconcile in LIVE, and it fires on every `visibilitychange`, so a screen-off/
on wrote `$AMMO,0`, held the player disarmed for 3 s and re-armed from the SPAWN magazine (a free reload on demand). Both
fixed with fail-first tests (`commitPick` on slot switch/tab, `RESUME_GAP_MS` 5 s gate). The MC UI residuals (F129) landed
in the same commit (88b4d20). New residual: **F133** (the kit-lock latch can outlive a re-pushed game).

The backhaul branch (PR #3, 24 commits) was merged into this line (6925d32): 22 conflicts, all additive, kept both sides;
the branch had committed `app/node_modules` and `site/node_modules` as SELF-REFERENTIAL SYMLINKS from its worktree
(`.gitignore`'s `node_modules/` matches directories only), and checking them out replaced the real folders — dropped in
the merge, folders restored, a bare `node_modules` line added to .gitignore. Two shared-index lessons for the memory file:
`git commit --only <file>` commits the FILE's working copy (it swept a peer's 139 uncommitted lines once), and a peer's
untracked file under app/ blocks a clean APK sidecar.

**App 0.2.0** cut and published (release `app-v0.2.0`, from a clean 47a71bd) — the wire gained `result`, so the 0.x MINOR
moved on both sides at once (`types.APP_MINOR = 2`, pinned to app/package.json by a test). Final counts on the pushed tree:
python 1458/0 (once the mirror pin landed), app 381, MC UI 287, e2e:kit/e2e:m2/koth/ui:logsync green, HUD screen-truth
300/0 on the merged bundle, site shots regenerated on HEAD.

## 2026-09-12 (field, midday) — ⭐ BACKHAUL FIELD TEST: the Pixel 10 on cellular played three FFA matches through a cloudflared quick tunnel

**Rig.** MC ran INSIDE WSL2 on the Windows PC (a clean worktree of merged main, `--tunnel`, real cloudflared 2026.9.1),
so its LAN address was behind Windows NAT and unreachable from any phone: every phone had to come in through the tunnel,
which made this a pure backhaul test. Pixel 10 (Wi-Fi OFF, mobile data) = TONY; Pixel 4 (home Wi-Fi) = OTHERGUY; both on a
tree build of merged main over wireless adb (APK 0.2.0 predates the merge and has no backhaul code; the build sha in
`hello.app_ver`, A29, is what told them apart). Tony drove the console from the Windows browser; I watched `/api/state`
and the MC log (`~/mc-20260912-field.log`, session store `~/.brx-mcp/mc/session-43db6f6b.sqlite`, three matches).

**One fix before anything worked (F136, closed).** The tunnel came UP in seconds on the first try, but every request
through it was 502: cloudflared's origin is `127.0.0.1:<ws-port>` and MC bound the node socket to the resolved LAN IP
only. Neither the stand-in tests nor the `--public-url` smoke run could see it. Fix: bind `0.0.0.0`, advertise the LAN IP.

**Proven, in order.** Join over cellular with Wi-Fi off (hello through the tunnel, MC stamps `reach: backhaul`) · a
stranger's hello without / with a wrong secret closed `4004 no_secret`, no record left · derived coverage FULL 2/2 on real
phones · a live FFA with every kill scored over backhaul and **KILL CONFIRMED on the shooter's HUD** (B30's gate) · END
from MC, result to both HUDs (losers included), recap settled · the A25 background log sync pulled both phones' logs with
no player action · store-and-forward across a REAL data loss: data off on the Pixel 10 for ~2 min mid-match, events made
while dark, data on → the ring flushed (last seq 309), the recap re-settled with exactly-once credit (TONY shots 2→12,
deaths 1→2, OTHERGUY kills 1→2, post_end=1) and the result was re-sent to both phones (LOSE / WIN) · tunnel down and back:
both nodes stale-not-gone at once, QR fell back to LAN-only, UP again in ~6 s on a new hostname, and after a clear-app-data
both phones rejoined as NEW node ids and were rebound to their players by gun name (A5.5) · Speed Reload works on the gun.

**What it cost, the two big ones.** (1) **F153 reconnect latency:** with data off the pub dial fails at once, the ladder
falls to the unroutable LAN url, and that dial has no pre-open giveup, so the phone sat in Android's ~2 min connect timeout
before trying pub again — ~3 min to rejoin after data returned, and a fresh QR scan waited behind the same hung dial after
the tunnel restart. Tony: "impatient players won't wait; it feels like something is wrong and we need to reboot systems".
(2) **F156 no way back to SCAN QR:** once the HUD holds an address there is no scan control in any state, so a tunnel
restart (new hostname, and no LAN path to learn it by `join`) stranded both phones until their app data was cleared.
(3) **F140 DNS:** the home router negative-cached the new hostname because a phone asked in the first seconds; ~250 s to
resolve (1.1.1.1 and carrier DNS had it at once). MC must not announce UP until a DNS-over-HTTPS lookup answers. For the
Pixel 4 I set Private DNS to Cloudflare over adb for the day (reverted after) — exactly the per-phone setup the feature
must not need. (4) **F142:** two --demo players were restored into the real session with one terminal line as the tell.
(5) **F146:** the §2.1 one-magazine guard blocked the push twice, once on a pistols-only fallback nobody chose, once on a
secondary sidearm against a pool that included Body Armor.

**Everything else Tony logged** is F135-F157 and F161, K7-K8, S37-S41, D5 in FOLLOWUPS (gun picker refresh = F161, gun-first join, label
alignment, QR density, the dead Android sweep, sniper chip, UNKNOWN network word, amber-on-backhaul, the unreachable
weapon info page, READY before the gun echo, hit during redeploy, low-health loop after death, missing streak medals,
locked editor after an unsaved customize, headset blinking green after redeploy, tie shown as LOST, WRONG WI-FI for a
stale internet node, the log-pull storm of 126 re-pulls, shields as a config option, a volume knob, Quick Switch with one
slot, gamertag on the gun card, voice preview = intro line, the INTERNET/LAN tag naming, hide an empty AFTER THE WHISTLE,
pistol balance). Fix lanes ran the same afternoon on branch `field-2026-09-12` (app transport · HUD · MC server · MC UI),
then a polish loop; see the next entry / the PR.

**Rig notes.** `pkill -f <launch string>` in the same shell command kills the shell itself (exit 144; three times) —
kill by listening port. The WSL resolver (10.255.255.254) lags minutes on new trycloudflare names; probe with
`curl --resolve` or a DoH lookup, never `getent`. Pixel 4 connect port was 38219, Pixel 10 45527 (pair ports change);
mDNS does not cross WSL. A `cat >>` with a mistyped path and no heredoc hangs the shell on stdin.

## 2026-09-13 (desk overnight → field midday) — the 2026-09-12 night's measured causes, the fix run they drove, and ⭐ a range control that killed three theories at once

**The night.** Four guns, outdoors and in. A team deathmatch where the guns were lit correctly blue and yellow and no
cross-team hit registered at all. A tagger that stayed in the game after the whistle, twice. A phone stuck in utility
mode on both an Android and an iPhone. Four guns connected with two showing in the lobby. Phones that could not reach
Mission Control at all at the start. And, outdoors, hits that stopped landing at 30-40 ft.

**Measured causes, not guessed.** `set_config` dumped every invalid-team player onto `teams[0]` on a mode switch, so
FFA to TDM put everyone on blue; and a silent un-push left `lobby_pushed` false with no re-push, so a team drag never
reached the guns' `$TID`. With friendly fire off, a team-rejected shot emits no `$HIR` at all, which is precisely the
zero-hit result observed. Both are fixed and both are now refused rather than silently possible.

**The proof layer.** The deeper problem was that nothing on screen distinguished "MC knows the game" from "the guns
hold it". Four proofs now answer that (ack currency, weapon echo, health pool, heartbeat config id), a RE-PUSH CONFIG
button cures the three that a push can cure, and `python -m brx_mcp.mc.diag <session.sqlite>` replays a match's
delivery afterwards. Where a proof cannot be made, it says so rather than showing green: an unchecked cell is a grey
dash, never a pass and never a fault. Two defects of exactly that shape were shipped and caught during the work, one in
each direction, both by looking at a screenshot after every assertion had passed.

**The range question, and what the operator measured.** This is the part worth remembering.
Three of my theories died in one afternoon, none of them to reasoning.
- Native, **in a real free-for-all game**, at 80 natural paces (about 200 ft), grey daylight: hits on every shot with
  both the AR and the shotgun, in **both** the indoor and the outdoor toggle state. So the game path reaches ~200 ft
  and **a stuck indoor mode is not what shortened range on the twelfth.**
- The toggle is instead a **beam-width control**: indoor tolerates the sight's dot about 1.5 dot-widths off centre,
  outdoor about 3. Roughly double the aim tolerance, measured on three guns. That is the first quantified difference
  between the two states, and it is a better justification for the venue reminder than the range story it was built on.
- Our `$WEAP` t41 already ships at the stock catalogue value for every venue, so it matches native. Run E is moot.
- The `iRPower, soundOnHit, rangeOutdoor, rangeIndoor` field list that reads like a venue control is recorded in
  `protocol/brx-protocol.md` as the wrong shape, already bench-probed, and emitted no IR.

What survives: the FFA game outdoors on the twelfth had friendly fire ON, so team logic was not masking anything, and
it still only registered close hits. Range really was short under our config. **The only remaining suspect is the
difference between our compiled frames and native's**, and one measurement settles it: MC's config pushed to that same
gun, same spot, same light, fired at the far mark and again at 30-40 ft. Confound to respect: today was grey daylight,
the twelfth was night, though darkness normally helps IR rather than hurts it.

A geometry note that makes the twelfth worse, not better: the beam's angular cone is fixed, so what makes close range
easy is that a headset subtends a far larger angle at 30 ft than at 200. The game failed at the easy distance.

**Method notes worth keeping.** Two commits were silently orphaned during the run and both were caught only by
comparing a committed blob against the working tree, never by `git status`. A clean merge is not evidence: one merge
was textually clean and semantically wrong, leaving a new port override unreachable from exactly the three test steps
nobody had ever executed. And a generator that wipes its output directory before binding its port produced an empty
directory that looked exactly like success; I committed that deletion before catching it.

**Bench items filed:** F165-F195, including the range control (F170), quantifying the cone in real angle (F171, F195),
what a gun emits after a `$WEAP` write (F166), and whether an armour grant clamps at the `$PSET` ceiling (F167).

## 2026-09-14 (desk) — the Stick takes its kind from Mission Control: the wire turned out to need no amendment

Tony's design call: the M5StickS3 should be programmed at MC during setup — respawn point, capture point, bomb
site, powerup, whatever the mode wants — and then carried out and placed. Written up as `spec/utility.md` §5g,
filed as **H8**. This entry records the one thing here that was measured rather than designed.

**The question.** Every sentence in `spec/utility.md` §5b/§5c says "utility **phone**". Does MC's arming path
actually require a phone, or is "phone" just the first client that happened to exist? If it required one, a
Stick would need a `contracts.md` amendment, a new `node_type`, or a private side protocol — and the third
option was the one worth killing early, because a second protocol is a second thing to keep in sync, which is
this repo's worst defect class (F99's warning about a third engine).

**What the code says** (read 2026-09-14, not run):

| checked | found |
|---|---|
| `mcp/brx_mcp/mc/envelope.py:37` | `hello` requires the KEYS `("node_id", "node_type", "app_ver", "seq_next")`. `node_type`'s VALUE is never validated against a vocabulary |
| `mcp/brx_mcp/mc/state.py:2239` | `set_station()` gates on exactly one string: `(self.nodes.get(nid) or {}).get("node_type") != "utility"` |
| `mcp/brx_mcp/mc/types.py:613` | `NodeView.platform` is `str | None` — free text, rendered, never matched on |
| `types.py` `STATION_KINDS` | all five kinds are already armable; `station_config` server side has been built since 2026-09-11 (F104) |

So an ESP32 that speaks the M-NET envelope **is** a utility node, with no amendment, no new kind and no MC
change. The arming half of this feature was already written and shipped for phones; the Stick inherits it by
sending four message kinds (`hello` · `welcome` · `station_config` · `status` at 2 s). That is the finding: the
work is entirely Stick-side, and it is smaller than it looked.

**Two things the read also settled.**

*A Stick cannot scan MC's QR*, which is the field default for a phone. That makes contracts §5's "mandatory
floor" (the typed address) genuinely mandatory here rather than a fallback: mDNS `_openbrx._tcp` is the
intended path and the serial console is the floor, so both get built or the box is undeployable on a router
that blocks mDNS.

*The radio plan falls out of §5b's existing rule* — "Setup needs WiFi; play does not." The S3 has Wi-Fi and BLE
on one shared 2.4 GHz radio, and a station needs no MC contact once the match is live, so the Stick joins at
muster, takes its config and drops the association for the match. ⚠ Recorded as reasoning, NOT as a result:
the coexistence jitter on the advert interval and the battery delta are both asserted from the shared radio,
neither has been measured, and **no Stick has ever been powered on** (H7's three gates are all unrun).

**What this does not do.** MC can arm all five kinds, but only **respawn** (§4) and **control** (§5d) have a
built player side; K2/K3/K4 do not. An operator who arms a Stick as a bomb site gets a box that correctly says
BOMB SITE and a field on which nothing happens. And `_refuse_station_change_in_play()` still refuses any
assignment once the phase is ARMED or LIVE — per-game reprogramming is the designed flow, mid-match is not,
and that rule is about the players' guns being re-armed, so it does not soften for a Stick.

**Left open under H8:** `STATION_SOURCES` has no value for a Stick (it is `ir_station` when shot to capture and
behaves like `phone` when it counts adverts), and `control{cmd:"release_utility"}` has no meaning on a box with
no HUD to return to — §5g.7 proposes dropping to UNASSIGNED rather than ignoring the operator.

**Amended the same day — the Wi-Fi link is a MODE, not a phase.** The "drop the association for the match"
plan above was written for a field. Tony's answer: build the capability to *stay* connected, for hills placed
around the house and still inside the house AP — which is verbatim the §5e roaming-hills use case (F95: one
hill in the garage, another on the porch, both on the house AP). So §5g.4 was rewritten as two association
modes, `muster` (default, drop for the match) and `held` (stay linked all game), and §5g.8 added for what
`held` is for.

Two consequences worth recording rather than discovering later. **The coexistence jitter moves onto the
critical path.** Under `muster` Wi-Fi and BLE never contend during play, so the shared-radio question could be
dodged; under `held` it runs for the whole match and becomes the bench number that decides whether `held` is
trustworthy at all. Still unmeasured, now load-bearing. **And the firmware must not assume the socket dies at
go-live** — no tearing down the WS stack, no discarding `node_key`, no stopping the 2 s heartbeat — because the
two modes differ only in *when* the association is dropped, which is one call site if it is written that way
from the start and a rewrite if it is not.

Checked while writing §5g.8, all three negative: `lan_coupled` appears **nowhere** in the code (not the mode
catalog, not `compile.py`, not `types.py`); `CONTROL_STATE` in `app/src/control.js` is still
`{held:1, contested:2, rising:4, falling:8}`, so the **`hot` bit is unimplemented on every side** and the Stick
is free to be the first to emit it; and its value is **16** per §5d.3, which is the authority — §8 5e's "byte
10 bit 5" names the same bit loosely, and a firmware that read it as 32 would be silently wrong.

boundaries now declare their inputs and state. Untrusted decoded bodies use `Record<string, unknown>` and narrow
session, join, player, start, event and command fields before use rather than passing wire data through `any`.

The polish loop found that `skipLibCheck` exempted the generated declaration from the new gate, that the first
pass used broad `any` types at the wire boundary, and that the declaration renderer duplicated the console's
alias/interface renderer. All three medium findings were fixed. A fresh correctness, boundary-safety and DRY
pass found no remaining medium-or-higher issue. Generated files are current and 29 generator tests pass. Full
validation: MCP system 1754 passed with 74 optional/dirty-shot skips; MCP extras 1827 passed with one dirty-shot
skip; the phone strict typecheck, all 501 phone tests and build passed; console typecheck/build, 514 tests and
24-step browser e2e passed; site build and 102 Playwright tests passed. Post-commit site-shot recapture remains
required because phone source changed.

## 2026-09-16 (desk) — contract-DRY execution, F42.14 stage pyright

Removed the gun stage from the pyright exclusion. `GunStage` now uses the shared `GameConfig`, `FrameBundle` and
`Player` contracts and local TypedDicts for its profile, decoded adverts, station presence, hill, stun and guided
walkthrough state. The HTTP page's action messages have per-message shapes; the action allow-list derives its
accepted keys from those shapes. Raw page JSON validates its common tag before dynamic dispatch, and an MC pull
validates the response root and config mapping before compilation. Existing method-level coercion and 400/502
behavior remain intact.

The polish loop found and fixed an unsound TypeGuard that promised all action fields after checking only the tag,
and duplicate action-field lists that could drift between the checker and dispatcher. A fresh correctness,
boundary and maintainability pass found no remaining medium-or-higher issue. Focused validation passed with zero
pyright errors across all 64 package files and 114 stage, mirror, server and pyright tests. Full validation:
MCP system 1755 passed with 74 optional dependency skips; MCP extras 1829 passed with no skips. No app or console
source changed, so the phone, console browser and site gates completed immediately before this phase remain current.

## 2026-09-16 — the 2026-09-13 evening playtest, written up from the MC store

Desk work, no hardware. Tony brought notes from the Sunday-evening playtest; this entry records what the
store says about them. Full sheet: [`../archive/game-test-2026-09-13.md`](../archive/game-test-2026-09-13.md). Ids filed:
**F206-F216**.

**Which session, and when.** Tony recalled it as 09-14. `~/.brx-mcp/mc/session-3782dc77.sqlite` dates it
**2026-09-13, 18:30:36-18:50:18 EDT**, and the three `GUN ECHO ≠ CONFIG` numbers in his notes match this
session's six `ack_config` rows exactly. So it is the **evening** of the same Sunday as the `$GSET` t2 range
control. Two players, three matches, both phones on APK 0.2.1+e19c0c8.

**What made this session cheap to diagnose.** The store carries the **compiled head MC actually pushed to each
gun** (`matches.config._heads`) and **six node-log uploads** with the BLE frame ring. Nine of the eleven rows
were root-caused without touching a gun. That is worth protecting: the 2026-09-11 session had neither, and
half of its rows needed hardware to get past a guess.

**The controlled comparison the three matches happen to form.**

| # | mode | venue | `$GSET` t1 (FF) | t2 | TIDs | hits | deaths |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ffa | indoor | **1** | 0 | both `$TID,1` | 28 | 28 |
| 2 | **tdm** | outdoor | **0** | 0 | 1 vs 2 | **0** | **0** |
| 3 | ffa | outdoor | **1** | 0 | both `$TID,1` | 10 | 2 |

Venue moves and hits keep coming; friendly fire moves and they stop dead. Match 2 fired **116 shots** (103 +
13) and recorded not one `hit_taken` and not one `death` in 42 s. That is **F206**, and it is **F49** at game
scale: `compile.py:978` sets `friendly_fire=(mode == "ffa")`, so every team mode we ship sends the value that
registers nothing.

And the store narrows *why* nothing matches. All 38 `hit_taken` facts carry **`shooter_team: 0`** while both
shooters were on **`$TID,1`** — and `protocol/brx-protocol.md` §5 documents `$HIR` t4 as the shooter's
effective `$TID & 3`, i.e. it should read 1. The emitted word is not carrying the team we set, and a receiver
comparing 0 against its own TID of 1 or 2 can never match. Note `shooter_num` is correct in the same frames,
so token 3 works and token 4 does not. ⛔ It is **not** `$PSET` t2 — a first draft of the write-up named that
and it is bench-proven inert (0/1/7 identical, protocol §3); the refutation is kept here because re-deriving a
dead theory is this repo's expensive mistake. The measured claim is narrow: *on a gun set to `$TID,1`, `$HIR`
t4 comes back 0*. The TDM match emitted no `$HIR` at all, so `$TID,2` is unread.

**Two facts already on disk sharpen that into something testable, and they point opposite ways.** Callsign's
own arm contains **no `$TID` frame at all** — `protocol/captures/2026-08-23-two-tagger-combat.txt`, and
`compile.py:1218` already says so in as many words — yet that capture's victim stream reads `$HIR,4,0,1,1,9,0,3`
twenty-three times, i.e. **shooter team 1**. So a stock-armed gun emits a real team without `$TID`. Against
that, our own bench (2026-09-07, the `$TID` row in `protocol/brx-protocol.md` §3) recorded tid 4 → team 0 and
tid 5 → team 1, so `$TID` *does* reach the word — which makes our 0 on `$TID,1` a contradiction with our own
measurement, not merely with the doc.

The suspect that reconciles them, untested: we send `$TID` as the **last frame of the head**
(`compile.py:1238`), a position that same comment block admits is ours and not Callsign's, and `$SPAWN` arrives
later — carrying, since **A23** (2026-09-12), the live `$SIR` table. Nothing re-sends `$TID` at spawn; the only
re-send in the tree is the team-change flip (`compile.py:1397`). Protocol §3 already records that `$SPAWN`
clears a painted LED colour. If it also resets the emitted team, every symptom follows and the fix is one
frame. Worth saying out loud that this would make F206 a **regression introduced by A23**, which is checkable
against the 2026-09-01 TDM session before anyone believes it.

**Two negatives worth as much as the positive.** `$GSET` t2 was **0 in all three heads** (the 14:04 pin was in
the running MC and `DRIVE_IO_MODE` was `"off"`), and **outdoor FFA registered at a rate comparable to indoor
FFA** — so the 2026-09-12 "everything outdoors is unplayable" pattern did not recur. The t2 fix held up in a
real game. It is outdoor evidence only: **F198**, the indoor phantom-hit question, is untouched.

**F201 is answered, and it was us.** Three weapons, six acks: the magazine is right every time and the reserve
is **exactly half** every time — sniper+extended_mags `8/24` vs `8/48`, amr `14/28` vs `14/56`, burst_rifle
`36/108` vs `36/216`. The half is not an approximation; it is **the t40 value MC itself wrote into the same
`$WEAP` frame**, since `compile.py` keeps the captured-frame invariant `tok17 == 2 * tok40`. The gun's `$ALCD`
reserve mirrors t40 and `frames.py` compares it to t17, so the check **cannot pass on any weapon on any gun**.
F201's question ("does the gun clamp reserve to a ceiling, or halve it?") had no correct answer: neither.

The instructive part is why no test caught it. `test_mc_config_proof` exercises the echo check against MC's
**own synthetic frames**, where both sides of the comparison come from the same generator — the same shape as
the id-uniqueness guard that scanned `## ` headings, and the sticker-id guard that read tracked files only. A
guard correct for the place it looked, and blind where the evidence lives. The fix carries a test built on a
real captured head or it repeats.

**The incident that ended the session**, at 18:47:34: a 45-damage hit, then ROCCO's status is **byte-identical
for 105 seconds** (`alive=True hp=45 armor=5 ammo=36 shots=25 src=gun`) while his node log shows no `☠ down`
and the frame ring shows `$BUT` press/release pairs still arriving. The trigger was being pulled and nothing
fired. `pool_src` said `gun` the whole time. **There is no staleness detector on the pool anywhere** — a gun
silent for two minutes is indistinguishable from a healthy idle one, on the HUD and on the MC board alike —
and the only exits an operator has are a whole-lobby RE-PUSH or ending the game. Tony ended the game. That is
**F208**, with **F212** (a `respawnGate:"trigger"` under an `auto` config) as the probable second half.

**And the respawn delay is not a delay.** In a game with `delay_s: 5`, twice: **five deaths and five respawns
inside one second**, every respawn at +0.0 s after its death. Outside the bursts, hits land at +1.6 s, +2.9 s
and +3.0 s after a respawn — the first field look at **F121/A23**'s spawn protection, whose bench gate has
never been run. Either it is not landing or it ends at `$SPAWN` while `write gun take` lands ~3 s later. The
asymmetry is exactly what Tony reported: hit reception returns before the trigger does (**F209**).

**The evidence is now committed.** `docs/evidence/2026-09-13-session-3782dc77/` holds the compiled heads, the
six acks, the event timeline, the deduplicated status stream and the six node logs, sanitised, with the
`extract.py` that rebuilds them and refuses to write a file if a sticker id survives. The store was ~900 KB on
one MacBook; the extract is ~100 KB and in git.

**Left explicitly unsettled.** Whether the sniper's extended-mags reserve is really 48 or really 24 (**F215**)
cannot be read off the store now that we know the echo field is a mirror — it needs one gun fired dry with a
count, which also settles A10c. **F210** (HUD loops if connected before the headset) and **F216** (the dead
flash while alive) are uncaptured; F216 should not be chased until F209 and F208 are fixed, since a burst of
five death/respawn pairs in a second will leave any LED state machine behind.

## 2026-09-16 (bench, evening into 2026-09-17): two-gun bench for F206, then a full game through Mission Control

Two-gun bench for F206: `Tactix-9498` as player 5 on `$TID,1`, `Tactix-FE30` as player 6 on `$TID,2`,
`$GSET` t1=1 (friendly fire on), no HUDs. A head then `$SPAWN` gave the correct `$HIR` t4 on both guns. The
phone's real spawn write ($PSET, the ten-row $SIR table, then $SPAWN) gave t4=0 both ways: the field bug
reproduced at the bench. A live `$TID` sent after that write restored the correct team. A split test found
the cause: a lone `$PSET` on a live gun cleared the team; the lone `$SIR` table did not. The rule now
shipped: send `$TID` after the last `$PSET` of any write, in the engine, the stage and the driver alike.

Step C ran through Mission Control with a Pixel 4 and a Pixel 5. A team deathmatch with friendly fire off
registered 43 or more hits, all with `shooter_team` 1 or 2, including after eight respawns (match ids
a30a17a859 and 7a12305958). F207 is fixed: the echo check now reads the gun's reserve against `$WEAP` t40.
The first config push acked on both guns after the echo-window fix. The trigger stayed dead until T-0,
confirmed by Tony: dead during the countdown, live in the match, live again after each respawn.

A Pixel 5 scan storm was measured: about eight Bluetooth scan stop/start cycles per second, 4,576 skipped
frames in 51 seconds. This explained the 13-second countdown gap between the two phones, the 3-second white
flash on the Pixel 5, missed taps, and the late kill sound. It is fixed.

Built and verified at the desk, not yet confirmed on hardware: A44 spawn protection, capped at 2.1 s, holds
until the gun can fire; app 0.3.0 carries `APP_MINOR` 3; A43 lets an operator start the next match without
picking a mode again; a pre-arm check; A45 and A46 report a stale pool; F209 now allows one death per life
(the 2026-09-13 burst was an outbox flush sorted by receive time, not a real burst); F210 adds a flap
backoff for a gun link that keeps dropping; F211 tells the HUD when Bluetooth is off; F212 fixes the
respawn gate label; F213 fixes the armour ceiling; a `--bench-volume` of 55 is available at Mission Control;
the Armory can claim a gamertag without picking a team; the venue popup is now a manual link instead of a
popup; the ALT procedure is corrected (press ALT quickly in succession during native configuration, at
power-on; Tony); the HUD shows when a control is pressed; and a ready player shows green READY in the
lobby too.

Polish loop: three iterations, no Critical finding. Fixes landed in commits fbeb9b62, 0cd811fd, a0ee6e19,
149981be, c913e26b and 313ae7ff.

Validation at the end of the session: the app suite passed 548 tests; the MCP suite passed 1886 tests with
two expected failures (stale site screenshots, and the published build still reading 0.2.1); the console
suite passed 539 tests; the full HUD screen-truth suite passed 346 checks.

Method notes: the bench box ran out of memory, from Gradle daemons plus another session's test suites
running at the same time, and the test harness killed Mission Control twice. Mission Control was run
detached for the rest of the session. A subagent used `git stash` despite the shared-worktree rule; nothing
was lost.

## 2026-09-17 (desk) — the parallel test suite

**Why.** Agents ran the four suites and the browser gates one after another: about 30 minutes, and 22 of them were
`ui:screens`, whose ~330 steps each wait out a 1.6-4.2 s page timeline. Serial timings on the 32-core WSL box:
screens 1310 s, mcp 85 s, site 52 s, the seven `webapp/mc` e2e scripts ~250 s, the rest ~30 s. `ui:e2e` failed
41/79 on main.

**What changed** (branch `test-suite-speed`). `npm run test:all [-- --ui]` runs every suite at once inside a memory
budget. `run_tests.py` runs one process per file, screens.mjs shards its steps, site Playwright runs fully parallel,
and no gate binds a fixed port. Measured: the unit tier takes 28 s; `--ui` (16 jobs) takes 127 s at a 6.5 GB peak
proportional set size (PSS). Without the budget the peak was 12.4 GB; per unit, a jsdom vitest worker is ~300 MB, a
Playwright worker ~300 MB, a screens shard ~240 MB, an e2e script ~600 MB. Budgets of 12, 8 and 4 GB took 97, 126
and 279 s, all green.

**Tests that failed under load, and why.** The app transport tests raced 10-70 ms real timers, and a failed one
left its Transport alive, so `node --test` hung for 10 minutes: they now run on `mock.timers`. Two backhaul tests
read a child's command line before its exec finished. The mock MC's 220 ms repush ack raced `games-load` and the
`game-edit` e2e step. The site 12j click could leave the HUD demo below the fold. `ui:e2e` had four stale steps and
a fake gun that did not echo the pushed ammo after A36, plus one product bug: the DESIGNER still listed the Energy
Launcher that 1c83b745 removed. F205 closed.

## 2026-09-17 (bench then desk): verification bench, operator menu, and a full polish loop

**Bench.** Two guns, Tactix-3D4F and Tactix-E20D, with a Pixel 4 and a Pixel 5, Mission Control running
with `--bench-volume`.

Proven: F206 (team hits) and the trigger held dead until T-0 both still hold. The first config push
acked on both guns. The lobby stayed in sync. A re-push after a health change acked. F215 is closed: the
sniper's HUD magazine and reserve numbers tracked the gun. F217 is closed: spawn protection gave no
damage in the first ~2.4 s after respawn, the protection cap released every time (9 of 9 phone-log lines
read `cap`), and the first-shot release never fired. Tony decided the respawned player must not get an
advantage; a survivor keeping about 0.3 s of the window is fine. F218 is closed: the charge-rifle match
gave no false GUN NOT FIRING report except during overheat lockout, and that false positive is now
fixed. The charge-hold check that F218 also covered is not yet proven clean on its own, so it is folded
into a new row, F247.

Found and fixed today: the Pixel 5 hung on a double-tap connect; a headset-off reconnect looped every 5
to 6 s and now backs off with an escalating delay; a RELINK 34 s into a match now bounds its reconnect;
a spawn write took about 62 s on the Pixel 5 because the plugin's reply channel was flooded by roughly 57
scan results per second, so writes no longer wait on that channel, and the beacon scan and the player
advert now run only when stations are in play, under a 25-per-second guard. The charge rifle's 12-round
cell was spending 10 rounds per full charge against an 80-round reserve (40/80 corrected). ALT reloaded a
one-weapon loadout (fn 98) instead of doing nothing. The HUD kill banner showed a player id instead of a
gamertag. An unread firmware version showed as an amber warning instead of no warning. Heat reached 108
during overheat with no overheat sound: `$PLAY,C19` sounds like the charge-cancel cue, not an overheat
cue, and this stays open. The NIGHT OPS toggle is locked while a match is LIVE. An MC restart mid-match
lost the running match; match resume is now built.

Built at the desk, not yet benched: MC match resume and adopt, the operator menu (RESYNC GUN, FORCE
RESPAWN, RELINK GUN, A47), HARDWARE READY with an ENABLE BACKHAUL button, MARK ALL READY, the energy
gauge (cell pills, NOT ENOUGH ENERGY, HOLD TO RECHARGE), a heat bar and a full-screen OVERHEAT warning at
heat 99 or above, a shot-ready cue, a results overlay, a night skin per player, NIGHT OPS dimming LEDs, a
bench volume of 65, and many other Mission Control UX changes.

Weapon facts (the charge rifle cell and the energy rifle) were coordinated with the brx-weapons session;
those facts belong to that session's own log entries, not this one.

**Polish loop.** Three rounds over today's range, no Critical finding. Fixes landed in commits
b7c095c4, 0003df1a, 16a8f72f, 3a02e263, 476d5b17, faa64843 and f8f7006a.

**Validation.** `npm run test:all -- --ui`: 17 jobs pass. The MCP suite gives the two expected failures

## 2026-09-17 (desk): merging the arsenal rework into the playtest branch

`origin/main` at 08aec5b7 brought the arsenal rework, node-driven recoil (S42) and the F68 headset repaint.
This branch had changed the same node, compiler and catalogue files. Sixteen files conflicted; every one was
resolved to keep both sides. The notable ones: main's amendment collided with A43 and became **A48**; main's
`node.md` sections collided with 3.13/3.14 and became **3.16** (recoil) and **3.17** (F68); main had already
taken **F230-F234**, so this branch's thirteen verification rows moved to **F235-F247**.

**What the merge actually broke, and what fixed it.** Recoil re-pushes `$WEAP` mid-life and follows it with an
`$AMMO` restore of the live counts. This branch has four other `$AMMO` writers: the spawn, the revive, the
operator RESYNC GUN (A47) and the stun disarm/restore (F15). Two of them in flight together lose: an accuracy
write beside a stun disarm hands the player their magazine back, and one beside a spawn puts the previous
life's counts back. `_holdAccuracyWrites(why)` now stands the writer down for 800 ms after each of those,
drops any verify still open, and re-asserts the live value once the hold lifts. The rejoin reconcile also
re-arms the model, because it re-arms the GUN with the frame's spawn counts. Recoil's overheat guard read
`this.overheatLocked`, a seam nothing ever set; it now reads the node's real `_overheating()`.

**The headset strip has one owner.** F68's 5 s repaint and this branch's spawn, revive, hit and operator
paints would otherwise write the same strip on two clocks. Every deliberate paint (`_headset`) now re-stamps
the repaint clock, so the interval is a backstop: it fires only when nothing else has painted for a full
interval.

**The Energy Rifle's overheat.** t38 = 150 switches it on, it locks at heat 99 and does NOT cool on its own:
only the reload lever vents it, about 35 a pull. Our rules hold. OVERHEAT clears `OVERHEAT_SHOWN_MS` (6 s)
after the last evidence, so the word cannot sit there for the whole life when a player simply stops firing,
while the heat BAR keeps reading hot -- the gauge is the standing fact, the word is the alert. A trigger press
is fresh evidence, so the word comes straight back while the player is still trying. A vented reading under
the line clears both the word and the lockout in the same frame. Two tests pin it with the Energy Rifle's own
numbers.

**Two new fields adopted.** The HUD picks the energy gauge from `weapon_class`, not from tokens in the weapon
id, and reads `rounds_per_charge` for NOT ENOUGH ENERGY instead of a hard-coded 10. `rounds_per_charge` lived
only in `weapons.json`, so `Weapon` and `WeaponView` now carry it and the node passes it to the HUD. The
class is wider than the old id guess: the Rail Gun, Plasma Sniper, Ion Sniper and Laser Cannon are energy by
class, so the Rail Gun's two pips became a percentage. Filed as **F248** for a decision, not fixed blind.
The Energy Rifle's new `caution` string needed no code: three screens already render `caution`.

**Validation.** `npm run test:all -- --ui`: 17 of 18 jobs pass. The mcp job fails only on the expected
published-build version check; the site-shots check skips on a dirty tree, and the shots do need a refresh
after this merge.

## 2026-09-17 (bench, office desk): arsenal levers: mid-life `$WEAP`, accuracy misses, Charge Rifle, headset flash

Runbook: [`../archive/bench-weapons-2026-09-17.md`](../archive/bench-weapons-2026-09-17.md). Two guns, Windows brx MCP over BLE, phone
apps closed. Shooter/victim swapped halfway. Full traffic logs: `C:\Users\Tony\.brx-mcp\captures\bench-weapons-2026-09-17-{A,B}.jsonl`
(not in the repo). Common head: `$GSET,0,0,1,0,1,0,0,1,*` (t7 = 0), `$SIR,0,0,,1,0,0,1,,*`, the MC `$BMAP` rows, bench AR.

**Step 1, hit check: PASS.** 3 aimed shots, 3 `$HIR` of 9, victim 999 → 972. A victim `$PSET` HP of **999 is accepted**.

**Step 2, mid-life `$WEAP` (t21 100, t22 50) on a spawned gun: PASS with conditions.**
- The gun keeps firing through writes: 71 shots across 12 writes, next shot about 60 ms after a write. It keeps taking
  hits: 13 hits of 9 landed during 20 writes (armour 70 → 0, then HP to death). The write applies in **30-90 ms**.
- Every write **resets the magazine to t39, the reserve to t40 and live accuracy (`$ALCD` t2) to t21**. `$AMMO,0,27,192`
  sent 50 ms after the write restored 27 exactly (visible 90 ms later).
- A write to a **dead** gun does not revive it (trigger dead, confirmed by Tony).
- A write inside a reload could not be placed: the tool loop took 3.2 s from the lever event to the write and the AR
  reload took **1.75 s** (lever 930.7 s → refill 932.5 s). A reload straight after a write ran normally. Not measured.
- Accuracy walk at 100 ms, t22 = 50: live accuracy steps down 10 per 1-2 shots and reaches 50 in 9-11 rounds.
- **Accuracy-model misses send no `$HIR` at all** (never a zero-magnitude hit over BLE). The victim still plays its
  `missShotHit` near-miss sound (2026-09-09 bench), so the miss is silent to our software, not to the player. Shots matched to victim hits
  by timestamp: live accuracy 90 → 38/40, 80 → 6/6, 70 → 7/7, 60 → 2/6, 50 → 5/12. Small samples.

**Headset flash (Tony's eyes).** Under continuous full auto the victim's hit flash turns **yellow after about 2.5-3 s
(18-30 hits)**, stays yellow for a hit 1.4 s later, and clears to green by itself within about 5 s even while single
hits land about once a second. Every hit in those bursts took full damage and the gun sent no extra frame. My first
reading (HP colour bands) was wrong: Tony corrected it, the headset never flashes red. F227.

**Sensor field at close range.** Aimed headset shots at point-blank and at 2 m reported `$HIR` t1 = **4** (gun body)
while the victim's gun sensor was uncovered; covered with a blanket, the same shots reported **0** (front dome). F228.

**Step 4, Charge Rifle (40/80 cell).**
- A full charge costs **10 rounds** (4 measurements) and +56 heat; a tap costs 1 round and +14 heat. Heat decays about
  30 per second. Rapid taps locked the gun at heat **103** for **4.8 s** (Tony felt 5-6 s); heat restarts from 0.
- A full charge takes 3-4 s to build by feel and **can be held indefinitely**.
- With **6 rounds** left a charge fired nothing, heat read **156**, and two reload-lever pulls did not refill until
  `$AMMO` did. From exactly 10 rounds a charge fires. F226.
- Damage through MC's `$SIR,8,0,,38,…`: charge magnitude 100 → **50**, tap magnitude 20 → **10**, on the headset
  (covered gun sensor), at t7 0 and at t7 50 (t7 sent mid-life). F225.
- `$SIR,8,0,,1,0,0,1,,*` for the same key **replaced** the fn 38 row: charge 90 → 90, taps 20 → 20.
- **`t37` is the tap damage:** t37 30 → taps land 30, the full charge still lands t5 (90). The old reading of t37/t38 as
  heat tokens was wrong for t37 (F229).

**Step 5, headset multiplier at `$GSET` t7 = 0: PASS** (second sitting, both guns re-armed from a full head, volume 65,
victim gun sensor covered, t7 set in the arming head). Burst Rifle (`<0,3>`, victim row `$SIR,0,3,,37,…`), 3 bursts
each. **Control, t7 = 50:** 9 hits on the front dome, magnitude 9, subtype 3 echoed, each landed **18** (x2).
**Result, t7 = 0:** 9 hits, same word, each landed **9** (x1). Dropping t7 to 0 removes the headset multiplier, as S44
ships it.

**Step 6, accuracy recovery: blocked by a gun difference (F230).** Same AR frame (100 ms, t21 100, t22 50), same firmware
(`$VERSION,v4.32,hds.59,4,,devhost.03` on both). `Tactix-3D4F` held live accuracy at 90 for 5 magazines (bursts of
14-32 rounds with 1-4 s pauses), at t7 50 and after a full re-arm at t7 0 (22 shots at 90), resting on the desk (24 at 90)
and swung while firing (29 at 90); with player 1 / team 1 it stepped once to 80 and held for 27 shots. `Tactix-E20D`
with the same frame, a steady aimed hold and t7 0 walked `90 90 80 80 80 70 70 70 60×7 50…` (floor at shot 16).
Headsets sat beside the guns in every run. Recovery timing was not measured.

**F230 debug, same sitting, after a power-cycle of both guns (logs `…-A3`/`…-B3`).** Modes on power-up: 3D4F indoor,
E20D outdoor. Both set to outdoor: 3D4F 28 shots at 90; E20D `90 90 90 80 80 70×8 60×4 50…`. E20D with `$GSET` token 5
(gyro) 0: `90 90 80 80 70 70 60 50…`. 3D4F at `t14` 60 ms, `t22` 0: 80 for 19 shots (median report gap 75 ms; Tony:
"rof was much faster"). Trigger reports: one `$BUT,0,1` and one `$BUT,0,0` per burst on both guns. 3D4F after
`$STOP`, `$CLEAR`, full head, `$SPAWN`: `90 80` then 80 for 30 shots. 3D4F with the same AR also in slot 1: 90 for 32
shots. No on-gun menu setting exists that could differ (Tony). Unexplained; headsets not swapped.

**Step 6 on `Tactix-E20D`: accuracy recovery measured.** AR at 100 ms, t21 100, t22 50, standard head (gyro 1, t7 0).
Walk-down varies between bursts: floor at shot 11, 14 or 21, or still 60 after 19 shots. Recovery after release, read on
the first shot after the pause (90 = a fully recovered first shot, one step below the ceiling): from 50 after **0.33 s → 70**,
after **0.45 s → 80**, after 2.0 s (from 60), 3.7 s and 4.3 s → 90. So recovery runs at about one 10-point step per
0.15 s and is complete in well under 2 s. While native-game mode was tested on `Tactix-3D4F`, a listen-only BLE link
received nothing during a full-auto burst (it answered `$PING` with `$PONG`): native games do not stream events.

**F230 tie-break: a third gun.** `Tactix-9498` (`$VERSION,v4.32,hds.59,4,,devhost.03`, outdoor mode, player 3 team 3,
the same floor-50 AR head): one full-auto magazine, live accuracy **90 for all 32 shots**. Two of three guns do not walk;
`Tactix-E20D` does, and it is the gun the 2026-09-09 accuracy result was measured on.

**Hit rate on `Tactix-9498` (live accuracy flat at 90).** Two full-auto magazines from 2 m at `Tactix-E20D`'s front dome
(gun sensor covered): **64 shots, all at accuracy 90, 59 hits (92%)**, all on sensor 0, shooter team 3. The probability
model works on the non-walking gun; only the walk is missing. During the same fire the victim headset's sustained-hit
flash turned **blue** (victim team 1); the earlier yellow flash was on a team-2 victim, so the F227 colour is likely the
victim's team colour (Tony's reading).

**F230, more negatives.** Battery (`$VOLTS`): `Tactix-E20D` walked at 84-97%; `Tactix-3D4F` held at 72-93%; `Tactix-9498`
held at 86-90%. BLE `$QUERY,*` (confirm, Tony's OK) on E20D and 9498: identical status arrays apart from player and
team. USB `QUERY` on 3D4F (COM5, `python -m brx_mcp usb-query`) diffed against the saved records of the other three: all
`v4.32`, `PCB-5`, `BTchip- 4`, `devhost.03`, `hds.59`, FieldID 1, PlayerID 0, NRFhost 1, NRFslave 1, devHost 1, tested
by the same initials. Gun burn-in and laser power are recorded on E20D and 9498 only, so the factory fields put the
walking gun and a non-walking gun in the same group. No hardware or stored-record difference explains F230. Attempts to make `Tactix-E20D` stop walking, all negative:
fresh USB record (only the name and the device PlayerID differed from August; the PlayerID mirrors the last `$PSET` id and
read 0 after arming as player 0, and the gun still walked to 50 in two magazines); a power cycle with the headset off and
back on (still walked, two magazines). A scan of every Callsign capture in `protocol/captures/raw/` for host-to-gun
commands our bench head never sends found only presentation commands (`$PLAY`, `$PLAYX`, `$HLED`, `$GLED`, `$SFLASH`,
`$NAME`, `$HLOOP`), none plausibly tied to accuracy.

**A written ceiling works on a non-walking gun, and misses reach the target as near misses.** `Tactix-9498` armed with
`t21` = `t22` = 50 (a mid-life `$WEAP` + `$AMMO`): the first `$ALCD` already read 50. Single shots from 2 m at
`Tactix-E20D`'s covered headset: **32 shots, all at accuracy 50, 12 hits (38%)**. Tony heard the near-miss whizz-by on
about half the shots at the target. Together with `Tactix-E20D` at 50-60 (7 of 18, 39%): live accuracy 50 is about a
38-40% hit rate, 90 about 92-95%, so the value is not a literal percent below 90.

**Step 4c: `t38` switches the Energy Rifle's overheat on.** `Tactix-E20D`, Energy Rifle frame with only `t38` = 150 added
(`…,D122,,,150,300,300,75,*`), 300-round cell. 54 trigger presses, 166 shots over about 110 s. Full auto raised heat about
+3 per shot net (`$ALCD` token 5: 90, 93, 96, 99) and firing stopped at **99**, about 30 shots or 2.9 s from cold. Presses
during the lockout fired nothing. Every stop came with 245-268 rounds left (not ammo). Heat did not fall by itself while locked:
it read 102 at the stop and 102 → 67 on the first lever pull 14 s later, then 32 and 0 on the next two pulls, and the gun
fired again from the same magazine (268). A held pull vented 100 → 65 → 30 → 0 in 0.5 s and then recharged the cell.
Each lever pull vents about 35 heat; a locked Energy Rifle needs the lever to recover (the Charge Rifle cooled by itself).

**Overheat sound by ear (Tony).** Played on `Tactix-E20D` with `$PLAY,<id>,2,6,,,,,*`: `D122` (the captured Energy
Rifle `t35`) "sounds like it reloaded"; `C19` (Charge Rifle `t35`) is the charge's early-release sound; `C04` not chosen;
**`D11`** (the SMG's captured `t35`) "was good"; `A16`, `A05`, `A01` not needed. With `t35` = `D11` in the Energy Rifle frame,
a real overheat sounded right ("yeah nice").

**Step 7, shields by `$LIFE` (confirm, Tony's OK).** `Tactix-E20D` armed `$PSET,1,0,45,0,70,…`: spawned at **shield 0**
(`$LCD,45,0,0`), so `$PSET` token 5 is the shield maximum, not a start value. Grants: `$LIFE,0,0,10` → 10, `+20` → 30,
**`+25` → 55** (25 works on shields; F58's failed 25 was an HP grant), `+30` → **70 (clamped at the `$PSET` maximum by the
gun)**. Eight AR hits drained 70 → 7 in steps of 9, the eighth took the last 7 and 2 HP: shield absorbs first. Refill:
7 × `$LIFE,0,0,10` 600 ms apart took the shield 0 → 70 in **3.6 s**, every grant echoed. By ear (Tony): the native
shield-hit sound is right, and a hum plays by itself once some shield is up (the `$PSET` shield loop); **no recharge
sound and no "Shields Online" play on a `$LIFE` grant**. Callsign plays a growing charge sound and then "Shields Online"
itself. Sound picks, auditioned live against real refills (`$PLAY` during a 7 x 10 grant run):
rejected `C08`, `H123`, `H124`, then `N100`, `A34` ("maybe"), `N18`, `N25`, `W12`, then the smooth risers `X04`, `R120`,
`W44`, `X32`, `ST10` (Tony: "none of those", and "it has to be like linear growth, no heartbeat wavelength"). `A08`
(ShieldOnHeal by ear, 2026-09-11) worked. Final pick: **`A34` played twice** during the refill, once at the start and once
halfway, then **`VA6Y`** "Shields Online" ("that last one was the best one yet"). Tony dropped the "Shields Depleted" voice line (`VA6X`) in favour of the
existing heartbeat `N74` looping while the shield is down ("if we do it like halo you are very vulnerable when shield is
low"): with 45 HP and no armour a shieldless player dies to 5 AR hits. One cue versus two was A/B'd twice: two is better. **Full cycle, 30 HP + 120 shield (Tony's preferred
shape: small HP, large shield):** the shield took 14 AR hits (120 in steps of 9, the 14th spilling into HP), three more
hits left 6 HP, then the emulated regen ran: heartbeat `N74` x3 while down, about 5 s delay, `A34` at the start and
halfway, 12 x `$LIFE,0,0,10` at 300 ms (shield 0 to 120 in **4.1 s**), `VA6Y` at full. HP stayed at 6: only the shield
returns. One artefact heard and traced: a male "arggghh" just before the recharge was the FIRMWARE pain line from the
`$PSET` Heavy voice slots (the hit that took HP to 6), not the `A34` clip, which replayed clean on its own. Heat restarts at 6 (one shot) after a lockout.

**Not run yet:** step 3 (t41 range, garden), step 4c (Energy Rifle heat), step 6 on `Tactix-E20D`, step 7 (shield
`$LIFE` grants). Both guns were left armed and hittable.

## 2026-09-17 (desk, after the bench): the arsenal rework ships

Merged to main as `08aec5b7`, `npm run test:all -- --ui` 17/17. Four lanes, three polish rounds. The catalogue drops
from 22 rows to **13 visible picks**, gains `weapon_class` (ballistic reloads, energy overheats or charges) and
`pickup_only` heavies, and loses the headset multiplier (`$GSET` t7 = 0, because 4 of the 5 hit sensors are on the
headset and players aim there). The ladder at the 115 pool: AR 1.20 s, SMG 1.33, Burst 1.42, Sniper 1.50, Shotgun and
Marksman 1.60, LMG 1.80, Suppressor 1.96. The Charge Rifle is modelled as one charge plus two taps (about 1 s from
release) with `rounds_per_charge` 10, and its `$SIR` key moved off the function that halved every hit. The Energy Rifle
overheats for real. Recoil is node-driven with F68 fixed first. Range moved from t41 to t2 with starting values.

**What the polish rounds caught, which is the part worth carrying forward:** three guards that could not fail. The
one-magazine guard was asserted as arithmetic only, so reverting the compiler to grade trigger actions left the whole
suite green. The lead rule accepted a lead claimed on `range_band`, a field that never reaches a gun. The declared-only
test checked accuracy but not range. Each is now driven by breaking the behaviour it protects and watching it go red.
The pattern to remember: a guard written in the same commit as the feature tends to assert the feature's own fixtures.

---

## 2026-09-17 (night, desk): two defects found by reading, and the perk catalogue

**Two bugs that no test could see, both fixed on main (`f4e7e263`, `test:all --ui` 17/17).**

1. **The recoil accuracy writer's overheat guard was dead code.** It read `this.overheatLocked`, a flag nothing in the
   engine ever set, so S42's writer was free to push a `$WEAP` into an overheat lockout. It now reads the gun's own
   heat from `$ALCD` token 5 against 99 (F229: the Energy Rifle stops firing at 99, does not cool by itself, and vents
   about 35 per lever pull). The old test asserted the flag, which is why it passed; the new one drives a real heat
   frame. Found by the playtest session reading the file.
2. **`weapons.json` carried two `recoil` blocks in 13 of its 22 weapons**, one from each lane of the morning's arsenal
   merge. JSON keeps the last key, so the first block was dead and the numbers in it never reached the compiler. The
   parsed catalogue was correct and every test passed. The guard now reads the key pairs with `object_pairs_hook`
   before the parser collapses them, over every JSON file in `brx_mcp/mc`. **The lesson is general: a merge that
   resolves "additively" inside one JSON object can silently discard a whole block, and no parser-based test can see
   it.** Also found by the playtest session, by reading the raw file.

**Damage over time, designed (S16, `weapon-design.md` §6.3b).** Tony: "we don't have any damage over time weapons, like
a poison gun". The catalogue has none, and the mechanism has been unblocked since 2026-09-09. The node route is
certain: `$LIFE` takes negatives, the victim's phone keys off the `$WEAP` t3 damage type echoed in `$HIR` token 2 (the
stock enum already has 11 = gas), and every tick after the first hit is local to one phone, so it survives an MC
blackout. ⚠️ The NATIVE route is unproven and was written up as such: fn 24's 1 to 3 delayed ticks were measured against
a **repeating** grenade beacon, while the 2026-08-26 sweep fired each status function once and saw nothing in 18 s, so
one word may give exactly one tick. `bench-perks-2026-09-18.md` §3 now fires single fn-24, 25, 26 and 27 shots and
counts them, and checks whether the stock Energy Launcher row (`$SIR,9,3,,24`) has been ticking victims all along.

**The perk catalogue (`perk-design.md`, S50).** Seven picks, each buying on the lever its neighbour sells: Body Armor at
+25 and preset-aware, Armour Piercing at about -60% damage, Quick Hands, Extended Mags, Quick Switch, Motion Tracker,
Second Wind. The AP price is the correction worth remembering: bypassing armour takes the effective pool from 115 to 45,
a 61% cut, so the -20% the draft proposed would have made it the best gun in the game. The research sweep and the lever
survey are folded into the document's appendix, including the rejected ideas with their reasons, so nobody re-derives
why a movement perk is impossible on a phone with no motion sensor wired. `weapon-design.md` §6.3c does the same for
weapons: the medic gun, the flux beam, the jammer and the crit weapon are all levers we already hold and archetypes the
catalogue does not have.
