## 2026-09-18 (evening bench, two guns + two phones + MC) — the verification bench, and five defects no test could see

**Rig.** Both taggers, both Pixels on 0.3.0 from this branch, Mission Control on the WSL box with
`--bench-volume`. Twelve rows closed; the defects below are the part worth reading.

**The verification rows.** F236 (a mid-match MC restart reclaimed the same match id with both nodes still
live, and the orphan-resume path was not even needed because the session snapshot carried it), F235 (the
operator menu: force respawn twice, resync end to end), F238-F245 (the energy gauge, the full-screen
OVERHEAT and its sound, the results overlay, HARDWARE READY against a real tunnel, MARK ALL READY), F240
(night ops dims the LEDs and the per-player skin toggles), F241 (the shot-ready cue at T-0, and the
trigger stayed dead through the countdown), F247 (ALT on a one-weapon loadout does nothing and the HUD
claims nothing).

**1. Recoil erased shots, then oscillated, then worked (F259).** The writer restored `$AMMO` from a
remembered count while its own `$WEAP` reset the magazine, so the gun never ran dry: Tony saw "31/32 back
and forth", then it settled on 32 and stayed there. The first rebuild flapped on hardware, and tapping the
phone's outgoing frames showed why: the restore's own `$ALCD` looked like a 26-round burst and
re-triggered the degrade, a loop of our own making. With an echo window owned by the write and a shot
account that books the trigger press, the final pass reads:

```
mag 28  degraded, node writes 70      mag 32  gun CONFIRMS 70   (the reset echo)
mag 29..10  70 held for 22 frames     release  crisp 100, confirmed
```

One degrade, one recovery. Tony heard the victim playing near-miss cues through the burst, which is the
independent proof the shots missed: an accuracy miss sends no `$HIR` at all.

**2. The same echo was booking phantom shots.** That reset frame was a 26-round decrement to every
consumer of `_onAmmo`, including `shots`, the accuracy denominator in the recap. Tony's 452 shots for 50
hits was inflated by our own writes.

**3. A player can be dead on the gun and alive on the HUD (F264).** Mid-match: "hud thinks im alive,
headset flashing like im dead. nothing on trigger". The phone read `alive: true, hp: 45, armor: 6` while
the gun was dead and refusing the trigger, so the killing blow's `$HP` never arrived. **The detection
worked** (MC showed `pool_stale: no_fire`) and **nothing acted on it**. A resync at t=71 s did not cure
it; a second resync and a force respawn landed together at t=97 s and he came back, so the respawn is the
probable cure and the two cannot be told apart from the feed.

**4. A bound phone's scoreboard froze and the HUD called it LIVE (F265).** Tony covered the other gun's
sensor, emptied a magazine for zero hits, and asked why his accuracy had not moved. It had not moved
because nothing on that board had: the phone's snapshot was **161 s old** with `wsState: bound`. Phone
held `shots 292, hits 50`; MC held `shots 452, hits 50`. `_boardAge()` prints LIVE whenever the socket is
bound, so it labelled a stale board as current.

**5. A fresh Mission Control never offers to adopt a running match (F261).** MC was stopped mid-match, its
session file moved aside, and restarted, which is the field case of MC on a different laptop. The phones
carried on (`phase: live`, `match_id`, `ws: bound`); MC came up in muster with both nodes `arm: live` and
`orphan_match: None`. `_note_orphan` opens with `if (nid in self.node_player ...)`, so a node must already
be bound to a player, which is exactly what a fresh MC cannot have.

**6. Two words, one pull, measured (F263).** Victim at 250/250 so nothing could die:

```
Shotgun  t5 45 / t12 70:  $HIR,0,0,8,2,45  then  $HIR,4,0,8,2,70   119 ms   armour 250 → 205 → 135
Plasma   t5 25 / t12 80:  $HIR,4,0,8,2,25  then  $HIR,4,0,8,2,80    57 ms   armour 126 → 101 →  21
Control (AR):             $HIR,4,0,8,2,9   alone
```

The second word carries `t12`'s own magnitude, proven twice with different numbers. ⚠ **Those are
Callsign's captured values, not what we ship**: our Shotgun is 20 and 20. The first write-up of this row
said "one-pull kill" and was corrected within the hour, which is the whole lesson: the frame you send is
not the frame the game sends unless `resolve()` gave it to you. No token separates the two words, the gap
varies (57, 88, 119 ms) and the sensor is geometry, so the collapse must key on the live weapon.

**7. "FULL COVERAGE" counts the URL a phone joined through, not what it rides (F256).** Both phones ran on
the venue WiFi with no SIM, and both joined through the `cloudflared` URL. The board read **FULL COVERAGE,
2 OF 2 ON THE INTERNET PATH**. `Session.coverage()` counts a node as covered when `reach == "backhaul"`,
and `reach` records only the join URL. Two phones on one WiFi, leaving through one tunnel, are a single
point of failure, and the operator reads that line before deciding the LAN can die.

**8. The HUD said OUT OF ENERGY while the gun still fired (F257).** The charge rifle sat at **7 of 40 with
no reserve**. The HUD showed the big **OUT OF ENERGY** prompt and the small **NOT ENOUGH ENERGY** note
together, over a gauge reading 20%, and the gun fired a tap perfectly well. A charge weapon with a tap
magnitude (`t37`) can always spend single rounds; it cannot pay `rounds_per_charge` for a full charge.
`hud.js` `energyOut` reads "below a charge and no reserve" as a dead end, and it is not one.

**Also settled by ear:** `VX73` is the reload nag ("yes that is the right sound"), `N101` plays on shield
depletion and `N102` on the first grant of a recharge, which moves the 2026-09-17 decision.

## 2026-09-18 (bench, office, two guns) — the `$SIR` table takes eleven rows, and armour piercing ignores BOTH layers

**Rig.** Two guns, victim and shooter, both driven straight over BLE from WSL (no phones, no MC). Victim
armed with the full shipped table, shooter's primary re-keyed to the cell under test at magnitude 9 so
every shot is directly comparable.

**⚠ The first three shots registered nothing and it was my error, not the hardware.** Team comes from
`$TID`, not `$PSET`, and I had sent `$TID,1` to both guns, so they were team-mates and the victim
correctly ignored friendly fire. Tony spotted it from the LEDs. The control that proved the rig
afterwards: a plain word took armour 70 to 61 with HP untouched.

**1. Eleven rows land (F254, closed).** S50's armour-piercing cell made every compiled table eleven
rows, and ten was the most ever proven. The eleventh row is the one under test here and it worked, so
there is no cap at ten and nothing is being dropped silently. Worth keeping in mind that the compile-side
guard could never have told us this: `assert_armor_piercing_armed` reads the compiled text, not the gun.

**2. fn 2 is real armour piercing.** Magnitude 9 through the `<4,0>` cell: HP 45 → 36, armour unchanged
at 61.

**3. Armour piercing ignores the SHIELD as well (the question that decides a shipped number).** Victim
at HP 45 / armour 70 / shield 120 (`$LIFE,0,0,120` after spawn; a spawn shield is always 0), five shots:

```
$HP,36,70,120   $HP,27,70,120   $HP,18,70,120   $HP,9,70,120   $HP,0,70,120  (died)
```

Nine off health every time. **Armour never moved and the shield never moved.** The victim died with a
full 120 shield and full armour standing. So fn 2 does not drain the layers in order, it ignores them,
and `weapon-design.md` §7.3's second branch is the live one: the Shields preset moves from 30 health to
45, because with 30 underneath a bypass is a hard counter rather than a triangle.

**4. Spawn protection covers the new cell (A44).** Against the disarmed fn-28 twin table, an AP shot
produced a `$HIR` and moved no pool at all: 45/70 before, 45/70 after. The registrar sees the word, the
player takes nothing.

**Method note worth keeping.** `$QUERY` over BLE returns a status array, not the `$SIR` table, so "read
the table back and count the rows" is not a thing this hardware can do. The only honest test of whether a
row landed is to fire the word that row answers and watch the pools. The gun's own `$LCD` (`hp, armor,
shield, ?, mag, reserve`) is the readout to use: it needs no phone.

## 2026-09-18 (desk, two guns, ~2 h): the perks bench, and a red bug closed by accident

Shooter `Tactix-FE30`, victim `Tactix-9498`, `$VOL,70`, bench AR at 9 damage unless stated. Every number below is a
`$HP` delta or an `$ALCD` timestamp off the wire, not an impression. Raw log: the session scratchpad.

**1. fn 24 does not tick damage. It makes the victim's gun cry wolf.** One hand-aimed shot registers
normally on the struck sensor with the pools UNCHANGED, and then the victim raises a `$HIR` **on sensor
0** every **5.07 s**, carrying the original magnitude, shooter and team, pools still unchanged, with no
sign of stopping (13 replays over 61 s). The shooting gun is idle throughout, so the replay is generated
inside the victim. **Only `$SPAWN` clears it.** Each hit starts its own timer: three hits gave
overlapping replays 80 ms apart. Tony, by ear: the real hit sounds normal, then every replay is a
grenade-style ticking build, a pause, a hit sound, a vibration and a green headset flash. A player
cannot tell it from being shot. **25, 26 and 27 do the same; 28 and 35 do not** (three hits each, no
replay in 20 s), so fn 28 remains the right row for a silent beacon.

**2. That is why the Energy Launcher dealt zero damage, and it is now fixed.** Its key `<9,3>` landed on
`gameconfig._SIR_TABLE`'s `$SIR,9,3,,24`. Reproduced and fixed on hardware inside a minute, same weapon,
same word: on **fn 24** a magnitude-115 word moved the victim **999 → 999**; on **fn 1** it moved
**999 → 884**. The row now ships as `$SIR,9,3,,1,0,0,1,,*`. The red banner at the top of
`weapon-design.md` had a one-line cause all along, and the guard tests that used the launcher as their
"deals no damage" fixture move to a synthesised one.

**3. F62 answered: a tagger rolls its own crits.** `$WEAP` **t6** is a straight percentage. At t6 = 20,
9 crits in 64 hits (14.1%); at t6 = 50, 54 in 119 (45.4%). Both within about one standard deviation of
the token. **A crit is the magnitude x1.5 truncated** (9 → 13) and **it echoes on `$HIR` token 6**, so
the victim's node can see a proc land. This is a whole mechanic class unlocked with no firmware change:
a crit-chance perk, a high-variance weapon, and the proc mechanism a poison round needs.

**4. F253 [renumbered **F255** on 2026-09-18: F253 collided with main's closed stun-cell row, which keeps the id] is not a live bug: the reserve a player carries is the catalogue's number.** With the real
compiled AR frame (t17 192, t40 96): after `$WEAP` the gun reports **96**, after `$SPAWN` it reports
**192**, after the `$AMMO` MC always sends it reports **192**. `spawn_ammo()` returns 192 and the HUD is
told 192, so gun and HUD agree. The 96 exists only in the ~200 ms between the `$WEAP` and the `$SPAWN`,
which is the window the 2026-09-13 field reading caught. What survives: a mid-match `$WEAP` re-push
resets the reserve to t40, which is exactly why S42's writer sends an `$AMMO` restore in the same
breath. That restore is load-bearing, not belt-and-braces.

**5. An anti-armour primitive exists, and it is fn 20.** Against an enemy it takes the magnitude from
**armour only**: 70 → 61 → ... → **0** at 9 a hit, health fixed at 972 throughout, and then it goes
completely inert (eleven further hits registered and moved nothing). So a weapon keyed there strips
plates and **cannot kill**. fn 2 and fn 6 are the opposite and are identical to each other: straight to
health, armour untouched, **five hits to kill a full-armour player instead of thirteen**. That number is
the case for pricing Armour Piercing at 0.4x damage rather than 0.8x.

**6. A mid-reload `$WEAP` does not break the reload** (this unblocks the stim pack, S51). Method: a
6000 ms reload so the window is wide, and a magazine size on the write that differs from the reload's
result, so the two cannot be confused. The write applies, and the reload still completes afterwards and
debits the reserve (magazine 0 → 5, reserve 192 → 187, 2.9 s later, twice). ⚠️ My first reading of run A
was "the write ate the reload", and it was **wrong**: the reload had completed silently because the
write had already filled the magazine, so nothing changed and the gun emitted no frame. Tony's "two gun
cocking sounds" was the tell I should have asked about first. Design note: a writer must not treat its
own `$AMMO` restore as final while a reload is in flight.

**7. The Charge Rifle's tap cadence is 285 ms, not the 500 ms placeholder.** Five taps gave 285, 270,
300, 285 ms press to press. The release costs exactly 10 rounds, each tap 1, the charge lands t5 (85)
and each tap lands t37 (20), all confirmed on the wire. So against the 115 pool the kill is a charge
plus two taps, **570 ms from release, not 1.00 s**: the Charge Rifle is meaningfully stronger than the
catalogue claimed. `CHARGE_TAP_CADENCE_MS`, `weapons.json` and §2.2 all move to the measured number.

**8. Sounds and the Energy Rifle recharge.** The Charge Rifle's captured `t35` `C19` is a charge-cancel
noise, not an overheat (Tony, on a real lockout); re-armed with **D11** on the same gun a minute later:
"that one sounds right". It now ships that override, verified afterwards from the compiler's own frame.
The Energy Rifle's full cycle is timed: heat climbs about 1 per shot and **locks at 100** after 76 rounds
in 11.6 s; a HELD lever vents it automatically in three steps of 35 over 540 ms; the cell refills **1.95 s
after release**, 3.81 s after the pull began, debiting the reserve by exactly the rounds restored. **A tap
does nothing**, which is what the field's "lever pull that did not refill" actually was.

**Method note worth keeping:** the one failed measurement today was a shot that registered nothing, and
the cause was a blanket over the headset AND a hand over the gun sensor, so the word had no receiver to
land on. The control that found it took 30 seconds: swap the row to plain damage, fire one, watch 9 come
off. Check the control before believing the result.

**Late addition, same session: the stun cell is fixed too, and it is really a flashbang.** [Superseded the same session: Tony settled on **smoke**, because a flashbang should sting and this deals no damage; the fn 23 row in `protocol/brx-protocol.md` §5 carries the settled name.] F253 was
filed and closed inside the hour. A20's stun put fn 24 on the EMP cell, so it carried the phantom bug we
had just found. **fn 23 is the primitive**: one hit and the victim's live accuracy goes **100 → 0 in the
same millisecond** as the `$HIR`, no pool moves at all, the gun keeps firing and spending rounds but
every shot MISSES, and it recovers on its own (0 → 2 → 4 → 7 → 12 over about 3 s) leaving nothing behind.
Twelve seconds later the only traffic was a battery report. Tony heard it from the other end: the
SHOOTER's gun "played whizzes for several shots", which is the near-miss cue, so a flashbanged player
still puts IR in the air and the person they are aiming at hears it go past. Tony's name for it is
better than ours: **"it's more like a flashbang than a stun"**. Superseded the same session: Tony settled on
**smoke**, because a flashbang should sting and this deals no damage (`weapon-design.md` §6.3e, S53). The
guard moved with it: `test_spawn_protection` asserts the phantom family reaches **no shipped table in
any configuration**, broken once to prove it goes red.

⚠️ One wasted run before that, worth the line: the first fn-23 attempt registered
`$HIR,4,**8**,...,20` and the victim's accuracy never moved. Protocol **8** is the Charge Rifle's tap,
which was still armed on the shooter, while the fn 23 row sat on key `<0,0>`. The null result was
correct and meaningless. Read `$HIR` token 2 before believing one.

## 2026-09-18 (MacBook, PacketLogger, one gun): Callsign's shield recharge, and a Shotgun that kills in one pull

Callsign on an iPhone XS, venue left on OUTDOOR (the default), Shotgun primary; the traced gun is the
victim. PacketLogger showed nothing at first because the iPhone's Bluetooth logging profile had lapsed
(it expires after a few days); reinstalling it from developer.apple.com's Profiles and Logs page fixed
it. Capture: `protocol/captures/raw/2026-09-18-callsign-shield-recharge.btsnoop` (scrubbed to the gun's
ATT records, transcript unchanged), decoded beside it in `protocol/captures/`.

**1. The shield recharge is `$BUMP`, which we had written off as inert (F65).** Callsign's "shield" is
the ARMOUR pool (spawn `$HP,45,70,0`; the shield token never moves). 6.2, 6.3, 6.6 and 6.2 s after the
last hit it sends `$PLAY,W20,3,6,VA8C,,,,*` and then `$BUMP,12,,1,,,*` at alternating ~0.4/0.6 s
gaps; each frame raises armour by 12 within ~50 ms and the last one clamps at 70 (0 → 70 in 2.46 s).
The shield break sends `$PLAY,VA8B` ("Shields depleted") and `$HLED,7,4,90,90,10,15,*`. Our 2026-09-09
bench used the APK's hp/armor/shields order, which is not the wire order.

**2. One Shotgun pull, two words (F71).** At 1149.9 s shooter id 1 landed magnitude 45 and then 70, 88 ms
apart, same sensor, and the victim (45 HP + 34 armour) died. Distance not recorded.

**3. Range.** Callsign's Shotgun `$WEAP` is byte-identical to the 2026-08-26 capture: `t2` 100, `t41` 75,
headset word `t13` 80 / `t42` 30. Its CONFIG implies the 70 word is the only one limited by distance; nothing in this capture varied distance, so that is an inference, not an observation (F254 [renumbered **F275** on 2026-09-18: F254 collided with this branch's closed eleven-row `$SIR` row, which keeps the id]). `$GSET` token 2 was 0 with the
app on Outdoor, so the app's venue switch is not that token.

Aside, not chased: two hits crossed from armour into health with uneven arithmetic (armour 16, hit 18 →
HP 45 → 25; armour 34, hit 45 → HP 45 → 34).

## 2026-09-18 (desk, evening, no gun): the LaserTagMods drive, read into the repo

**Why.** On 2026-09-18 Jay (LaserTagMods) shared his "Everything BRX" drive: the stock gun firmware images
V2_01 to V4_31 (our guns run v4.32), his JEDGE 6.0 ESP32 sources, Battle Company's 2018 "Battle Royale" app,
BC's `$WEAP`/`$SIR`/`$PSET`/`$IRTX` spreadsheets, changelogs and manuals. Six read-only lanes processed them
(reports in `~/jay-drive-private/`, not in the repo); this entry is what the desk integration established and
changed, on branch `jay-drive-integration` (five commits, unmerged). One folder of the drive is restricted at
Jay's request and was not opened; nothing below rests on it. **Evidence level for every firmware fact: a
disassembly of V4_30/V4_31, not a measurement on v4.32.** The four-session bench that turns these readings into
numbers is `bench-firmware-levers-2026-09-19.md` (20-claim checklist).

**1. F206 has a static root cause, and the fix is in.** V4_31 keeps ONE team byte (0x1FFFAE40). `$TID` t1,
`$TEAM` t1 and `$PSET` t2 all write it; the weapon-fire call reads it as the outgoing team; the hit handler
compares it, as a full byte, against the shooter team in the word (which is also the tid ≥ 4 alias bug). Last
writer wins. `gameconfig._pset` hard-coded t2 = "0", and `engine.js` writes one of `pset_pool` (a full `$PSET`)
right before every `$SPAWN`. So MC's head was right after `$TID`, and every gun went live as team 0 at T-0. With
`$GSET` t1 = 0 the firmware read every enemy hit as a same-team hit and dropped it: 116 shots, 0 hits in TDM,
while FFA (t1 = 1) worked. Our "`$PSET` t2 is inert (0/1/7 identical)" row was measured with `$TID` sent after
the `$PSET`, so it could not see this. Fix (`d0f4c729`): every `$PSET` builder takes the team and the compiler
passes the `$TID` value; `$TID` is re-asserted right after every `$SPAWN` (Jay's hosted path sends `$TID` a
second time 2 s after the gun's own start, for the same reason); an infection flip is a revive onto the new team
and a turned player revives with the flip's burst; `assert_team_byte_consistent` refuses a bundle whose `$PSET`
disagrees with its `$TID`, and `test_f206_team_byte.py` breaks the builder and watches it go red. Bench: levers
sheet §1 runs a-f. The `$QUERY` reply carries the team as its second field (F271): the read-back that would have
caught this on every gun at the lobby.

**2. The command rail has three tiers now** (`cbd05419`). `protocol.KNOWN_COMMANDS` names each command with the
token count the V4_30 handler reads and whether v4.32 has shown the effect; the newly understood ones
(`$STUN,<ms>`, `$BUMP,<amount>,<hp>,<armour>,<shield>,<sound>`, `$LIFE`'s mode token, `$PRES`, `$TMP`, `$INVU`,
`$BHIT`, `$FIREX`, `$DIE`, `$TEAM`, `$PID`, `$IRTX`, `$RADSK`, `$UP`, `$KK`, `$IT`) are sent without confirm and
the reply says "not bench-proven". `protocol.DENIED_COMMANDS` is refused even with confirm: factory restore, DFU,
pairing, the paired-device clear, the Arena/Retail IR word-format switch (`$IRT`), factory and laser tests,
zombie toggles, `$SITE` (forces team 2 on a later reset), `$RESET`, the gun's own radio-module frames, the
`SETUP` console word, and `$DPLAY`. The list reaches the phone as `NODE_DENIED_COMMANDS` (generated contract);
`engine._write` and the stage drop such a frame and log REFUSED; `compile()` refuses to build one.

**3. The transport.** The V4_31 main loop reads ONE serial byte per pass from a 1 KB buffer; the parser has no
length limit and no timeout (60 tokens, then it wraps); a lost `*` corrupts the NEXT frame (tokens 1-59 keep
their old text); six audio waits (`$DPLAY` among them, reachable over BLE) block the loop with the port unread;
there is no hardware watchdog. That is a screamer mechanism that no BLE command can reach. Our arm is 43 frames /
1012 B / 75 packets, 13-14 of them fn-28 registrar rows of 21-22 bytes (a packet plus a 1-2 byte runt). The
design of record is `spec/transport-hardening.md` (`ba840b94`): write budget, block pacing, the runt rows, write
with response, `$QUERY` read-back, and a lock-up detector (silence plus no `$PONG` = power-cycle; the other half
of F208). Built now: the deny list on the node, and `brxlink.WRITE_PACING` with the block pause OFF. Filed:
F269-F273. The S42 live-accuracy writer, the biggest load (up to 476 B/s), was removed on
`cut-live-accuracy` (now merged), so this branch left it alone.

**4. What the reference gained** (`protocol/brx-protocol.md`, tagged `[disasm]` `[sheet]` `[apk2018]` `[jay]`):
the `$SIR` switch is fn 0-52 (fn 24-27 are 5/4/3/2 s fuses whose p5 names the cell that fires; fn 34/35 register
on a DEAD gun; fn 23 writes an accuracy modifier of -100; fn 38 halves the damage that reaches HP; fn 30 doubles
from sensor 1 and kills silently; fn 33 kills silently; fn 50-52 repaint only); p5-p8 are Modifier (a stun in ms
at 64+), RangeOutdoor (splash re-emit), Random, RangeIndoor; `$WEAP` t1 is `WeaponIRSource` (every stock melee
= headset only), t2 `GunRangeOutdoor`, t19 `ReloadType`, t20 the full fire-mode enum (7-12 = Burst1-6, which
fits the bench), t25/t26 `MuzzleFlash`/`Volume`, t30-t36 sound names, t41/t42 the indoor ranges; `$PSET` t6 is
`CriticalDamageBonus` and t7-t23 are seventeen sound ids (our "sixteen from t8" was off by one; the code was
right); `$SPAWN` t1 is the starting shield; `$START`/`$STOP` arm and disarm IR reception; `$LCD` t3 is the shield
and t4 the slot byte; `$VERSION` and `$QUERY` fields; the gun emits `$DD,<killer>,<team>` on death (Jay reads
it); the native hosting vocabulary (`$AS` actions and enums, `$SP` winner, `$UP` events, `$KK`, `$IT`, `$RADSK`);
protocol-15 station words (magnitude 6/8/10/50); the gap-sweep leads. **Three places the bench result was kept
over the disassembly:** `$WEAP` t37/t38 (bench: the overheat pair; sheet: HeadsetDirection/Repeat), the `$LIFE`
token order (bench: HP, armour, shield; sheet: the reverse), and the x1.5 crit at t7 = 0 (V4_31 explains it: the SHOOTER multiplies
the outgoing damage by (100 + `$PSET` t6)/100, t6 = 50 as shipped, and the victim's t7 is a second layer; levers
sheet §9 confirms both). Rows corrected: `$BUMP`
"inert" (our 3-token shape asked for nothing), `$STUN` "no-op" (we sent 0 ms), `$PSET` t2 "inert", "dead guns
accept no IR" (fn 34/35 excepted), `$MELEE` (no handler in V4_30), "JEDGE drives Serial1" (it is a BLE central),
the `$GSET` t2 polarity in `callsign-extract` (1 = indoor), the A20 stun row (fn 23, not 24), P16 (shield IS
BLE-writable, our own 2026-09-11 result). Nothing changed on the wire. A late V4_31 trace
removed the one cheap win that looked open: the range tokens t2/t41 (and t13/t42, `$IRTX` field 8) set the
**IR carrier frequency**, `38000 − 125 × (100 − range)` Hz on the gun and 140 Hz steps on the headset, not the
power (power is only the indoor/outdoor duty, 20 % / 38 %); that is the bench range curve, a receiver band-pass
knee. With t1 = 1 the melee swing goes to the headset as an `$IRTX` at range t2/t41 and never reads t13/t42, so
the empty t13/t42 on our melee row is not a K4 lead. t41/t42 replace t2/t13 only indoors and when non-zero
(why t41 was inert outdoors); t37 feeds the headset direction field and max(1, t38) its loop field when t1 is 1
or 2; `$GSET` t3 is stored and never read.

**5. Two corrections to the 2026-09-18 perks bench entry above, from the same reading.** The endless fn-24 replay is
explained: in app mode an expired fuse injects a protocol-9 word, and our `<9,3>` row WAS fn 24, so every replay
re-armed the fuse; with `<9,3>` on fn 1 the prediction is one delayed hit and silence (§8 tests it). And "25, 26
and 27 do the same" needs re-timing: the firmware says 4, 3 and 2 s.

**6. Later the same evening, Tony reversed the S42 cut: live accuracy stays.** The `brx-latest-playtest` session
rebuilt recoil to write on a state change only, at most three `$WEAP` + `$AMMO` pairs per burst, so the load that
motivated the cut is now a budget to meet, not a reason to remove the feature. This branch took `cut-live-accuracy`
back out and matches main on every recoil file, so that session's PR applies on top. The soak tool now carries the
recoil writer (`match`, and `recoil-oscillate` as the worst case), and F274 holds the writer to the per-gun write
budget.

## 2026-09-18 (desk, two guns, ~50 min): firmware levers session 1, first half

Two guns: Tactix-E20D and Tactix-3D4F (renamed from its factory `Tactix2` with `$NAME,Tactix-3D4F,*` at the start of the session).
Driven by the brx MCP tools throughout. This is session 1 of `bench-firmware-levers-2026-09-19.md`, run against
the V4_30/V4_31 disassembly claims.

**F276 Shotgun words.** Tactix-3D4F armed with `$WEAP` t5 = t12 = 20, t6 = 0 (the flat 20/20 frame), fired at Tactix-E20D, 5
single pulls. Every pull gave TWO `$HIR` of 20, 60 to 75 ms apart: sensors 0 and 4 on four pulls, and both on
sensor 0 on the fifth pull, still as two distinct frames that both registered. So the 159 ms "beacon block"
duplicate filter does NOT merge identical words, even when they land on the same sensor. F276 is not a defect:
the published 3-pull kill stands.

**Screamers A1 then A2.** A1: `$DPLAY,A10,4,*` (the shield loop, a looping sound). The gun sent nothing back, no
`$PONG` to two pings 8 s apart, and no reply to a follow-up `$PLAYX,0`. It was silent throughout (no audio played),
and the BLE link dropped about 15 s later. The only words the gun spoke that session were "connected" and "phone
disconnected". After a reconnect it answered `$PING` at once, with no power cycle needed. A2, the control:
`$DPLAY,U37,4,*` (a one-shot sound), answered `$PING` immediately. Reading: a looped `$DPLAY` blocks the serial
reader for at least 15 s and drops the link, but this run recovered on its own; call it a partial screamer rather
than a permanent lock, and keep `$DPLAY` on the never-send list regardless.

**Levers §1, F206, hand-sent frames, `$GSET` t1 = 0.** Run a (`$PSET` t2 = 0, `$TID`, `$PSET` t2 = 0 again,
`$SPAWN`): 3 pulls, 0 hits, reproducing F206. Run b (the real team in both `$PSET` frames): 3 pulls, 6 hits, with
`$HIR` token 4 = 2, the correct shooter team. Root cause and fix confirmed at the wire level. Run f, a real TDM
through Mission Control, is still open.

**Levers §18 then §22.** Reply decodes first: on a live gun at 9 HP, `$QUERY,*` returns `$LCD,9,0,0,0,6,24`, and
30 ms later `$QUERY,1,1,9,0,0,V3I,1,20,T01,0,,0,…` (player id 1, team 1, maxima 9/0/0, respawn sound, gyro 1, slot
0 damage 20 and sound T01, then empty slots). `$LIFE,0,0,0,*` returns `$HP,9,0,0`. Dead-gun probe: a gun killed
by one hit gives `$HP,0,0,0` then `$LCD,0,0,0,0,6,24`, with NO `$DD`. On that dead gun, `$QUERY` answers with
`$LCD,0,…` at once, and the rest of the `$QUERY` body arrives about 2 s later with no trailing `*`. `$LIFE,0,0,0,*`
and the bare `$LIFE,*` both return `$HP,0,0,0`. `$PING` returns `$PONG`. Reading: poll a gun's state with
`$LIFE,*`, and reserve `$QUERY` for a one-off correction, since its reply holds the dead gun's print loop busy for
about 2 s.

**Levers §21, `$TMP` t4 on Tactix-E20D.** `$TMP,,,,-30,,,,,,,,*` dropped `$ALCD` accuracy from 100 to 70 at once, echoed
by the gun's own `$ALCD`. It held at 70 for 60 s with no walk-back. The magazine went 6 to 5 to 4 across two shots,
and the following `$TMP,,,,-60,...,*` left the magazine at 4: no magazine reset. The -60 write read back as 40, so
the value is absolute, not a further subtraction. Pushing the Shotgun's `$WEAP` again left accuracy at 40 (not
cleared by the weapon push), while the magazine reset to 6 and the reserve read 12, the known t40 transient.
Smoke collision: with `$SIR,0,0,,23,0,0,1,,*` armed on Tactix-E20D, one Tactix-3D4F pull (two words) dropped accuracy to 0
with the other pools unchanged; a `$TMP,,,,-30,...,*` sent 2.4 s later then read back as 70. The later write won.
`$TMP,,,,0,...,*` restored 100.

**Method notes.** Wait a beat between a pull and the reading. The MCP instrument sends `$QUERY` and `$LIFE` only
with `confirm=true`. Both guns were power-cycled at the end.

## 2026-09-18 (desk, one gun + the ESP32 IR rig, ~55 min): firmware levers session 1, second half

Tactix-E20D alone, hit by the ESP32 IR rig's emitter (board B, COM8), configured as player 5, team 3, magnitude 9,
protocol 0. The rig emits three words about 90 ms apart on a shot, and every word registered.

**`$STUN`, first attempt.** Sent at t = 94.9 s. The trigger pulls at +3.4 s and +5.5 s gave `$BUT,0,1` with NO
`$ALCD` (no shot left the gun). The pull at +8.0 s fired normally. This run is CONFOUNDED and its numbers are not
the record: Tony pressed reload (`$BUT,2`) just as the stun started, and the Shotgun reloads per shell, so the
reload rather than the stun could explain the missing shots. A control and a clean redo followed.

**`$STUN`, controlled redo.** Magazine refilled first, no reload pulled in either run.
- Control, no stun: 5 pulls about 2 s apart, 5 of 5 fired (`$ALCD` 6 → 1).
- `$STUN,6000,*` sent at t = 230.86 s. Pulls at +3.45 s and +4.73 s gave `$BUT,0,1` with NO `$ALCD`. The pull at
  +6.08 s fired, and so did every later pull. **A native timed stun is CONFIRMED, about 6 s long, and it is
  SILENT**: the gun plays no sound of its own while stunned.
- Sound: Tony picked **`X17`** by ear as the stun cue. Battle Company's UART sheet pairs X17 with the concussion
  grenade ("high pitch ringing and temporary disabling of tagger"). Because the gun plays nothing on its own, the
  node must play `$PLAY,X17,3,10,,,,,*` itself alongside the stun write.

**`$TMP` t8 (incoming-damage modifier).**
- `$TMP` t8 = -50 (`$TMP,,,,,,,,-50,,,,*`): three 9-damage hits took 4 each (932 → 928 → 924 → 920). CONFIRMED.
- `$TMP` t8 = -100: three hits registered as `$HIR`, and HP stayed at 920 across all three. The gun kept firing
  normally throughout. CONFIRMED, and this is spawn protection with no fn-28 table: the hit is seen and answered,
  but nothing is taken.
- `$SPAWN,,*` clears `$TMP`: after `$SPAWN,,*` then `$TID,1,*` (t8 still at -100 from the run above), two hits took
  the full 9 each (999 → 990 → 981). CONFIRMED. So a spawn-protection t8 write must land AFTER `$SPAWN`, never
  before it.
- The `$SIR` table survives `$SPAWN`: the post-spawn hits above registered on the `<0,0>` fn 1 row that was sent
  only once, at arm, with no `$SIR` row re-sent at spawn. CONFIRMED. So the per-life `$SIR` re-send that levers §23
  budgets at 28 frames a life is not needed to keep the table alive; only the live table and the t8 write are.

**`$BUMP` (levers §5, claim 5).** Tactix-E20D re-armed with `$PSET` maxima 45/70/0, `$SPAWN,,*` and `$TID,1`; `$LIFE,*`
read back `$HP,45,70,0`. Every `$BUMP` self-emitted an `$HP` reply, and a follow-up `$LIFE,*` read-back agreed
each time.
- `$BUMP,-20,1,1,1,,*` → 45/50 (armour takes it first).
- `$BUMP,-80,1,1,1,,*` → 15/0 (the overflow past armour cascades into HP).
- `$BUMP,-10,1,0,0,,*` → 5/0.
- `$BUMP,50,1,1,0,,*` → 45/10 (heals HP, and the overflow spills into armour).
- Flag controls at 45/10: `$BUMP,-5,1,0,0,,*` → 40/10 (the HP flag alone, armour untouched);
  `$BUMP,-5,0,1,0,,*` → 40/5 (the armour flag alone); `$BUMP,-5,0,0,0,,*` → no change and **no `$HP` reply at
  all**. **CONFIRMED**: `$BUMP,<amount>,<hp 0/1>,<armour 0/1>,<shield 0/1>,<sound>,*`. The shield flag and the
  sound token are still untested. The no-flags case explains F65: its "inert" probes set no pool flag, so the
  gun rightly did nothing. `$LIFE,*` served as the read probe throughout: it returns `$HP` with no pool change.

**The smoke collision, controlled redo** (Tactix-E20D, `<0,0>` = fn 23, the rig fires one smoke word; accuracy read with
a harmless `$AMMO,0,6,24,1,*` refill, which reports accuracy without changing it).
- Control, no write: accuracy 0 at +0.9 s, +1.9 s and +5.7 s after the smoke hit; 100 at +9.1 s and +12.8 s. fn 23
  holds accuracy at 0 for about 6 s, then restores it in one step (matches the V4_31 6000 ms timer). A trigger
  pull still fires while it is held at 0.
- Test: smoke hit at t0, then `$TMP,,,,-30,,,,,,,,*` at +1.8 s. Accuracy read 70 at +1.8 s and +4.5 s (where the
  control read 0), then **100 at +8.9 s**.
- **Reading, CONFIRMED:** a `$TMP` t4 write cancels an active smoke (the last writer wins), AND the smoke's own
  ~6 s timer later resets t4 to 0 regardless, which erases whatever the recoil writer had set. Rule for the single
  accuracy owner (S55): never write t4 while a smoke is active (about 6 s from a fn 23 `$HIR`); once it ends,
  re-send the owner's current value.

**`$TMP` t9 (magazine).** Tactix-E20D, Shotgun, clip 6, reserve 24.
- `$AMMO,0,6,24,1,*`, then `$TMP,,,,,,,,,50,,,*`: the magazine went 6 → 9 at once (top-up = clip × t9 / 100 = 3).
- The same frame sent again: 9 → 12. **A re-send adds again**, a trap for any writer that repeats itself.
- Then 3 shots (12 → 9), then a full shell reload: it loaded shell after shell from 9 to **33** and emptied the
  reserve (24 → 0). This run is confounded by the two stacked t9 writes above, so its ceiling is not the record.
- **Clean run:** `$SPAWN,,*` (clears `$TMP`), `$TID,1,*`, `$AMMO,0,6,24,1,*`, one write of
  `$TMP,,,,,,,,,3,,,*` (the magazine itself stayed at 6, because 3% of 6 rounds down to 0), 3 shots (6 → 3), then a
  full reload: it **stopped at 9**, which is clip 6 + t9 3.
- **Reading, CONFIRMED:** on a reload the magazine cap is clip + t9, with t9 read as RAW ROUNDS, not a
  percentage. The write itself tops the magazine up by clip × t9 / 100 at once. A re-send adds again rather than
  replacing. So Extended Mags on `$TMP` means t9 = the extra rounds, written once per life after `$SPAWN` and
  followed by an `$AMMO` fill. Whether t9 applies per slot or as one flat count across every slot (V4_31 reads it
  as the latter) is NOT tested here.

**Levers §13 step 3, the kill-confirm protocol-15 sweep. Result: NULL for native callouts.**
- Tactix-E20D row `$SIR,15,0,,,,,,,*` (Battle Company's sheet "call out for audio" row, fn empty). Magnitudes 1-11
  (skipping 6, 8, 10) fired from the IR rig as player 0, team 1 (Tactix-E20D's own team): no sound and no `$HIR`.
- Control: the row changed to `$SIR,15,0,,28,0,0,1,,*`. A team-1 word still gave no `$HIR` (fn 28 does not
  register an ally, with `$GSET` t1 = 0). A team-3 word DID register: `$HIR,0,15,0,3,3,0,0`, with no pool change.
- Sweep with team 3 and the fn 28 row, magnitudes 1-39 (skipping 6, 8, 10): EVERY word registered as
  `$HIR,0,15,0,3,<m>,0,0`, with no pool change and **NO audible callout** on any of them (Tony, by ear, across
  all three blocks). Magnitudes 40-60 were not sent.
- **Reading:** the gun does not announce a protocol-15 word on its own, at least in app mode. Callsign's own
  kill-confirm voice (`V3A`/`VBA`) is an app-side `$PLAY`, per Battle Company's UART sheet, not a native gun
  reaction to the protocol-15 word. Every protocol-15 magnitude DOES register cleanly and silently, though, so
  Open BRX can define its own kill-confirm word on top: the victim's node sends a protocol-15 word carrying the
  killer's id, the killer's gun registers it silently (fn 28 on `<15,0>`), and the killer's node plays the voice
  line. Open question for a later bench: can a DEAD gun still emit `$IRTX` through its headset?

**Death emission and `$IRTX`, both CONFIRMED with controls.** This answers the open question above.
- **A dying gun emits no IR.** Tactix-E20D's headset domes faced the ESP32 receiver (board A, COM7), and it was
  killed over BLE with `$BUMP,-9999,1,1,1,,*` (the gun answered `$LCD,0,0,0,0,6,24`, no `$HP`, the same shape as
  a lethal `$LIFE`). No IR frame arrived for 12 s. An earlier run had killed it with a rig word instead, and the
  only frame the receiver saw was the rig's own, a possible collision, so the BLE kill above is the clean run.
  Tony heard "get some" at the death, a voice line, not an IR emission.
- **A host `$IRTX` does reach the headset, and forwards even from a dead gun.** Tactix-3D4F carried
  `$SIR,15,0,,28,0,0,1,,*` and `$GSET` t1 = 1, with Tactix-E20D's headset domes about 30 cm away. Live
  Tactix-E20D, `$IRTX,100,15,1,1,7,0,0,100,1,,0,*` gave Tactix-3D4F `$HIR,4,15,1,1,7,0,0`. Tactix-E20D was then
  killed over BLE; the SAME `$IRTX` frame still gave Tactix-3D4F `$HIR,4,15,1,1,7`, and a magnitude-9 frame gave
  `$HIR,4,15,1,1,9`. A `$LIFE,*` afterwards confirmed Tactix-E20D was dead (`$HP,0,0,0`) throughout. **A dead
  gun still forwards a host `$IRTX` out through its headset, and the headset emits the exact word.** (A first
  attempt at this step got nothing at all, because Tactix-3D4F, the receiving gun, was itself dead: a dead gun
  ignores an incoming fn 28 row, which is a separate fact from the sending side above.)
- **Reading.** A kill confirm carried over IR is buildable on the node with no firmware help: the victim's node
  sends `$IRTX` with a protocol-15 word carrying the killer's id through the dying or dead gun's headset, the
  killer's gun registers it on a `<15,0>` fn 28 row, and the killer's node plays the confirm voice. The same
  path supports the revive beam (levers §16) and a dead-player beacon.
- **Also noted:** the `$AMMO` set-mode clamps at the base clip and ignores a `$TMP` t9 magazine bonus:
  `$AMMO,0,9,24,1,*` on a gun with t9 already raising the cap to 9 still read back magazine 6, not 9.

**Levers §16, headset loop. CONFIRMED.** `$IRTX,100,15,1,1,5,0,0,100,100,1000,0,*` (field 9 = 100, field 10 =
1000 ms) sent to a live Tactix-E20D: Tactix-3D4F registered `$HIR,…,15,1,1,5` once a second by itself, 8 repeats
(one extra landed on sensor 0), with no further host frame sent. `$IRTX,…,5,0,0,100,0,1000,0,*` (field 9 = 0)
stopped it at once; nothing arrived in the next 5 s.

**Levers §16, revive beam. CONFIRMED.**
- Tactix-3D4F carried `$SIR,14,0,NULL,34,,,,,*` and was killed over BLE with `$BUMP,-9999,1,1,1,,*`. Tactix-E20D
  sent `$IRTX,100,14,1,2,1,0,0,100,1,,0,*` (the team field set to Tactix-3D4F's own team, 2). The DEAD
  Tactix-3D4F registered `$HIR,4,14,1,2,1,0,0`, with `$HP,0,0,0` confirmed both before and after. **fn 34
  registers on a dead gun: CONFIRMED.**
- `$LIFE,30,0,0,1,*` (set mode) on the same dead gun then gave `$HP,30,0,0` twice, and a follow-up `$LIFE,*`
  read back 30. A Shotgun pull from Tactix-E20D then hit the revived Tactix-3D4F normally: 21 then 19, HP
  30 → 9 → 0, dead again. **`$LIFE` set mode revives a dead gun to take hits again: CONFIRMED.** Whether a
  revived gun can FIRE was not observed, since it took a kill again before that was tried.

**`$LIFE` revive details, a second run (Tactix-3D4F).**
- After `$LIFE,30,0,0,1,*` a trigger pull FIRED (`$ALCD` 6 → 5): a revived gun is fully live, not just able to
  take hits: it fires, takes hits and reports normally.
- The headset kept its death flash going after the revive; only `$HLED,,6,*` stopped it (Tony, by eye). Revive
  recipe: `$LIFE,<hp>,0,0,1,*` then `$HLED,,6,*`.
- **`$TMP` survives death plus a `$LIFE` revive, unlike `$SPAWN`, which clears it.** Sequence: `$TMP` t4 = -30
  (accuracy 70), then a BLE kill (`$LCD,0,…`), then `$LIFE,30,0,0,1,*` (`$HP,30`), then an `$AMMO` probe still
  read accuracy 70.
- The magazine is kept across the death: 5 rounds before, still 5 at the `$LCD,0,…` that marked the kill.
- **Caveat:** tested only on a gun killed over BLE (`$BUMP,-9999,…` or a lethal `$LIFE`), never on a gun stuck
  in the F264 stall state (dead on the gun, alive on the HUD). Do not assume this revive recipe cures F264
  without a bench run against that specific state.

**`$TMP` t5 (fire-rate modifier), Tactix-E20D with the Shotgun (`$WEAP` t14 = 800, shell reload).**
- Control: 13 pulls about 0.4 s apart; 6 fired, spaced 800-840 ms, matching the t14 interval.
- `$TMP,,,,,-50,,,,,,,*` (no `$ALCD` reply to the write, unlike t4 or t9), then the same rhythm: 6 fired, spaced
  810-1110 ms. **No measurable shortening**, where V4_31 predicts `t14 × (100 + t5) / 100` = 400 ms.
- **Retest on the bench AR, CONFIRMED.** Tactix-E20D re-armed with the bench AR (`$WEAP,0,,100,0,0,9,,…`, t14
  = 100, full auto) and `$AMMO,0,32,384,1,*`. Control, trigger held: a round every 105 ms (11 rounds in 1.0 s).
  After `$TMP,,,,,100,,,,,,,*`, the same hold gave a round every about 205 ms (195-210 ms). **t5 scales the
  full-auto fire interval by `(100 + t5) / 100`, exactly as V4_31 predicts.** `$TMP` was reset to all-zero
  afterwards.
- **Reading.** t5 works on an automatic weapon but showed no effect on the Shotgun's shell-fed pump cycle
  above (t5 = -50, no change from 800-840 ms). Record both: CONFIRMED for full-auto weapons, no effect seen on
  a shell-reload weapon.

**`$TMP` t7 (outgoing damage) and t4 (real hit rate), Tactix-E20D with the bench AR shooting Tactix-3D4F (999 HP,
`<0,0>` fn 1), about 1-2 m. Last test of the sitting.**
- t7: control, 3 taps, 3 x `$HIR` damage 9. After `$TMP,,,,,,,50,,,,,*`, 3 taps gave 3 x `$HIR` damage 13
  (9 x 1.5, truncated). The word itself already carries 13, so t7 is applied by the SHOOTER before the word
  leaves the gun. **CONFIRMED.**
- t4 and the real hit rate: control at accuracy 100 (`$TMP` unset), one burst of 20 rounds (magazine 32 to 12)
  gave 20 x `$HIR` (HP 933 to 753, 20 x 9, every round connected). After `$TMP,,,,-50,,,,,,,,*` (`$ALCD`
  accuracy 50), a similar burst of 17 rounds (32 to 15) gave 7 x `$HIR` (753 to 690, 7 x 9), **41%**, matching
  the 2026-09-17 `$WEAP`-based reading (7/18 at 50-60). **`$TMP` t4 changes the real hit rate, not only the
  displayed number: CONFIRMED.** So the recoil writer can move fully onto one 20-byte `$TMP` frame, with no
  magazine reset and no `$AMMO` restore needed. `$TMP` was reset to all-zero afterwards.

**Shotgun as shipped, 21/19.** One Tactix-3D4F pull gave two `$HIR`: 21 then 19, 90 ms apart. (Separate from the F276
frame above, which was hand-built at the flat 20/20 to test the duplicate filter directly.)

**Native accuracy walk (F230), after a power cycle of both guns.** Both armed with the bench AR at `$WEAP`
t21 = 100, t22 = 50 (walk allowed), full auto, `$GSET` t5 = 1 (gyro), the same frame on each.
- Tactix-E20D, handheld, 2 s hold: accuracy 90, 90, 80, 80, 80, 70, 70, 70, 60 ... 60 (16 rounds). It walks 10
  points every about 3 rounds.
- Tactix-3D4F, handheld, 2 s hold: 90 after the first round, then 90 for all 17 rounds. No walk after the
  first step.
- Tactix-E20D resting flat and still on the table, 2 s hold: 90 x3, 80 x6, 70 x2 (11 rounds). It still walks,
  perhaps slower than handheld, though this is one run each and only a hint.
- Tactix-3D4F then fired 11 rounds at Tactix-E20D: 11 of 11 hit, accuracy 90.
- **Reading.** The walk is per gun (the same frame produces different behaviour on each) and is not mainly
  driven by motion: a resting Tactix-E20D still walked. Both guns take the same first 100 to 90 step. Next
  step unchanged: `bench-firmware-levers-2026-09-19.md` §19 step 18, the read-only USB SETUP-menu read
  (device type, gyro, recoil device) on Tactix-E20D against Tactix-3D4F.

**Method note.** The ESP32 IR rig works as a single-tagger emitter: every one of the three words it sent per shot
registered on the gun. Both guns were power-cycled at the end.

## 2026-09-18 (desk, one gun + the ESP32 IR rig, ~40 min): firmware levers session 1, third sitting

Tactix-E20D alone, hit by the ESP32 IR rig's emitter (board B, COM8), configured as player 5, team 3, magnitude 9,
protocol 0, one word per emit. Victim arming for the control and for §23: `$CLEAR`, `$START`, `$GSET,0,0,1,0,1,0,0,1,*`,
`$PSET,2,0,999,0,0,50,...`, `$SIR,0,0,,1,0,0,1,,*`, `$TID,2,*`, `$SPAWN,,*`, `$TID,2,*`.

**Control.** One rig hit gave `$HIR,0,0,5,3,9,0,0` and `$HP,990,0,0` (999 to 990, 9 damage): the arming pattern and
the rig both work as expected for the rest of the sitting.

**Levers §23, spawn protection without the fn-28 twin table (claim 24).**
- **Step 2, `$STOP,*`: DIFFERENT from the sheet's prediction.** A hit still gave `$HIR,0,0,5,3,9,0,0`, but no `$HP`
  frame, and a `$LIFE,*` read-back showed HP 990 with no damage taken. So `$STOP` blocks the pool effect of a hit,
  but it does **not** stop the gun registering the hit and sending `$HIR`. The sheet's step 2 expected no `$HIR` at
  all: refuted on that one point.
- **Step 3, first attempt: CONFOUNDED, not the record.** `$SPAWN,,*`, `$TMP,,,,,,,,-100,,,,*`, `$TID,2,*`, then
  three hits: each gave `$HIR` (token 1 read 0) with no `$HP`. Then `$TMP,,,,,,,,0,,,,*` (step 4's write, t8 back
  to 0) and one more hit still did no damage, HP holding at 999. Only `$START,*`, plus a `$GSET` and `$TID`
  re-send, cleared it: the next control hit did the full 9 (999 to 990). **Reading: `$STOP` survives `$SPAWN`.**
  The `$STOP` sent for step 2 was never lifted by the `$SPAWN` in step 3, so the zero damage in this run cannot be
  told apart from ordinary spawn protection — the run is confounded and was redone clean below. **Also noted, not
  explained:** `$HIR` token 1 read 0 while `$STOP` was in effect and 4 once the gun was live again, from the same
  rig word. Record as an observation only.
- **Step 3, clean redo: CONFIRMED.** Starting from a live (non-stopped) gun: `$SPAWN,,*`, `$TMP,,,,,,,,-100,,,,*`,
  `$TID,2,*`. Three hits, each gave `$HIR,4,0,5,3,9,0,0` **and** `$HP,999,0,0`: registered, zero damage. This is
  the record for step 3.
- **Step 4: CONFIRMED.** `$TMP,,,,,,,,0,,,,*`, one hit: `$HP,990` (990 from 999, the full 9).
- **Step 5, order control: CONFIRMED.** `$TMP,,,,,,,,-100,,,,*` sent BEFORE `$SPAWN,,*` and `$TID`: one hit did the
  full 9 (999 to 990). `$SPAWN` zeroes `$TMP`, so a spawn-protection t8 write must land AFTER `$SPAWN`, never
  before it.
- **Reading.** Steps 1 (levers §23, second half), 3 (clean) and 4 confirm the 7-frame design (`$SPAWN,,*`,
  `$TMP,,,,,,,,-100,,,,*`, `$TID`, `$AMMO`, `$BMAP,0,0`, later `$TMP,,,,,,,,0,,,,*`) replacing the 28-frame fn-28
  twin table (F121, F269). Step 5 confirms the write order. Steps 2 and 3's confounded run add one more design
  rule: **anything that sends `$STOP` must send `$START` before the next life**, because `$STOP` outlives a
  `$SPAWN` and keeps blocking damage until explicitly lifted.

**`$BUMP` shield flag and sound token (levers §5, claim 5), closing the two gaps the second half left open.**
Tactix-E20D re-armed with `$PSET` shield maximum 60; `$SPAWN,,*`, `$TID,*`; `$LIFE,0,0,40,*` gave shield 40.
- `$BUMP,-10,0,0,1,,*` (shield flag alone) gave `$HP,999,0,30`: the shield flag gates the shield pool on its own,
  the same way the hp/armour flags already do.
- `$BUMP,-50,1,0,1,,*` (hp and shield flags, no armour) gave `$HP,979,0,0`: 30 came off the shield to empty it,
  and the remaining 20 cascaded into HP (999 to 979). **CONFIRMED: shield drains, then cascades into whichever
  other selected pool is open**, matching the V4_30-read order the §5 header already described.
- `$BUMP,-1,1,0,0,X17,*` sent twice: Tony heard a sound both times (missed the first, asked for a replay, heard
  it clearly on the second). Control `$BUMP,-1,1,0,0,,*`: Tony heard nothing. **CONFIRMED: the sound token plays
  the named id, and an empty token plays nothing.** `$BUMP` is now confirmed in full on every field (closes the
  two open gaps left in F65's row).

**`$TMP` t6 (reload time), levers §21 step 17.** Bench AR (`$WEAP,0,,100,0,0,9,,,,,,,,,100,850,32,384,1400,0,0,
100,100,,0,,,R01,,,,D04,D03,D02,D18,,,,,32,192,75,*`), magazine forced to 3 with `$AMMO`, fired dry, one reload
pull. Timed from `$BUT,2,1` (the lever pull) to the refill `$ALCD` (magazine back at 32).
- Control, t6 unset: 1784 ms.
- `$TMP,,,,,,50,,,,,,*` (t6 = 50): 2756 ms, **x1.55**, close to V4_31's predicted x1.5. **CONFIRMED.**
- t6 back to 0: 1800 ms, matching the control.
- **Unexplained, not a t6 effect:** on the t6 = 50 reload Tony heard a "disabled trigger" style sound. He says
  this happens sometimes on reloads anyway, and the wire shows only the lever press and the release either side,
  so record it as an observation, not a t6 finding.

**Not tonight.** F230 (native accuracy walk) closed earlier today on `main` (commit `e2824811`); not re-recorded
here. `$TMP` t10 (crit chance) is untested, and needs two guns. The node's own `$PLAY,X17,4,6,,,,,*` stun cue is
built in a separate commit, desk work rather than a bench result; see `HANDOFF.md`.

**Method note.** Same rig and arming pattern as the second half. The gun was left armed at the end of the
sitting, ready for levers session 2.

## 2026-09-19 (desk, no gun, overnight): the cure, two merges, a release, and three checks that checked nothing

**What this sitting was.** Not a bench. The engineering that followed the evening bench and the two
firmware-levers sittings: building on what they measured, merging 58 + 13 commits of another
session's work, and publishing the app. Every bench fact cited here was measured elsewhere and is
written up in the entries above.

**F264 has a cure, and it asks rather than guesses.** The log scan (2,385 session databases, 650
lives reconstructed) had already shown the node detects this in 16 s and does nothing. It now sends
one probe on three unanswered trigger pulls and acts only on the reply: health zero reaches the
existing death path, so the death is booked and the ordinary respawn cures the gun; alive with an
empty magazine writes NOTHING, because there the gun is right and the node's count is wrong; alive
and loaded re-asserts the arming from the GUN's own reply tokens, never from our belief. No reply at
all does nothing except say so on the operator's board. A 20 s divergence poll runs alongside.

⚠ **The poll nearly disabled the detector it serves.** Any pool frame resets the unanswered-pull
count, so a player pulling slower than once every ~7 s could never reach three again. A solicited
reply now refreshes the pool clock and leaves the pulls standing, and only `$LCD` may be solicited:
a shot landing inside a poll window would otherwise read as our own reply, and three coincidences
would manufacture the fault the poll exists to find.

**The probe flipped twice, and the second flip came from the gun.** It was built on `$QUERY`, then
the bench measured a dead gun holding that reply's body for ~2 s while answering `$LIFE`
immediately. A heartbeat is precisely what hits a dead gun repeatedly, and `$QUERY` was the frame on
the timer, so the reply window (1500 ms) was SHORTER than the answer from the one gun it existed to
identify. `$LIFE` is every timer now; `$QUERY` is sent once, only after a gun has proved itself
alive, where the hold cannot happen. It could not be dropped: `$HP` carries no magazine, and the
magazine is the only thing separating an empty gun from a stuck one.

**A word that means two opposite things (F279).** `$LIFE` all-zero is the safest question we can ask;
`$LIFE,<hp>,0,0,1,*` revives. One token apart, and the word cannot say which was meant. Unfixable
where it lives (we do not touch firmware, and it is the only command that reads the pools without
changing them), but tractable because an all-zero add moves nothing by ARITHMETIC rather than by
convention. A predicate now says so on both sides of the wire. The first symptom was five shield
tests going red, because a regen grant and a probe are the same word.

**The recoil ladder we shipped this evening is mistuned, found while writing its bench sheet.**
`after_heavy` is 6 rounds on every weapon, so heavy arrives ~0.5 s into a held trigger and owns 84%
of the AR's magazine, 93% of the SMG's, 98% of the Energy Rifle's. Not two stages: one stage with a
half-second transient in front. The cause is that the threshold derives from the ladder's DEPTH,
which is unrelated to how fast a held trigger should stop rewarding you, which is why the Stinger is
the only weapon with a real two-stage feel and got there by accident. Fractions of the magazine make
it worse (the hose becomes immune, the precision weapon fragile); seconds are the right unit but not
the problem. Time-to-kill bounds it. Retune proposed, not taken (F268).

**A spec that printed numbers the wire never carried.** Four rows of `spec/node.md` §3.15 were the
proposal, not the shipped derivation, and both spec and engine claimed three weapons DECLARE a
`heavy` value. No row declares any of the six fields (S54). Corrected to what ships.

**The range retraction reached fourteen files**, including two public manual pages still teaching
that a token controls emitted power. Every measurement kept; only what they prove changed.

**⚠ Three checks that checked nothing, and two were in the tools built to catch the third.** (1) The
suite verdict read off `grep "job(s)"`, which matches the RUNNER'S BANNER, printed before anything
runs: a file that did not parse reached `main` twice on the strength of it. (2) A mutation harness
matching `^✖ (F264: ...)` silently skipped every test without a colon, reporting three caught
mutations as UNCAUGHT, which is the direction that invites repairing a working guard. (3)
`node --check` proving a file parsed after a comment terminator was removed, blind to the fact that
the removal had swallowed a real `export` into the comment. **Mutation testing found all three;
review found none.** Written up in `gotchas.md`.

**Landed:** app **0.3.0** published as `app-v0.3.0` (F220 closed; DEBUG-signed, so the first
release-signed build will not upgrade over it), two merges of `origin/main`, and 19/19 jobs green
including the browser tier. Two defects in the first merge never appeared as conflicts: a call to a
function F209 had deleted, and the stage and engine performing two steps in OPPOSITE orders with
both lanes writing the same justification.

## 2026-09-19: the pre-game office test, and the fixes it drove through 0.4.1-0.4.4

**What this sitting was.** Tony ran a live office test on two phones (Pixel 4, Pixel 5) against fresh
guns (Tactix-E20D, Tactix-3D4F) with freshly paired headsets, working through app 0.4.0 up to 0.4.2
across the session. Full raw findings are dated 2026-09-19 and folded into `FOLLOWUPS.md` and this
entry; nothing here duplicates a row's own text.

**The link-loop incident (P0).** After a phone's first connect to a gun, the gun's BLE link dropped
every 5-12 s, and the gun-headset link dropped with it, looping continuously. The phone kept writing
only the old probe (`$STOP`, `$PHONE`, `$VERSION`) then `$PHONE` on each relink, never breaking out.
**The control that mattered:** a headset power-cycle done with the PHONE's own Bluetooth radio turned
OFF cured both guns outright (P5/Tactix-E20D and P4/Tactix-3D4F). Pairing was intermittent whenever
the phone's Bluetooth stayed on during the cycle. That control points at the phone's own Bluetooth
stack, not the gun or the headset, as the thing disturbing the link, and raises an open question
whether Android's Fast Pair / Nearby scanning is involved. F210's flap backoff was found broken three
ways during triage (the flap window too short, a double drop counted on one failed connect, and the
back-off not actually stopping the retry loop); 0.4.1 fixed those three, which is a mitigation, not a
root cause. Filed as **F293**, still open.

**Scan and connect timings.** The Pixel 5's first connect to Tactix-E20D took 14 s
end to end (12:43:38 tap to 12:43:52 linked), with the drop loop starting 5 s after. The Pixel 4's HUD
showed a stale "gun connected" label in the top-right corner while the centre flashed GUN LINK LOST
for about 10 s during a connect timeout. Bluetooth scan lists were empty on both phones until Tony
tapped "set my gun"; the scan does not appear to start before that screen. 0.4.2 added a "Connecting
to <gun>..." progress screen with a retry count, and 0.4.4 fixed the progress bar overflowing its box
and paused the Mission Control LAN sweep during a connect (the likely cause of a ~4 s freeze after a
tap). The picker's retry count also used to read "Retrying (7 of 5)" once the loop went unbounded;
fixed in the 0.4.4 review pass to clamp instead.

**Ghost nodes, twice, same root cause.** (1) A Pixel 4 running as a respawn station reopened as a
player (app 0.4.0); it rejoined Mission Control under a new node id, and the OLD node id's record
stayed `online: true`, armed and assignable, with arm failing silently against the stale record;
clearing the station on Mission Control did not remove it, and it took a manual `DELETE
/api/stations` + `/api/nodes` to clear. (2) Clearing the Pixel 5's app storage gave it a new node id;
Mission Control kept the OLD node id listed as an online, kitted phone with `gun_linked: true` while
the Armory correctly showed the gun green under the new node. **Both were already fixed same day**,
ahead of this write-up: commit `88ead536` makes `snapshot()` project the net layer's own 8 s
freshness flag onto every node (so the console reads one judgement instead of three disagreeing
thresholds), refuses a new assignment against a stale node, and releases a stale node's claim on a
gun the moment a fresh hello or status claims that same gun. No open FOLLOWUPS row existed for this
(it was root-caused and fixed within the same session), so nothing closes in the ledger for it; it is
recorded here as the evidence.

**F206, run f: PASSED.** The last open gate on F206 (`$TID` resetting to 0 on every `$PSET` write) was
`bench-firmware-levers-2026-09-19.md` §1 run f, a real Team Deathmatch through Mission Control with two
guns. It ran clean: cross-team hits registered on both sides. `bench-plan.md`'s "Pre-game check" and
"Sitting 5" both listed this as their first step; both are struck.

**Spawn protection redesigned, not patched.** The single-mechanism spawn protection F121/F209
described (protection ending at the gun's first shot or a 2.1 s cap, with no separate weapon-arming
delay) is exactly what let a shooter see a protected player flash "hit" with no damage, and let a
respawning player fire while still protected — both observed live today. App 0.4.3 (commit `9fe9dcfc`,
review fixes `9f234a2b`) replaces it with two named respawn profiles, settled with Tony: **timed
respawn** — no protection by default (`respawn.protect_s` 0/1/2 s), the trigger held for a
`weapon_delay_ms` of 500/1000/3000 ms and never live until 0.5 s after protection ends, HUD says
REDEPLOYED / ACTIVATING WEAPON SYSTEMS, and a death within the spawn-kill window raises an escalating
DOWN warning; **station respawn** — 2 s visible protection by default (0/2/3 s), trigger live at once,
a white shield blink on the headset. Go-live is equal for everyone: the live `$SIR` table lands at
T-3 while the head still holds every trigger, and a late start puts the table ahead of `$SPAWN`. A shot
never ends protection on either profile. **F121 and F209 are closed as superseded** by this rebuild;
see `archive/followups-closed.md`. One observation kept, not chased: on 0.4.2, before the 0.4.3
rebuild landed, a hit landed damage 0.73 s after a respawn (09:49:52.4 against a 09:49:51.7 respawn)
even though the same protection window blocked other hits cleanly (green headset flash, no damage).

**Everything else fixed through the day, by build.** **0.4.1**: the flap-backoff quiet period, one
retry loop instead of two, gun-picker fixes (the IR headset no longer listed, scan-hit coalescing, a
15 s scan timeout with SCAN AGAIN). **0.4.2**: the connecting-screen progress state, a scan-settle gap
after a stopped scan, paced picker scans. **0.4.3**: the respawn-profile rebuild above. **0.4.4**
(office-test fixes): the connecting-screen progress bar kept inside its box, the LAN sweep paused
during a gun connect, a 400 ms debounce on the low-health voice cue that cancels outright if a death
follows within the window (so a killing hit no longer lets "health critical" play after death), the
scoreboard overlay moved clear of the game-time readout, extra left padding on the Teams tab's current-
player row, fixed-width K/D/A columns on the game-over screen, an immersive-fullscreen patch for
`MainActivity.java` (the styles.xml patch alone is ignored on API 30+), and the debug menu's redundant
NIGHT button removed. **Review fixes** (same day, eight findings): the low-health debounce now tears
down on match end / panic / BLE drop; SET MY GUN during a background reconnect no longer fails
silently; operator RESYNC no longer overwrites a timed respawn's weapon-delay hold with `$BMAP,0,0`;
`stage.py`'s mirror of the respawn profile only marks a genuine timed respawn, not every legacy revive;
the picker's retry count clamps instead of overflowing; `android-setup.sh` now fails loudly instead of
silently skipping its fullscreen patch when `MainActivity.java` already defines `onCreate`. Perk
gain/cost lines shipped as one server-computed pair on both UIs the same day (unrelated to the office
test, folded in by the same integration).

**Down-pattern misread, corrected, not solved.** Tony read the down animation as hit feedback: three
single flashes (the native death blink), then the down-rearm loop's four double flashes, which do look
like hit flashes. `0d58d92f` slowed the rearm cadence from `$HLOOP,2,750` to `$HLOOP,1,2500`, a cadence
change rather than the LED-language redesign `led-language.md` calls for (slow, steady, dim, no flash
bursts). Whether the slower cadence alone now reads as "down" is unconfirmed; filed as **F296**.

**Left open, filed today:** the link-loop root cause (**F293**, 🔴), the Mission Control LAN sweep
missing `192.168.0.55` on WSL (**F294**), ten pre-existing `webapp/mc/test/screens.test.tsx`
overlay-step failures found on `main` by an unrelated `--ui` run (**F295**), the down-pattern LED
redesign (**F296**), a BLE setup-reliability metrics bench — connect time, first-connect success rate,
no headset drop on connect, all measured rather than anecdotal (**F297**, 🔴, Tony's P0), and a real
Shields-preset match, never yet run (**F298**).

## 2026-09-19 (desk, no gun): the Toxin Rifle ships, and a balance simulator for every weapon

**Decision (Tony):** every Toxin Rifle hit poisons. A 15% proc "feels bad", so `crit_pct` is off the row.

**The first sweep was wrong.** It gave the Assault Rifle 8 damage, typed by hand. The gun deals 9
(`damage_per_pull`). The weak anchor made the Toxin Rifle look 17% too strong. The simulator now reads every
number from `WeaponCatalog`. With the real anchor, the shipped row (8 direct, 4 a second for 5 s) sits at
kill-rate parity: 1.06 in 2v2, 0.95 in 10v10, 51% in a 1v1, and about 20% of its kills land after contact broke.

**`mcp/tools/balance_sim.py`** replaces the toxin-only script. It runs a duel matrix, team play against a
slot-kind anchor for 2 to 10 a side, and field sweeps. A 100%-accuracy duel reproduces the catalogue's
hits and time to kill for every mechanic it models. Whole-catalogue findings: the Charge Rifle dominates
outdoors (about 1.4). The Shotgun sits below parity even with every fight at close range (0.89 to 1.05),
because the catalogue itself gives it a 1.6 s kill against the rifle's 1.2 s. The two inputs that move every
answer are invented: the focus-fire rate and the length of a line-of-sight window.

**Built on the node (S16, S53):** the `FrameBundle.dot` table, the tick clock in `engine.js` and the stage
mirror, the credited `death` fact with `dot: true`, the cues (H23, V4G "Cough!", not yet ear-checked), the
HUD poison countdown and the smoke tell. The polish review found that the tick echo was matched by time only.
Under sustained fire that booked a tick as a hit, or a hit as a tick. The echo now must match the pool and
the amount. It also found that a hot joiner with the Toxin Rifle got no `$SIR` row. MC now refuses that join.

**Unhiding found a validator bug, not a gun bug.** `validate()` reported "NO ROW" for `<11,0>` because it read
only the permanent rows. The compiled table always carried the row. Nothing had checked a hidden weapon.

**`main` was already red** before this work: 10 app-screens steps (RELOADING, SWITCHING, REDEPLOYED,
Quick Switch #43, breaker-1) and the mc-koth stale-server cascade. Checked on `535c8e96`. Filed as F290.

## 2026-09-21 (desk, no gun): F287 evidence-gated operator RESYNC

Operator RESYNC no longer writes a re-arm burst merely because its `$LIFE,0,0,0,*` probe was queued. The phone
and stage now wait for actual transport admission and completion, then accept only that probe's positive `$HP`
inside a 1.5 s answer window. A dead answer takes the ordinary death path; no answer, an expired or pre-send
answer, a write failure, link/life/phase change, stun or reconcile produces no `$TID`/`$AMMO`/`$BMAP`/`$SIR`
burst. The phone defers `operator_result`: MC says DONE only after positive proof and gets a specific refusal on
every terminal failure. The stage mirror also preserves a timed respawn's trigger hold.

Three polish passes found and closed the hard races: timeout starting before a queued BLE write, a pre-send HP
being mistaken for proof, background stage probes stealing ownership, an expired reply landing before the next
tick, duplicate success after synchronous failure, and the stage's write-lock boundary differing from BrxLink.
Focused transport/operator/stage/cure/parity coverage is green; the final full UI-inclusive gate follows this
entry. No visual UI changed, so browser screen-truth verification was not applicable.

## 2026-09-21 (desk, private input, no gun): R4/T2 v4.32 screamer code-read

The stock v4.32 image was verified against the recorded SHA-256 and loaded only into a private Ghidra project. The
8-byte updater header is not part of the payload; reliable code/string references require file offset 8 at address
`0x8008`. The main processor is Kinetis/Teensy-class, not Nordic: the nRF52 strings identify the separate radio
module. No firmware, listing, decompiled source or long string dump entered the repository, and no command was sent.

### `$DPLAY` wait — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

The sound path parses a channel, selects one of five audio objects, starts the requested clip, then polls that
channel's playing flag every 10 ms. There is no timeout, serial-parser call or watchdog escape inside the wait. A
looping clip can therefore keep the flag asserted and strand the main loop with both UART rings unread. This agrees
with the mechanism behind A1 but not with all of its symptoms: A1 was silent, dropped BLE after about 15 s and
recovered on reconnect, so the bench result remains a partial screamer rather than proof of an infinite audible
lock. Bench step A1c runs the same loop through nonblocking `$PLAY`; A3 checks the other channels.

### Receive rings — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

Both radio-capable UART paths use 1,024 ring slots and reserve one slot to distinguish full from empty: **1,023 usable bytes**.
The producer wraps at 1,024 and refuses the arriving byte when its next index equals the consumer
index. The application parser consumes one byte per invocation. A7c alternates 1,022-byte and 1,029-byte no-gap
ping streams, then repeats with phone pacing; it measures the practical drain/overflow boundary, not the allocation.

### Split frame — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

Parser state lives across invocations and has no inter-byte timeout. A comma advances the token index, while a new
`$` clears token 0 and resets the index but leaves tokens 1..59 intact. Ordinary BLE packet boundaries therefore do
not lose a frame; loss of a byte or terminator is the dangerous case. A7b splits one state-changing `$AMMO` frame
mid-token into distinct writes with 60 ms and 2 s gaps, three runs each, and requires magazine 23 every time.

### `$*` reset — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

`$` opens a frame and clears token 0/index. `*` snapshots the 60-token working set, dispatches it, and the common
return path clears all 60 working tokens. Bare `$*` therefore dispatches an unmatched empty command and reaches the
same complete cleanup. This closes the old code-trace gap, not the bench gap: A4 still compares an unterminated
`$AMMO` sequence with and without `$*`, three times each, before any automatic reset is built.

Focused docs hygiene was written red against the missing T2 evidence boundary, then green after these four entries
and A1c/A4/A7b/A7c were present. R4 remains open for T2's untested-levers pass and T3-T5. The next P0 desk task is
F272, using the now-pinned no-answer mechanism to build the phone/MC lock-up detector.

## 2026-09-21 (desk, no gun): F272 lock-up detector

F272's software path is complete. In a LIVE, alive life, 8 seconds without any gun frame starts two serialized,
all-zero `$LIFE,0,0,0,*` reads 3 seconds apart. Only writes the BLE layer confirms as delivered count; any gun
frame cancels the sequence, and silence through the second 1-second reply window latches `gun_locked`. The phone
then owns the screen with the power-cycle instruction, while MC carries a strict true-only optional contract and
shows the warning only from a current linked node. Active head recovery temporarily clears that positive claim so
MC cannot say POWER-CYCLE while the phone says KEEP POWER ON; retry exhaustion restates it. Older nodes,
false/junk fields, stale rows and offline rows make no lock claim.

The expected power-cycle drop preserves the verdict. On relink, the node restores the complete head first; only a
confirmed head write clears the instruction and books one desync death through the configured auto, scanner or
none respawn policy. False and rejected head writes are paced, capped and never start a premature respawn clock.
The F272 read shares F264's solicitation accounting, so its `$HP` answer does not erase independent no-fire
evidence and the existing cure/poll/readback paths cannot collide with the two-read sequence.

Red-green coverage includes the 8/3/1 timeline, frame cancellation, queued/failed writes, persistence, panic and
match boundaries, bounded recovery, every respawn policy, strict MC ingestion and stale/offline suppression. The
polish loop closed recovery-before-write, shared-probe ownership, tight retry, panic/persistence, MC freshness and
alert-accessibility failures. Browser screen-truth checks pass on 891x411 and 667x375 in day and night, and MC
passes at desktop and mobile widths with current, stale and missing-field data. This was a desk run: the 8-second
threshold remains provisional until the idle `$VOLTS` cadence is measured, and A1c/A13 must still produce the
stable-radio screamer this detector is designed to identify.

## 2026-09-21 (desk, private input, no gun): R4/T2 v4.32 untested-levers code-read

The corrected v4.32 project was read without sending a command. This pass found two direct contradictions and six
useful evidence boundaries. An unresolved gun-side read is not evidence that the older claim is false: several
paths format or forward state whose effect may live in the headset or another controller. Every item below stays
provisional until its named bench discriminator runs.

### Melee — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

The gun image contains no `$MELEE` command name. Its `$BHIT` branch accepts one event byte, while `$FIREX` accepts
five fields and forwards them; neither supports the old one-field `$FIREX,<slot>` bypass as written. The complete
gyro/button-map/fire chain and the emitter are not resolved in this image. Bench §2 now compares a real swing with
`$BHIT,8,*` under three button-8 mappings, then measures barrel and headset emitters with weapon source 0 versus 1.

### `$BHIT` — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

Corrected v4.32 contradicts the documented seven-field injected-hit shape. This dispatcher branch converts only
token 1, routes it through state/event handlers, and does not consume the seven alleged hit fields. Bench §3 holds
token 1 constant while changing the tail, then changes only token 1; pool movement or a `$HIR` must follow the
tail before this can still be called a damage injection on v4.32.

### `$SPAWN` shield — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

The v4.32 `$SPAWN` branch reads no command token before it resets state and enters the common spawn routine. The
shield argument in the v4.30 claim is therefore ignored on this path. Earlier empty-token spawns beginning at zero
are consistent with that reading but do not test a non-empty argument. Bench §6 compares complete, independently
re-armed `$SPAWN,0,*` and `$SPAWN,50,*` trials and
reads `$LCD`; any argument-dependent shield disproves this code reading.

### `$PRES` and `$INVU` — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

The names are present, but optimized adjacent strings prevent a safe command-to-handler assignment. The apparent
`$INVU` reference belongs to the following command, and the only nearby `-100` write belongs to an indoor-mode
path. A nearby multi-field formatter is forwarding evidence, not proof of a local per-cell multiplier. The gun
image therefore does not establish either old claim. Bench §7 separately distinguishes registered zero-damage,
filtered hits and a no-op, then tests whether clearing `$TMP` t8 restores damage and whether team state changed.

### Fuse functions 24-27 — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

**2026-09-23 correction:** the five-value formatter was misidentified as `$SIR` from an adjacent literal.
Independent literal decoding shows that it appends an empty string at that point. Its initial runtime string
still needs identification. The native handler has compiled
effects, while the hosted handler reads a RAM table. The recovered hosted switch confirms four timer
durations of 5,000, 4,000, 3,000 and 2,000 ticks for functions 24–27. Their expiry result still needs a capture.
Bench §8 points each fuse at a different plain target cell, timestamps three runs, and separately tests
self-reference so a recursive table loop cannot masquerade as a periodic fuse.

### Splash re-emit — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

The five-value forwarding function contains no p6/p8 re-emit branch, and its role is unresolved.
The recovered hosted hit switch has a separate conditional IR-output call after its effect cases.
The exact row field and on-air result still need a capture. Bench §11 masks the shooter from the receiver,
compares p6 = 0 with 1/50/100, repeats at two distances, and
requires a treatment-only second air word after B's `$HIR`; an invulnerable-B control separates re-emission from
pool damage.

### `$RADSK` — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

The name participates in headset/native-mode message tables, but the corrected references do not prove that an
inbound `$RADSK,*` refreshes a link timer. Bench §15 uses three no-injection headless drop measurements, then a
two-minute 4-second cadence followed by a stop. Survival only during injection plus a repeatable post-stop drop is
the required keepalive result.

### Protocol-15 station words — **CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN**

No inspected v4.32 gun-side path switches on magnitudes 6, 8, 10 or 50. Existing captures prove particular station
words on real hardware, while Jay's host gives them broader names; this gun read does not merge those evidence
classes. Bench §17 uses an exact hosted registrar control for all four values and the already-proven native station
setup for magnitude 6. One same-team pulse already revived 4/4, contradicting the older two-pulse rule. “Perk” for
8 and “proximity” for 10 remain blocked as bench claims because the source does not name an observable gun state;
the generic injected magnitude-50 native-callout test is likewise blocked until its exact stock mode and arming
transition are specified, while the real grenade capture already establishes the word itself.

The red docs-hygiene test requires all eight provisional labels and all eight bench-section discriminators. R4
remains open; the next code-read inside T2 is the complete `$SIR` function table. Three polish passes tightened the
state resets, timing controls and evidence boundaries, then moved the four version-conflicted commands (`$PRES`,
`$INVU`, `$BHIT`, `$FIREX`) behind explicit confirmation instead of leaving unresolved effects on the known-safe
rail. The final review was clean. The repository gate passed all six jobs; stale F272 site shots found by that gate
were regenerated against the current UI tree.
