## 2026-09-22 (desk): F265 bound-phone scoreboard freeze

The freeze was server-side. Mission Control ingested every phone's status heartbeat into the scorer, so its own
row reflected miss-only shots and the lower accuracy, but that path never called the existing score publisher.
Only hit/death event paths did. A continuously bound phone therefore received clock replies and honestly appeared
connected while its last score snapshot could remain minutes old.

A red two-node session test reproduced the field shape exactly: node 0's miss-only heartbeat moved its scorer
total to 25 shots, while neither node received a `score` frame. The status path now invokes `_push_scores()` after
ingestion. Its existing complete-body comparison sends the changed shared `rows` board to every bound player and
sends nothing for an identical following heartbeat. The wire shape is unchanged and older phone apps already
consume it.

UI-build verification exercised the real WebSocket and rendered HUD path with two browsers. After a credited hit,
BRAVO fired four miss-only rounds; ALPHA's open PLAYERS board showed BRAVO's accuracy decrease, still read LIVE,
and kept the row fully inside a 740×340 viewport. The complete browser flow also passed its older-server and
old-session compatibility fixtures. No new route, field, persisted value or control was introduced, so separate
400/500 and old-data migrations do not apply; deployment still requires restarting the MC process so it serves
the corrected status handler.

## 2026-09-22 (desk): S55 one accuracy owner

The recoil writer moved from a compiled `$WEAP` mutation plus `$AMMO` repair to one absolute `$TMP` t4 frame.
Red coverage first proved the old writer emitted no t4; the finished contract proves no recoil path writes a
weapon or ammunition frame, including retry and a full 36-round magazine. The fake tagger now models t4 as an
absolute accuracy modifier that survives `$WEAP`, reports through `$ALCD`, and clears on `$SPAWN`. F274's match
and oscillation patterns now replay the same one-frame writer; its required hardware soaks remain open.

Native fn-23 has priority. Smoke and EMP suppress node t4 writes for the measured six-second recovery window;
repeated Haze extends it even while accuracy is already zero, an accuracy-first frame order gets a 400 ms pairing
hold, and the latest recoil target is reasserted once after native recovery. The shared HUD pill now renders
RECOIL / RELEASE TO STEADY while recoil is degraded, with smoke retaining presentation priority. Flinch and
stance were deliberately not inferred from unspecified mechanics.

Targeted engine/fake/soak tests passed. Screen truth passed on 667×375 and 891×411 in day and night themes with
the real demo engine/writer path: the recoil pill replaced the reticle, fit without overlap or animation, used the
night palette, and cleared after release. ProofShot independently recorded the same transition with zero console
or server errors; artifacts stayed outside the repository. Three polish-loop passes closed native-effect overlap,
reconnect reassertion, flat-weapon cleanup, fake/demo acknowledgement fidelity, timing flakiness, and weak screen
assertions; every Critical/High/Medium finding was fixed. The exact full UI gate passed all 19 jobs, including the
complete 476-case screen matrix.

payload, persisted data, or visual contract changed, so stale-server/data migration and screenshot proof do not
apply. The exact full UI gate follows before commit.

## 2026-09-22 (desk): F173 isolated diagnostic reader

The diagnostic HTTP route previously handed the live store's only SQLite connection to an executor thread. A red
route regression proved the worker received the exact writer object. It now passes only the store path; inside
the worker, `build_report_path` opens and closes a dedicated read-only handle and begins one read transaction so
the report's many SELECTs share a stable snapshot while delayed facts may still arrive.

The polish loop also found that the existing raw `file:<path>?mode=ro` spelling misparsed valid filenames with
URI metacharacters. The shared CLI/HTTP helper now derives an encoded absolute file URI. A real store under a
percent-bearing directory proves that path, while lifecycle coverage pins open/BEGIN/build/close ordering and
closure on analysis failure. The full MCP suite and repository gate passed. UI-build verification does not apply:
the route, response shape and operator controls are unchanged, and no frontend file changed.

## 2026-09-22 (desk): F175 diagnostic binding attribution

The diagnostic grouped status rows by physical node and then chose the last reported `player_id`, so a mid-match
rebind put the first player's pools, first-live frame and counter history beside the second player's pushed head.
A red synthetic store reproduced two named bindings plus an unnamed heartbeat on one node. The report now retains
the physical-node summary, makes its singular player null when ambiguous, preserves explicit ids in arrival order,
and adds status-derived attribution rows for each player and the null/unknown bucket. The CLI prints those rows.

Shots need different treatment because the heartbeat carries a cumulative counter. Arrival-ordered deltas assign
only each change to the heartbeat's binding; a counter decrease starts a new baseline, so resets add the new count
without double-counting earlier play. Invalid samples do not move that baseline, and arbitrarily large persisted
integers remain diagnosable without float coercion. The first polish pass found and closed that huge-integer crash
and clarified that a CLI row's first-live pool is the first frame for its attribution, while the JSON node summary
retains the physical node's first frame. Pass 2 was clean. The MCP suite and full repository gate passed.
UI-build verification does not apply because no frontend consumes this diagnostic endpoint and no UI changed.

## 2026-09-22 (desk): F174 previous-match diagnostic on the runway

The phase guard rejected every diagnostic in ARMED, so arming the next game made the previous game's evidence
unreadable at exactly the point an operator might still be investigating it. A red route test put a third match on
the runway over two finished store rows. ARMED now allows only a token-gated request whose explicit `match` differs
from `start_info.match_id`; the worker remains narrowed to that captured id. Full-session, current-match and
missing-current-id requests still 409, and LIVE rejects even an explicit previous match with recap guidance.

The first polish pass found the LIVE+narrow boundary was implemented but not pinned; that regression and a corrupt
ARMED-state control were added. Pass 2 was clean. The MCP suite and full repository gate passed. UI-build
verification does not apply because the existing diagnostic endpoint has no frontend consumer or control.

## 2026-09-22 (desk): F181 deterministic M2 accuracy settling

The M2 browser walk tried up to six times to catch a numeric provisional ACC row while the mock's one-second timer
kept adding random kills. Under load the same player could cross the ten-shot settling threshold between the test's
choice and its DOM read. The mock now has a query-gated `norandom` fixture that retains clock, status and rendering
ticks but suppresses only background score mutation. The walk drives one kill (six shots), then reads the real
rendered `~50%` cell directly with no retry loop.

The red control fixes `Math.random` at zero while the fixture is active. Without the production gate every tick
adds another kill to the same player and the assertion deterministically sees settled `50%`; with the gate, the one
explicit kill stays provisional. The override spans the DOM snapshot and restores in `finally`. Two polish passes
closed that restoration window and then returned clean. Focused mock, full M2, TypeScript and Vitest gates passed;
the exact full UI gate follows before commit. No UI contract changed, so canonical screenshots do not move.

## 2026-09-22 (desk): F184 utility-to-HUD station cleanup

RELEASE retained a station record until the phone proved that it had reloaded, but the subsequent HUD hello never
consumed that record. ITEMS therefore kept showing the same physical phone as OUT OF WI-FI. The initial red test
assumed utility and HUD reused one node id; polish pass 1 caught that production deliberately keeps separate
`brxu` and `brx` identities, so that implementation and its false-fidelity test were discarded.

BACK TO HUD now persists the old utility id and takeover key. The HUD offers that proof only to a trusted MC;
NetServer accepts it only on a phone hello, acknowledges it in `welcome`, then consumes the old net identity and
hands the validated id—not the key—to Session. Failed welcome delivery leaves the proof retryable, wrong keys and
utility-role claims cannot consume it, and the client erases the one-shot proof only after acknowledgement.
Session removes the old ITEMS and ARMORY rows, shrinks lobby allow-lists after binding the new HUD, and freezes a
departing live station's last self-authoritative report for recap. Accepted and failed RELEASE paths are covered.

Six polish passes closed false same-id test fidelity, accepted-release recap loss, premature/wrong-role proof
consumption, failed-welcome retry, restart persistence, a final-heartbeat race, malformed/partial counter handling,
post-END tally correction, and persisted-proof cleanup coverage; the final pass was clean in all three lenses.
Focused station/network validation passes 81 tests, resume/station validation passes 74, and the app's focused
transport/utility validation passes 25. The A41 built-app flow passes at both phone sizes. A real Chromium MC step
renders two utility cards, applies the confirmed HUD snapshot, captures the one-card ITEMS panel, and reports clean.
Older apps safely degrade to a retained unassigned card until stale pruning, node eviction, or an update plus a
fresh utility→HUD round trip. The exact full UI gate passed all 19 jobs, including the complete screen matrix;
canonical shots are refreshed after the source commit as required for app/MC UI changes.

The exact full UI gate passed all 19 jobs after the clean polish pass.

## 2026-09-22 (desk): F188 Designer PLAY loads before KIT

`PLAY THIS NOW` applied the draft and moved straight to KIT without calling `POST /api/games/load`, so phones were
never told which game was selected and the KIT rail said `NOT LOADED YET`. The red component test observed only
`phase:kit`; it now requires LOAD before the phase change for unnamed and named drafts. Failed steps stop the flow,
and a synchronous pending guard prevents duplicate save/apply/load work or navigation during the transition.

The gun-status copy now says `GUNS NOT CONFIGURED YET`, which is the separate, correct fact before the LOBBY head
push. Real Chromium against a fresh Python MC proved Designer → loaded KIT with no gun write. A stale-server route
refusal stayed in Designer, left MC in BUILD and showed the restart remedy. UI-build verification passed at desktop
width with no page or console errors. Three polish iterations fixed every Critical/High/Medium finding, including
named-draft coverage and a shared synchronous lock for PLAY, SAVE, APPLY and BACK. The exact full UI gate passed all
19 jobs.

`cwd` and `PYTHONPATH` pinned to this worktree's `mcp/`, so only the interpreter is borrowed.

The focused resolver test and typecheck pass. A direct `env -u MC_PY ONLY=real npm run e2e:kit` launched the real
Python MC and passed the complete desk KIT-to-LOBBY walk. This changes only the test harness, so UI-build verification
and canonical screenshot refresh do not apply. The second polish pass was clean after adding the Windows
`Scripts/python.exe` candidate. The full six-job repository gate passed after registering the imported helper as a

## 2026-09-23 (desk): S56 what hit me, built

`$HIR` token 5 is the raw IR-word magnitude, so MC now sends each player's loadout with the magnitudes its words can
carry, read from that player's compiled `$WEAP` frames (Armour Piercing changes them). The phone matches a hit's
magnitude to the shooter's loadout, then to the catalogue for a pickup, and names nothing when two weapons share it.
Damage dealt reaches the shooter only through MC's best-effort relay of the victims' facts, so the down line says
PARTIAL while hits may still be missing. Two findings on the way: a push that compiled and sent one player at a time
could send a roster naming a later player's new weapon beside its old magnitudes (now compile all, then send), and
the phone dropped any relay older than the kill-sound age gate. The polish loop also caught a down line frozen at
PARTIAL and a long line running off the SE screen.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): F309, coverage from the phone's own transport

Each phone now reports `status.transport` on every heartbeat, from the Capacitor Network plugin. It claims
`cellular` only while the network its socket bound on and the current network are both cellular: Android can move the default network
to mobile data and leave the tunnel socket on the field Wi-Fi. MC counts a phone as covered only when it joined
through the tunnel AND reports cellular (`_independent_path`). `coverage.level` is `full` only when every bound
phone is covered, and the A31 off-grid warning uses the same test. Before this, `_off_grid()` read a `backhaul`
key nothing set, so every game whose end MC decides carried the warning. The chip reads "Internet: N of M phones · K on cellular", green
only at full. Unproven on hardware: what the plugin reports with Wi-Fi and mobile data both on. brx2 holds the
four phone checks, and F309 stays open as a bench row for them. Lows open: a rostered player with no phone does not block `full`, and the platform run page
still tells Android players to switch mobile data off.

## 2026-09-23 (desk, no gun): F293 phone-side review, the `btlink` reader, S57 bench steps

The firmware lane stopped early at Tony's direction and moved to another session. The one reading it left for
F293: none of the stock images holds the gun's Bluetooth radio firmware, so no code read can show the radio's own
link timeouts. The rest of this sitting read our own connect path. **Readings, not bench results:** our code has
no 5-12 s disconnect: the engine's silence watchdog forces a drop only after 150 s, and the F272 probe only sends
reads. The Android plugin connects over LE with `autoConnect` off and asks for MTU 512 after every service
discovery, and we never set a connection priority. Our drop callback carries no reason, so the phone's own log
cannot say who ended a link. Before 0.4.1 the picker could list and connect to the IR headset, which fits the
Bluetooth-off cure but is not shown to have happened.

`python -m brx_mcp.btlink` now reads an Android HCI snoop log, or the bugreport zip that holds one, and prints the
link-layer timeline: connects, parameter requests and updates, the MTU exchange, data-length and PHY changes and
every drop with its HCI reason. Each drop gets `phone-host`, `gun-host` or `link-loss`, with the last gun frame
before it, and a peer that is not the gun is flagged. Five tests build synthetic captures; removing the local
Disconnect check fails one of them. brx2's `bench-2026-09-24.md` step 1.3 takes the A/B/A captures (B = Fast Pair
and nearby scanning off), and the decision table went to brx2 with the command line.

For S57, the 2026-09-18 log already answers part of brx4's list: a dead gun forwards a host `$IRTX`, magnitudes
1-39 are silent, and a dead receiver ignores fn 28. Seven observation steps for the rest (self-report, dead
receiver on fn 34, team gating for field 4 = 0-3, duplicates against the 600 ms dedupe, the exact 21-28 frame,
range against a live shot) went to brx2 for the same runbook.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): brx-net, the MC socket stays on the field Wi-Fi

Tony's rule: players never change connection settings. On Android, a field Wi-Fi with no internet loses the
default route to mobile data, so the MC socket to a LAN address left over cellular and never arrived; the docs
told players to turn mobile data off. The new local plugin `app/plugins/brx-net` opens each MC socket natively
(OkHttp). A LAN URL goes on the Wi-Fi network, which the plugin holds with a no-internet network request; the
tunnel goes on the default network. `NetSocket` plugs it into the transport's `wsFactory` and the LAN sweep.
It compiles and a debug APK builds (JDK 21), but it has not run on a phone: F311 holds the bench check. Three
polish rounds added the held request, log-upload backpressure, a 10 s ping and close-code clamps. Every player
instruction in the runbooks is now a host action (turn the tunnel on) or a diagnostic.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): S57 IR callout bus, built

When a player dies, the victim's phone makes its own dead gun send one protocol-15 word, and every gun in range reports
it as a silent `$HIR`. The player field names the killer and the magnitude names the victim's team, so one word
carries both. Nothing is relayed: Tony's rule is one word per event, from the gun where it happened. MC now ships the
silent `<15,0>` row in every mode and a `callout_team` no player holds, so the gun's own-team drop spares everyone.
The polish loop found that kill confirms paired on a bare timestamp, which dropped MC's cue for the second kill of a
double kill when only one IR word landed; they now pair one-to-one. A gun-recovery DOWN no longer broadcasts. What the
design assumes but the bench has not shown (self-report, team gating, duplicate spacing, range) is on the 2026-09-24
sheet.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): recoil by trigger pull, and Tony's balance rules R1-R10

Tony replaced the recoil derivation: rungs count rounds per trigger pull, scaled by calibre (5 clean rounds at 8
damage), and a trigger release resets the count while the weapon is crisp (S54, F268, F280). He then set duel
rules and a stochastic sim checks each one: a charged Charge Rifle beats an Assault Rifle, a bursting Assault Rifle
beats an uncharged one and beats full auto, close-range guns win inside the headset word's range and lose past it,
the Shotgun beats the SMG up close, the bursting Assault Rifle beats the Burst Rifle, and pistols finish kills but
lose to rifles. The first pass found that the 350 ms tap "fix" was the player's own rate, not a gun setting; the
real lever was the tap damage (t37). The polish loop found that the sim ignored crits, which made the Burst Rifle
look 3 points weaker than it is; Tony widened its burst gap to 550 ms and kept the crit. Every number is a sim
result on Standard, not a bench result: F308 lists the gun-side checks, and F310 covers the Shields preset.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): S57 objective callouts

Under Tony's rule (one word per event, from the device where it happened), most objective events needed no new word.
A grenade hill's magnitude-50 capture word is already its one broadcast. A phone control point's BLE advert is its
own. An M5Stick hill now sends the grenade's capture word once on a flip, so phones treat it as a grenade, when the
game's `station_source` is `grenade` (the Stick's own value is H8). CTF has host events but no field producer, so
its codes (29-36) are reserved. One finding from reading the code: MC runs friendly fire off in every mode but FFA,
and the capture word carries the new owner's team, so the capturing team's guns may drop it (F312). Block 7 step 8
settles it; a relay is ruled out, so the fix would be a team-blind `<15,0>` row.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): the full death screen (S56)

Tony asked for "a video game recap of what you did in that life and the current game state". The DOWN overlay now
has three parts. The left column names the killer, the weapon and the final hit, with its damage and where it
landed. Below that, THIS LIFE gives time alive, rounds and kills CONFIRMED, then damage taken per source and dealt per
victim. The right column keeps the countdown or the station hint. THE GAME NOW gives the clock, the team race or
FFA place, the hill owner and the match line. It is rendered by the new pure module `app/src/hud/deathscreen.js`.
The S56 ledger gained `finalHit`, `pickup`, candidate `names`, per-life `shots`, `kills`, `aliveMs` and poison `ticks`.

Three honesty rules came out of the review. Dealt is never a numeral 0, because a relay may be missing. Kills say
CONFIRMED, because MC drops a kill confirm once the victim's report is stale (`scoring.py`). There is no per-life
accuracy, because the gun counts rounds, not trigger pulls, and a charge weapon would read far too low. MC's board
stays on screen off the link, with its age beside each number; the old rule hid it.

Nine demo stages drive real lives through the engine. `screens.mjs` has 36 death-screen steps at both widths, and
three older DOWN steps were moved to the new rules. Each new guard failed once with its fix removed. Three polish
rounds found and fixed a station hint that squeezed the killer block into one word per line, the spawn-kill warning
covering the killer, poison ticks counted as hits and a test that passed on a missing block. The last round was
clean. The Lows and the field check are F313.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): the death screen, simplified with Tony

Tony looked at the first death screen and called it "a financial report". He wanted to see what killed him and a
few callouts. We iterated on a static gallery of real renders: 11 variants, frozen snapshots from the stage, shown
side by side in his Chrome. The result has three parts:

- **The killer line:** a skull icon, the killer chip and the killing weapon, with PICKUP, CRIT, "A / B" or POISON.
- **Up to four callouts:** taken, dealt, kills and time alive, as numbers with icons.
- **The game strip:** the clock, the team race out of the cap, the hill, and the player's kills and deaths.

The per-source tables, THIS LIFE's words, the final-hit damage and its gun or headset icon are gone. The damage
number went because the killing hit is capped by what the player had left; the stage's −71 was an artefact of its
one-frame death. A callout with no value is not drawn, and a number still waiting on Mission Control is greyed with
an out-of-sync icon instead of "1+". The station hint now reads "HEAD TO YOUR TEAM'S RESPAWN STATION / THEN PULL
THE TRIGGER". The hit chip uses "A / B" too.

The real-browser pass found three defects, all fixed:
- **Missing stages:** none of the death-screen stages (or the S57 callout stages) were in the stage sidebar.
- **Poison deaths:** a lethal tick answers `$LCD` and never `$HP`, so it was never booked, and a poison death showed
  the dart. `_death` now books it.
- **App restart:** a restart while down left an unexplained empty column. It now says the numbers were not kept.

The tap on GUN LINK LOST reaches its handler from the death screen. The screen-truth suite has 42 death-screen steps,
including a text-size sweep at the 11px floor. The old `down-recap` stage and F313's Lows remain.

## 2026-09-23 (bench): M5StickS3 first bring-up

Tony ran the Stick's first power-on against `docs/bench-sticks3-2026-09-23.md` (H7). Gate 0 (toolchain) passed:
the Arduino IDE's bundled arduino-cli built the sketch (835 KB of 3.3 MB) with the M5Stack core 3.3.9 and
M5Unified 0.2.21, staged in `C:\Users\Tony\brx-sticks3\m5sticks3` because the Windows arduino-cli cannot build
from a `\\wsl.localhost` path; the FQBN needs `CDCOnBoot=cdc`. Gate 1 (flash, boot, serial) passed: the factory
firmware ignores a software reset, so the first flash needed download mode, holding the Stick's small side
button (also the power button) while plugging in USB; later flashes worked without it. A charge-only USB-C
cable drops the Stick from the port list while its screen stays on.

Gate 2 (IR receive) failed. The rig's own emitter word decoded on the rig's own receiver at 1 ft, but the Stick
decoded nothing at 2 ft. One burst at 6 in with the room lights off carried the word's bit timing correctly but
a short sync fused it to noise, so no decode. Gun shots at 3 and 6 ft never decoded, at any aim tried. A noise
stream of about 4 bursts a second of 144 us pulses on a roughly 1.53 ms grid came and went; a thumb over the
receiver window stopped it. A/B/A tests on the Grove IR emitter and on the rig emitter's aim were inconclusive.
**An earlier Grove "result" is retracted**: it read counts from a separate serial session after the capture
lines had already been discarded, so it proved nothing.

M5's own docs explain part of the gap: the IR circuit runs off the M5PM1 EXT_5V rail, which M5Unified leaves
off by default. Our firmware lacked `M5.Power.setExtOutput(true, m5::ext_none)`; fixed and pushed. After the
fix, gate 4 (IR transmit) half-passed: the rig decoded the Stick's HILL beacon twice, clean and genuine, but
every transmit is followed by `rmt: rmt_receive(401): channel not in enable state`, so the receiver does not
re-arm after a transmit and HILL mode goes deaf after its first beacon (filed as F314). Gate 3 (BLE advert)
passed: a laptop `bleak` scan heard the Stick's printed UUID byte for byte and `brx_mcp.beacon.decode` read it
correctly, which confirms the README's "BLE UUID byte order" unverified item. Gates 5 and 6, and gate 4's
gun-decode half, were not run. Tony stopped the session to let the receiver be fixed at the desk; next time
reruns gates 2, 4 and 5.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): F314, why the StickS3 does not decode a gun shot

With the aim fixed at 1 ft, the rig's receiver decoded the gun (proto 0, player 4, team 1, magnitude 22), and the
Stick's receiver produced one burst per shot of the right length, with no noise around them. So the receiver works,
but its output is distorted: the 2 ms sync arrives split and marks are stretched or shrunk, so the strict decoder
rejects every word. A tolerant re-read was tried on all 627 bursts captured today. It read one gun word correctly, but
it also produced wrong words that passed both parity checks, so it stays out of the firmware. RMT records edges and
cannot stretch a mark, so the likely cause is overdrive at close range (M5 asks for 30 cm), then aim. The rerun is a
distance ladder with a fixed mount; `hardware/m5sticks3/tools/rawscan.py` shows what arrives without decoding it.

## 2026-09-23 (desk, private images, no gun): R4 follow-on code read

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** The v4.32 gun emitter at `0xc984` uses the existing 125 Hz per range-point carrier
slope and selects two fixed output settings from the indoor flag. The v2.08b path at `0xb160` matches. The v4.32
startup path at `0x16c10` loads mode 0, 1 or 2 from stored state. A `$WEAP` t2 ladder therefore remains a carrier
test, not a power ladder. S48 needs a scope measurement of carrier and duty in each physical mode, followed by a
controlled `$GSET` t2 / `$IRLVL` check. Aim and reflection results need shaded receivers and the F232 first-two-pull
control.

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** The ordinary v4.32 loop calls `0x20f5c`. Its six-second timer drives a selected
GPIO low and releases it after more than 500 ms. The image does not establish which inbound frame refreshes that
timer, whether the pin resets both radios, or whether it caused F293. Capture that GPIO with both link states and
Android Bluetooth logs during several drops. The radio module firmware is absent from the images.

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** The v1.34 headset was re-imported at flash base `0x4000`; the earlier base-zero
decompile gave wrong function addresses. Its fatal-hit path at `0x8e60` emits a word with protocol 15, the incoming
player and team, and magnitude 2 in one stored mode. The existing native TDM capture contains such a post-kill
word, but the capture does not prove which device emitted it. A receiver beside the victim headset must identify
the source. A dead headset skips local hit application; gun forwarding to `$HIR` remains open. The recorded
14 ms duplicate across sensors 0 and 4 requires callout deduplication.

An earlier suspected `$SIR` path calls a five-field forwarding function at `0x9470`, which can use a second serial
destination. Independent literal decoding found an empty appended string, so the adjacent `$SIR` literal
does not identify this function. The gun image does not define
the receiving controller's full effect table. The F308 `$BUT`/`$ALCD` order also remains open; record
timestamped raw frames through full auto. The earlier T2 screamer mechanisms stand. The ranked evidence and bench
steps are in `firmware-image-research-plan.md`. No image, disassembly or decompile entered the repo.

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** The protocol-15 magnitude-2 death word also exists in headset v1.27 at `0x7c60`.
V1.34 adds a stored headset identity selector at slot `0x15c`, which the hit path compares with an incoming player
ID. Test equal and different IDs at fixed team and magnitude before assigning a gameplay effect. A branch near
`$CLEARDEVICE` appears to reach a persistent-state reset at `0x19760`; the command mapping remains provisional, so
the deny rule stands. `$FREE` and `$CDFU` have no confirmed callable handler. The first header word in all five
tagger files equals payload length; the second word's high half remains unexplained. No command was sent.

## 2026-09-23 (desk, private images, no gun): R4 broader function pass

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** Private decompiles and function indexes now cover all seven images. The gun's
carrier slope changes by version: 300 Hz per range point in 2.01U, 75 in 2.02c, and 125 in 2.02e, 2.08b and
4.32. All five routines cap at 38 kHz for range 100 or above and use the same two output settings. Command-name
presence did not expose this difference. Calibrate any older gun on its own hardware before sharing a range preset.

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** V4.32 routes native protocol-15 words from decoder `0x233c8` to `0x26338`.
The magnitude-2 path requires active/running state and matching team/player ID, then applies a 161 ms gate,
updates a native string field and toggles GPIO 8/7. Hosted reception uses `0x24aec`. This strengthens the case
that the headset's fatal-hit word has a native role, but the exact effect remains unknown. The ordinary hit
processor at `0x21dcc` also has 55, 125, 160 and 250 ms suppression checks with different field comparisons.

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** Headset v1.34 separates 20-interval and 25-interval IR with parity checks.
The 25-interval path can apply a hit. Protocols 9 and 10 start a further IR output sequence, whose word remains
partly decoded below. The transmitter has a 51-tick repeat gate, probably milliseconds. A mode-5 death branch, absent from
v1.27, immediately applies configured LED colour. Another branch sets a delayed LED state. The duration and
visible difference need a bench check. The remaining code gaps are listed in the R4 plan. No command was sent.

**Headset protocol-9/10 refinement, code-read:** v1.34 hit handler `0x8e60` stores the incoming
protocol, player ID, team and magnitude when the protocol is 9 or 10. It sets an extra bit to 1 and a
subtype field to 0, then calls `0x7920(100,...)`. That routine calls `0x5c1c` to build a 25-bit word and
`0x5b94` to queue IR output. Under the 6-bit ID / 2-bit team layout, the word retains the four incoming
fields and carries the extra bit and subtype. A second layout uses 7-bit ID / 3-bit team fields and omits
the subtype. The enable guard and on-air repeat count still need a capture. The game meaning of this
re-emission remains unknown.

## 2026-09-23 (desk, private image, no gun): native FFA, Death Match and Survival setup

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** In v4.32, the `$AS` start path at `0x1ce7c` stores the base-mode field at
RAM `0x1fffc36c` and the separate rules field at `0x1fffba94`. Native setup at `0x1c95c` groups base modes
0–2, 3–4 and 5–6. A further setup at `0x16a58` calls different default writers for modes 0–2 and 5–6.
The first writes five values `100, 3333, 3333, 200, 15000`; the second writes
`100, 0, 0, 200, 6000`. Their destinations are respectively RAM `0x1fffa88c`, `0x1fffa1c0`,
`0x1fffad68`, `0x1fffa644` and `0x1fff9aa4`. The 3333/0 values feed separate pool-recovery
accumulators in `0x24574`. Pointer tracing links `0x1fffa1c0` to health `0x1fffa6b4` and
`0x1fffad68` to armour `0x1fffc4d8`; the drain helpers identify those pools. The 15000/6000
value gates that recovery path after a timestamp at `0x1afe6`, which fits a 15 s versus 6 s delay.
The ordinary-loop caller checks a 1,000-tick interval before invoking the recovery routine. With other
modifiers at zero, `3333` contributes 33, 33, then 34 pool units across three allowed calls through
a `/100` accumulator.
The shared `200` adds to a separate shield accumulator; a positive result drains about two shield units
per allowed call. The shared `100` bounds that shield path. The exact realised rates depend on timer,
active-state and modifier gates. The zero-counter path requires positive health, a 1,000 ms gap,
counter `0x1fffba95 = 0`, and elapsed time beyond the configured delay. The updater also checks three
state bytes before applying recovery. A nonzero counter takes a separate one-second path. Do not label
these values lives, game time or respawn. Modes 5–6 also enter an extra native-choice
path at `0x14374`; the later team trace below identifies values 0 and 3.
Both writers also copy selected bytes into the health and armour maximum fields before writing those
five constants. Those copies come from `0x1fffad97` and `0x1fffa9bd` and reach `0x1fffa890` and
`0x1fffc72c` respectively. The constants therefore do not define fixed starting health or armour.
All four older tagger images contain writer pairs with the same two five-value sequences: 2.01U at
`0xe814/0xe868`, 2.02c and 2.02e at `0xe3a8/0xe3fc`, and 2.08b at `0xdd6c/0xddc0`.
This establishes version continuity for the raw defaults. It does not prove identical runtime gates
or gameplay timing on older guns.

The community `$AS` mode map and the on-gun mode names agree with 0 = Free For All, 1 = Death Match and
5 = Survival/Infection. The firmware branch structure supports distinct setup families. Complete presets
and all conversion conditions remain open. The later native-hit entry records
the death branch that changes a mode-5/6 player to team 3.
The v1.34 headset's stored mode value 5 changes fatal-hit LED behaviour; the code does not yet connect that
headset value to the gun's base mode 5. A native setup capture, `$QUERY` read-back and fatal-hit capture for
each mode can test the remaining field mapping. No `$AS` start command was sent.

**Same-team hit flag:** `0x14064` sets RAM `0x1fffc151 = 1` for menu mode 0 and clears it for other modes.
The remote `$AS` action-1 path bypasses `0x14064` but calls `0x1c95c`. That routine resets the flag,
then sets it to 1 for mode 0. `0x16c10` copies it to active flag `0x1fffc3f0`, read by native hit handler
`0x21dcc`. Both start paths therefore enable same-team hits in FFA and clear the flag in TDM and Survival.

**Menu team values:** `0x142dc` selects TDM team 0 or 1. `0x14374` selects Survival team 0 or 3; its
mode-6 branch can also select another choice. Both write selected byte `0x1fffc0c9`, which `0x16c10`
copies to active team byte `0x1fffae88`. The mode-5/6 death branch later writes that active byte to 3.
This supports Human = 0 and Infected = 3 in Survival. The manual supplies the displayed team names;
the code read establishes the numeric values and conversion write. FFA's menu branch selects one common
team value, 1, with its same-team hit flag enabled.

| Native base mode | On-gun team selection | Same-team hit flag | Five setup values | Death branch |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0, Free For All | Shared value 1 | Enabled | `100,3333,3333,200,15000` | Ordinary death path. |
| 1, Death Match | 0 or 1 | Cleared | `100,3333,3333,200,15000` | Ordinary death path. |
| 5, Survival | 0 or 3 | Cleared | `100,0,0,200,6000` | One-time branch writes team 3 and rebuilds loadout. |

The same-team flag and five setup values apply to menu and `$AS` action-1 starts. The remote `$AS`
team for TDM and Survival can depend on a prior scratch byte, so those team values need a capture.
The table lists raw firmware values. The code-derived recovery roles above still need a bench timing check.

**Remote `$AS` fields:** the parser stores action, base mode, rules, lighting, minutes, respawn and volume.
Action 1 calls `0x1cb60`, then `0x1c95c`, `0x16c10` and `0x16a58`. The mode 0 branch writes team 1.
The TDM and Survival branches read a team-choice scratch byte that `$AS` does not explicitly fill.
The parser stores lighting at `0x1fffc0c8`, but `0x16c10` can replace it with stored setting 199 when
`0x1fffc8d4` is zero. The meaning and state of that condition on remote start remain open.

**Further code read:** `0x16c10` converts the selected game minutes to seconds, with zero left as zero.
`0x16a58` applies the selected weapon ID. The base mode alone therefore does not fix game time, respawn or
weapon. The on-gun indoor choice is stored separately. The emitter at `0xc984` uses about 38% duty when its
indoor flag is zero and about 20% otherwise, including the night value. No base-mode override of that flag
was established.

The timer path is now narrower: `0x16c10` sets active byte `0x1fffa6a4` only when the chosen time is nonzero.
`0x15590` computes an end timestamp from that time. The ordinary loop calls `0x17ebc`, which counts down while
the current time is before the end and calls round-end routine `0x15078(100)` at expiry on one branch.
Another branch sends event 2 twice. Zero time leaves the timer byte clear, so this path does not end the
round immediately. The exact winner choice and other game-end triggers remain open.

## 2026-09-23 (desk, private images, no gun): native hit rules and kill output

**CODE-READ, NOT BENCH-PROVEN.** V4.32 decoder `0x233c8` sends hosted hits to the RAM-table handler
`0x24aec`, which indexes the table by protocol plus subtype times 16. It sends native hits to `0x21dcc`.
The native handler directly branches on protocols 0–14 and their subtypes. Protocol 15 goes to `0x26338`.
This gun image therefore does not show a nine-field `$SIR` row table loaded for native FFA, Death Match or
Survival. The native effect map is compiled into those handlers. The five-field formatter at `0x9470`
appends an empty string after an initial runtime string. Its complete command name is unresolved.
Both formatters copy that initial C string from RAM `0x1fff9b5c`. The image does not contain the RAM
contents, and the constructor that fills them has not been identified.

On native death, `0x216bc` queues an 11-field serial message with values
`200,15,<killer ID>,<killer team>,2,0,0,100,1,0,0`, after a 50 ms wait, when its broadcast flag is set.
The same eleven argument values occur in death-path calls in every sampled gun image. The formatter
addresses are 2.01U `0x9c04`, 2.02c/2.02e `0x9be4`, 2.08b `0x9c70` and 4.32 `0x9fd0`.
The per-version gates and final serial prefix have not been compared.
The formatter appends `M` after an initial runtime string, then those eleven numbers. The v1.34 headset fatal-hit
path at `0x8e60` can also emit protocol 15, magnitude 2 with the incoming shooter ID and team; another
stored state selects protocol 8. The native TDM receiver capture saw the protocol-15 word after a kill.
That capture did not isolate which headset path produced it or count simultaneous emissions.

**Output route refinement:** gun formatter `0x9fd0` serialises 11 fields to a controller stream, with an
optional second stream. It does not drive an IR pin. The headset's serial parser at `0x7c44` can call IR
transmitter `0x7920`; its autonomous fatal-hit path can call the same transmitter. The transmitter builds
one frame and drives up to four spatial emitter channels. Those channel passes do not prove four protocol
repeats. A 51-tick gate can suppress a near-duplicate request. Thumb literal decoding confirms the appended
`M`, but the initial runtime string remains unidentified. The end-to-end headset route still needs a trace.

**Headset serial-route bound:** v1.34 parser `0x7c44` copies an eleven-field frame's protocol, ID, team,
magnitude, flag and subtype into the same RAM bytes that its IR encoder `0x5c1c` reads. In one parser
branch, it calls transmitter `0x7920` once after a 200 ms gate. Another branch sends immediately and
uses fields 9 and 10 as a repeat count and interval. For count 1, that path requests one immediate and
one scheduled send. The interval caps at 200 ms. The autonomous fatal-hit block separately calls
`0x7920` once with protocol 15 and magnitude 2. The parser dispatcher appears to select `0x7c44` on
an `LED` token, while the gun formatter's full prefix remains unknown. Thus the eleven-field values
fit this headset payload path, but the code read does not prove that the gun's death frame reaches it.

The killer gun's native protocol-15 magnitude-2 branch at `0x26338` requires an active game and matching
own ID and team. It drops repeats within 55 ms, then applies a 161 ms credit gate. An accepted word
increments a byte, updates a display string and toggles two GPIOs. No FFA/TDM-specific test appears in
that branch. No native `$DD` broadcast has been proved. A hosted BLE kill previously produced `$HP` and
`$LCD` without `$DD`.

The `0x216bc` death branch for base mode 5 or 6 tests a one-time flag, writes team byte
`0x1fffae88 = 3`, changes two pool-related values to 150 and rebuilds the loadout. It waits 6 s and then
1 s before returning alive if a pool remains positive. This strongly fits Survival/Swarm conversion;
the exact team labels, pool meanings and IR traffic during conversion still need a native capture.

**Native effect map, bounded code read:** `0x21dcc` receives protocol, shooter ID, shooter team, magnitude,
flag, subtype and source. It applies active-state, team and repeat gates before the protocol branches.
The damage candidate is magnitude plus a stored percentage, with an optional chance to make it zero.
The map below names direct code effects, not confirmed game labels. Several branches also depend on the flag.

| Protocol | Direct native branch behaviour |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Four weapon-damage subtypes, with subtype effects and flag-dependent multipliers. |
| 1–3 | Special teammate pool additions or state changes. An opposing-team branch has limited effects. |
| 4–5 | Store timed status values, then continue to the pool path. |
| 6 | Request a protocol-6 IR output, then possibly continue to the pool path. |
| 7 | Set a timed status and skip direct pool damage. |
| 8 | Four status subtypes; several request a further protocol-8 IR output. |
| 9 | Subtypes 0–3 call a timed routine with 5, 4, 3 or 2 seconds; later subtypes can request protocol 10. |
| 10 | Four subtypes with effects and conditional protocol-10 output, then the pool path. |
| 11 | Four status subtypes, some with protocol-11 output; no direct pool damage. |
| 12 | Can request protocol 0 or 12 output; other branches change status and may enter the pool path. |
| 13–14 | Apply audio or status changes; pool damage depends on the flag. |

The final pool routine drains shield, then armour, then health, matching the earlier bench drain-order result.
It calls `0x216bc` when health reaches zero.
Do not name an effect poison, heal or stun from this table without matching the branch to a controlled
capture. The native handler has no `$SIR` row index; the hosted handler does.

**Hosted switch follow-up:** the companion v4.32 decompile recovers the `0x24aec` switch that the
read-only decompile omits. The RAM lookup byte is a function ID from 0 to 52. A zero entry returns without
an effect. Cases 1–8 take an enemy hit, while 9–15 take a teammate support hit; the open-team flag can
relax either gate. Cases 16–17 have separate enemy and teammate branches. These are firmware gates, not
per-mode `$SIR` presets. The dispatcher also applies 250 ms registration, 55 ms repeat and 160 ms
same-word guards. The helper `0xcc5c` can apply a stored percentage when the incoming IR flag is set.
Its second argument is the decoded word's flag byte, not the `$SIR` row's p5 field. The exact runtime
percentage source and active-slot gate remain to be traced.

The recovered switch supplies these further bounds. Function IDs 18–22 have teammate grant paths and
opposing-team subtraction paths. The existing bench map identifies their pool effects, but the anonymous
helpers do not prove every pool name by themselves. Function 23 changes an audio state to `-100` and
enters the hit-report path. Functions 24–27 allocate up to four timed status slots, with code durations
of 5,000, 4,000, 3,000 and 2,000 ticks respectively. The existing single-shot bench observed repeat
events, but the code read does not assign a gameplay name to the status. Functions 28 and 31–35 mostly
register hits without direct pool helpers. Functions 29, 30 and 33 enter damage helpers. Function 30
doubles its magnitude only for source parameter 1; the normal decoder calls this handler with source 4,
so the documented back-sensor interpretation needs a targeted capture. Functions 36 and 37 apply their
documented t7 formula only for source values below 4. Function 38 passes half the final helper result
to the health routine. Functions 39–49 return without effect. Functions 50–52 call the same colour helper:
50 for an opposing team or open-team mode, 51 for the same team, and 52 unconditionally. Their branches
clear report flags, so they do not emit a hosted hit report on this path. The case bodies are in the
private v4.32 companion decompile, `0x24aec` (cases at `0x24d90` through `0x2544c`).
The direct gun IR decoder calls `0x24aec` with source 4, while the bench observed source-0 headset
scaling under functions 36 and 37. Ghidra finds two further parser calls hidden inside the large
dispatcher. One is in the `$HIT` branch and passes a dynamically parsed source value. The other reads
source from a struct byte. Their exact live headset routing still needs confirmation, but source 4 is
specific to the direct decoder, not a constant for all hosted hits.
The generic seven-field hit record calls `0x24aec` in hosted mode and `0x21dcc` in native mode.
Its fields are protocol, sender ID, team, magnitude, extra byte, subtype and source. The gun-side
mode gate therefore selects compiled rules or the RAM table after parsing the same record shape.
The headset v1.34 decompile has no confirmed `$HIT` sender, so automatic headset reports and their
cadence remain unproved by these images.

**Score trace limit:** the protocol-15 magnitude-2 byte at RAM `0x1fffb2f5` feeds display routine `0x156d8`.
The ordinary loop clears it after about 7.5 s, so it is a short-lived feedback counter, not a proven
match score. A separate byte at `0x1fffadc4` resets at setup. Ghidra xrefs show one increment in a
de-duplicated text/table dispatcher branch at `0x1ff42`, plus spoken-number and serial/event reads.
No direct native-kill path increments it, so it is not a proven kill or winning score.
Timeout routine `0x17ebc` does not inspect a score. It clears active play and calls `0x15078(100)`
on one branch. That value reaches UI and audio
selection, not a visible score comparator. The death handler increments RAM `0x1fffae14` once;
setup resets it, and spoken-number and serial/event paths read it. No read compares that count
with a win threshold or another player. Two parsed command/event branches also call `0x15078`.
Neither its body nor these inspected callers reads a score or selects a winner by score.
The radio module's firmware is absent, so its contribution to scoring remains unknown.

**Protocol-15 event map, bounded code read:** `0x26338` first rejects a repeat with the same player ID and
magnitude inside 55 ms. Selector 1 takes a separate active-player path and can send an event after 1,251 ms.
Selectors 2 and 3 return. The following magnitude branches apply to selector 0 and out-of-range selectors.

| Magnitude | Direct native branch behaviour |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Requires active state and positive life; writes two state bytes and calls a further state routine. |
| 1 | Requires zero life, own team and other flags; calls a state routine or emits event 3. |
| 2 | Requires active state and matching own team and ID; after 161 ms, increments the short-lived feedback byte and updates display and GPIOs. |
| 3 | Requires an active game; conditionally requests a new protocol-15 word with magnitude 5. |
| 6 | Uses timed and mode-dependent station or objective state; one branch counts pulses within 9 s. |
| 8 | Requires own team and positive life; changes state and can emit event 5. |
| 10 | Requires positive life and an opposing team; copies a stored display string. |
| 11 | Requires a matching stored player ID; changes state or emits event 6 or 8 by team. |
| 49–50 | Select a team-dependent display string; 50 can emit event 13. |
| 51–54, 56–58 | Select a fixed sound string and play it. No accepted branch for 55 was found. |

These branches do not establish a complete game-rule name for each magnitude. Keep protocol 15 reserved
until native captures or source clarification map the remaining events.

## 2026-09-23 (desk, no gun): firmware documentation review and next bench sitting

An accuracy and duplication review corrected the hosted kill summary in the levers sheet. The 2026-09-18
hosted death had no `$DD` and its magnitude sweep had no audible callout. The earlier native TDM receiver
capture did see protocol-15 magnitude 2 after a kill. These are different modes, so the hosted negative result
does not refute the native word. The code still leaves its serial-to-headset trigger and recipient behaviour open.

The [sittings 4a–4b bench sheet](../bench-2026-09-24.md#block-2b-the-native-kill-word-and-the-firmware-readings-r4-70-min-plus-20-optional) gives two bounded native/hosted capture
sessions with six trials in three alternating pairs, plus receiver, ordinary-hit and pool controls.
Run them after the screamer transport controls. They test the source
and receiver of the native word. It does not verify every FFA, TDM, Survival, scoring or indoor/outdoor rule.
No command was sent and no firmware file entered the repo in this review.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): the pre-APK integration pass

Three read-only walkers covered today's work: the phone as a player (about 80 stage, width and skin combinations in
headless Chromium), MC as a host (TDM, FFA and KOTH, a stale-server run with REST and WebSocket fields stripped, a
forced 500), and cross-feature consistency (code and unit tests). Findings, all fixed and each pinned by a test that
fails without its fix:

- **The killing weapon was wrong on the kill that matters most (High).** On a killing blow the gun can report the
  victim's remaining pool as the magnitude (the overkill clamp, `$HIR` token 5). That value named a random
  catalogue weapon on the death screen and in the shooter's DEALT relay. When token 5 equals the whole pool, only an
  exact loadout match stands; otherwise the one weapon that shooter used on this player this life; otherwise none.
- **MC's live feed was empty after a reload mid-match (High).** The snapshot's feed now seeds it.
- **The recoil chip lingered over the death screen for half a second (High).**
- **A smoke, EMP or Breacher word stole a poison kill (Medium)** by landing between the lethal tick and its `$LCD`.
  The rule reads the last hit that moved a pool, in the engine and the stage mirror.
- **The briefing's LOADOUT label lost its L (Medium).**
- **MC's MARK ALL READY refusal named the tab the host was on (Medium).** It now names the phase and the next step.

The walkers' clean flows included the hit chip, the S57 callouts, the respawn profiles, panic, KIT values against
the compiled `$WEAP`, F178, F256/F309, F191, history and the narrow MC widths. Two polish rounds; the second was
clean. `test:all -- --ui` passed 19/19. The Lows are F316. Not covered: real BLE relink, real backhaul status, and
F309's live cellular count, which all need hardware.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): StickS3 tooling and manuals (F314)

No hardware. The manuals agree with the bench: M5 powers the StickS3's IR from EXT_5V, asks for 30 cm between sender
and receiver, and uses a 128-symbol RX buffer. Receivers of this class need a gap longer than five times any burst of
over 70 cycles, and BRX's 2 ms sync (76 cycles) followed by 500 us gaps breaks that, which fits the split sync and
stretched marks. The RX re-arm error is an open ESP-IDF issue on the S3 (esp-idf 17811). Built: a `SELFTEST` loopback,
the 128-symbol buffer (bench to confirm), `hardware/m5sticks3/tools/stick.py` (one CLI for compile, flash, serial, RAW
and BLE, with a vendor-id guard), the `m5stick-bench` skill, and a README that is the one guide to the Stick. A CLI was
chosen over brx-mcp tools: the work is toolchain staging and serial capture, and it matches the existing bench scripts.

## 2026-09-23 (desk): the HUD visual-QA pass, QA-01 to QA-24

A read-only reviewer's 28 findings (two Critical, ten High, twelve Medium) were split into five lanes by screen, each
in its own worktree: the first-launch path, the live HUD, one callout component, kit/briefing/results, and the
utility page. The lanes merged with append-only conflicts in `screens.mjs` and two CSS/markup seams, where lane C's
new callout card replaced the old full-screen kill banner that lane B had restyled for night.

The fixes include the two Criticals (night first-launch buttons at 1.1:1 now read at 4.5:1 or better; frozen HP
and ammo dim and say STALE after a gun drop), one callout card for MC kills, S57 IR callouts and hill captured/lost,
night versions of the kill, low-HP and taking-fire moments, 44 px hit areas on the SE, and the utility page fitting
landscape. QA-15 was a stage artefact (the stage had no transport); QA-16 was superseded by Tony's own choice of an
out-of-sync icon on the death screen.

Every new gate failed on untouched code first: the night-contrast check on the first-launch path, the stale-value
assertion on a gun and an MC drop, night recap own-row contrast, the callouts and hill cards on screen, the spawn
shield and stun stages, utility landscape fit, and a merge-review check that nothing on the night gun picker is
green. Three polish rounds found a kill that flashed and buzzed twice, an MC drop dimming the phone's own deaths,
a weak night READY UP, and then a double kill whose second kill gave no buzz. Each is fixed and pinned; the third
round's findings are in the commit. Decisions for Tony and the Lows are on F316.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): SPECTATE fits every player; Tony's MC QA decisions

Tony: a wall that drops the players who do not fit is not responsive. The board now measures its room.
`fitBoard` keeps the normal size, then shrinks row type to the 16/18 px floor, then adds columns as the width
allows, and only then pages every 8 s, with a PAGE n / N · N PLAYERS strip. Players are dealt to pages by a steady
order, so a rank change cannot skip anyone. Checked in a browser on a demo MC, with every player seen, none
clipped and none off-screen:
- 12 players at 1024x600: 2 columns;
- 24 players at 1024x600: 2 pages;
- 40 players at 1024x600: 3 pages;
- 12 and 40 players at 1920x1080: 1 and 2 columns.
Tony's six decisions are in F319. The bench items are already in bench-2026-09-24.md: F309 in 4.8, F311 in 4.8
item 5, and F312 in 7 step 8.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): S57 kills name the victim (A55)

Tony asked why an IR kill confirmation could not name the victim. One protocol-15 word has one player field, and
`DOWN_BY` spends it on the killer. On his choice, the victim's gun now follows `DOWN_BY` with `DOWN` naming itself,
250 ms later (clear of the headset's 199 ms single-shot guard). Receivers pair a `DOWN` with the oldest open
`DOWN_BY` for the same victim team 150 to 600 ms after it, and add the name to that callout with no second cue. The
review found that a lost first word could swap names and that a same-killer double kill inside the 600 ms dedupe
gave the killer an extra ENEMY DOWN; both are fixed and pinned. Every phone in a match needs this build, because an
older phone reads the second word as a second death. Not bench-proven: the two-word timing on real headsets is a
step for the next sitting.
