## 2026-09-24 (desk): chaos testing, the hard-scenario suite for MC, nodes and guns

Tony asked for proof that MC, the phone nodes and the guns hold up in hard matches. Chaos testing
(`mcp/brx_mcp/chaos/`, guide `docs/chaos-testing.md`, skill `.claude/skills/chaos-testing/`) plays whole matches
on the real MC stack (Session, NetServer, Compiler, store) with 2 to 20 MockNodes on the real WebSocket. A seeded
run applies trades, drops, batch flushes, duplicate and reordered facts, clock jumps, late joins, stale heads,
MC restarts and crashes. After every step it checks 15 invariants against an independent ledger of the facts.
CLI: `python -m brx_mcp.chaos list|run|replay|explore|soak`. A failure writes a trace that `replay` runs again.

Bugs found and fixed (all MC restart paths; each has a scripted regression scenario in CI):
- F326: a restart lost a hot joiner's facts (not in `_match_players`, so no binding in the snapshot);
- F327: a second restart lost the facts of a phone offline since the first;
- F328: the frag cap's re-derivation dropped an offline phone's facts, and could move the cap;
- F329: a crash inside the 2 s snapshot debounce of a hot join lost the joiner's facts.
Also fixed: `MockNode` reset an ARMED hot joiner to LOBBY on its `config`; `engine.js` keeps the phase.
Filed, not fixed: F330 (a replay can credit an assist after a backward clock jump), pinned as an expected failure.

Seeds run: the CI set (19 runs, 0.9 s to 3 s each) plus about 600 explored seeds over tdm, ffa, koth, frag race,
clock-hostile, scale-20 and crash-mixed. After the fixes the only red was F330. Mutation checks: breaking the seq
dedup, the team-kill rule, the cap tie, the second END, possession max-merge, F326 and F329 each turned a run red.
The gun-serial test (split, star-less and lost chunks, soak-pattern traffic through the real reassembler) and the
stage test (stun, smoke and poison together; death mid-reload and mid-swap) found nothing. The soak showed flat
MC memory (+1 MB over 17 matches); the first reading grew 4 MB a match, all of it the MockNodes' own inboxes.
`test:all` gains a `chaos` job of about 6 s. Three polish rounds.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): S58 powerups built behind a flag

Tony's design, in one session of decisions: Rockets, Rail Gun and Overshield spawn on the match clock like Halo
(first after one interval, then every interval; one item waiting at most); a player stands within about 1 ft of the
station for 1 s and the STATION awards the first ready claim (player advert bits 4 and 5, station byte 15 `taker`);
weapon pickups swap, the overshield (+75, `$LIFE` mode 2, no regen, no decay) stacks; an operator reset from the Stick
or MC. Built in three lanes (MC server, MC console, phone and phone station) against `docs/spec/powerups.md` and A56,
everything off unless MC runs `--powerups`. A real-MC browser walk (8 MockNode phones, one station) passed every
control. Three polish rounds found and fixed: a frozen player advert in every station game (AdvertGate), a station
re-opening an awarded spawn (`station_update.reset`, a queued `taken` report dated by `age_ms`), a grant lost to a
stun restore, an overshield undoing an unechoed poison tick, an MC restart re-opening taken items, a stale schedule
pushed after a match, and HUD overlaps. Easy Reload players do not claim weapon items, since the grant rewrites ALT
(open for Tony, S58). Nothing here ran on a gun: the gate is bench Sitting A 3.3 and the 4.11 RSSI calibration.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): the StickS3 station screens land, plus the A58 lock

The approved station-screen mockups (`mockups/render.py`) reached firmware: `station_screen.h` (a pure,
host-tested state-to-`ScreenSpec` model, with `HomeNav` for the 20 s idle return and the 1 s A-hold home
gesture) and `station_render.h` (M5GFX into an off-screen `M5Canvas`, pushed once a frame) (`7d3aab31`). Two
polish rounds followed. Round 1 (`25ed6096`) found that M5Unified's button hold threshold defaults to 500 ms,
so the RESET screen's "2 s knock-safety hold" was firing at a quarter of its designed time; `setHoldThresh` now
sets A to the model's 1 s (home) and B to 2 s (RESET). It also wakes the backlight on an MC frame (welcome,
arm, station_update, release), moves the screen sprite to PSRAM with a DRAM fallback, and adds the STATS
page's BATTERY row. Round 2 (`764dffd3`) added the remaining wake triggers: a self-spawn, a local claim award
and any link state change, matching the MC frames. Not yet wired, by design: hill capturing/contested, the
respawn revive count, an empty pickup and the settings flow (documented as open in the README).

Then the A58 match lock landed on `main` (`7f6765b9`): `station_config.lock_s` (0-7200 s, RAM-only, so every
boot starts unlocked; `release_utility` also unlocks) locks the operator controls, showing LOCKED with the
time left on a B-hold RESET and a padlock in the status strip; A still pages and goes home. A+B held 7 s force
restarts the Stick regardless of lock state, with a countdown after 2 s. `status` gains `uptime_s`,
`boot_count` (NVS-backed), `assoc` and the lock time left, so MC can raise a RESTARTED flag.

The side button's own lock is a documented TODO, not a regression: the StickS3's side button is wired to the
M5PM1 power-management IC, not the ESP32, so a single click resets the Stick and a double click powers it off
no matter what the firmware says. The installed M5Unified 0.2.21 source confirms the PM1 sits at I2C 0x6E
(`M5PM1_Class::DEFAULT_ADDRESS`) and that `M5.Power.M5pm1` exposes public `bitOn`/`bitOff` register writes,
but its register table (`M5PM1_Class.cpp`) stops at 0x45; registers 0x49 bit0 (disable the single-click reset)
and 0x4A bit0 (disable the double-click power-off) are not named anywhere in M5Unified or M5GFX. `mc_link_glue.h`'s
`pmicSetSideButtonLock()` logs the intended state and writes nothing, so a locked Stick can still be restarted
or powered off from the side button; the restart drops the lock and MC sees a moved `boot_count`. Do not touch
0x49 bit7 (the download-mode lock) once the registers are confirmed. Nothing here ran on a gun; no PM1 write
was ever sent.

## 2026-09-24 (bench): Block 0 preflight, Block 1 connect reliability, Block 1.4 the F293 fix re-check, Block 3 trigger order, fire intervals, pickup slots, the heavy-pickup auto-equip, and melee (F297, F293, F308, S58, K4)

Gun: Tactix-FE30 and its headset, firmware v4.32. Phones: a Pixel 5 on Android 14 running 0.4.6 release, and a
second Pixel 5 on Android 11 with the factory Android System WebView 83.0.4103.106.

**Block 0, preflight.** The main checkout was pulled and `~/bench-0924.env` written. `webview_eval.py` could not
bind port 9222: measured, a Windows portproxy rule holds it. Fixed with a `--port` flag (`43d7071e`, on `main`);
the runbook now passes `--port 9229`.

0.4.6 release rendered blank on the Android-11 phone: measured. `app.js` used `||=`/`??=` (Chrome 85 syntax) and
the build set no esbuild syntax target. A fix on a side branch, not `main` (`e01b26c3`), targets chrome60
(Capacitor's floor), adds polyfills for `globalThis`, `Object.fromEntries` and `Promise.finally`, and a startup
guard below the floor. Measured against the fix build `0.4.7+9ab9fe0b` on the same phone: the JavaScript now runs
(the HUD and the scanner both appear in the page text), but the layout is unusable, shifted left and off the
visible screen. So the syntax fix lets the app boot; it does not fix the CSS. Filed as **F334**: the WebView-83
floor is still open and the fix is not merged.

The Android-14 phone's 0.4.4 debug build was uninstalled (its app data wiped) and 0.4.6 release installed.

**Block 1.1, laptop, 10 cold connects (F297).** The first attempt, run from Tony's own terminal, is CONFOUNDED:
the tool never sent `$PHONE,*`, so `$VERSION` got no reply and the headset read `no_reply` on runs 1-3, then it
crashed at run 4 with no error captured. Fixed by `9b94e318` (`connect-metrics` sends `$STOP,*` then `$PHONE,*`
before `$VERSION`, already on `main`). The clean re-run, with Tony waiting for the headset to settle before each
run, measured: first-attempt success 10/10, link time median 1.37 s, p90 2.41 s, max 3.76 s, headset drops 0/10,
errors 0. Log: `C:\Users\Tony\.brx-mcp\captures\connect-metrics-D9502F98FE30-1790254224.jsonl`.

**Block 1.2, the Android-14 phone on 0.4.6 (F297/F293).** Times from the phone log (1 s resolution) plus Tony's
stopwatch.
- Try 1, tapped after the headset settled: measured stopwatch 2.5 s; log connect 13:11:22Z, linked 13:11:23Z; no drop.
- Tries 2-4, the app left open with auto-reconnect while the gun and headset were power-cycled: measured, each
  time the app reconnected 5-10 s after the power-off, then the gun dropped the link twice, about 6-10 s after
  each relink. After 2 drops the app logged "keeps dropping ... Is the headset on? Backing off 5000 ms", then
  reconnected and held. The HUD's "headset off" / "reconnect now" warnings cleared only at "gun link held: flap
  count cleared", 30 s after the hold; measured stopwatch from headset settled to warnings off: 35 s and 36 s.
  Reading: after a relink the app sends only `$PHONE,*` (one frame) and never `$VERSION`, so it never learns the
  headset's own state.
- Try 5, the app closed, the headset settled, then the app opened: measured linked about 2 s after open
  (13:17:28Z to 13:17:30Z); no drop in 30 s.

**A/B/A, headset join time (gun power-on to the headset-connected sound), same phone.**
- A1, app open: a LOOP. Measured reconnects at 13:21:12, 13:21:20 and 13:21:34, each followed by a drop, then
  "Quiet period: no reconnect for 30000 ms", then GATT_CONN_TIMEOUT connect failures. The headset never joined
  in over 60 s.
- B1, app force-stopped: measured 16 s; the headset needed two power-cycles to recover from the blocked state.
- B2, app closed: measured 15 s.
- A2, app open: measured phone connected at 6 s, headset at 15 s, phone connected again at 21 s, warnings
  cleared at 52 s.

Reading, not a verdict: an early phone connect while the headset is still joining can block the headset from
joining (seen in 1 of 2 A runs); A2 alone does not show it happens every time. This reproduces F293's link loop
on demand, with no btsnoop capture, so runbook step 1.3 (the bugreport-based A/B/A) was not run.

Proposed fix, folded into F293: after each relink send `$VERSION`; while its token 2 reads `?`, do not count a
drop as a flap and do not back off 5 s; clear the headset warnings on the first `hds.N` reading, not on a 30 s
timer; consider not reconnecting until the gun's advert has been seen for a few seconds after a power-cycle.
Re-measure the A/B/A once the fix lands. Cosmetic, folded into the same fix: `rejoinGun` logs "match in
progress — reconnecting" with no match in progress (`app/src/app.js`).

**Block 3.1, release order (F308).** Same laptop MCP session, gun armed from `armgen.py 1 5 ar volume=65`.
Control: measured, one tap gave `$BUT,0,1`, `$ALCD`, `$BUT,0,0`, in that order. 7 short bursts (4-6 rounds,
about 0.5 s each) and 2 long holds (19 and 22 rounds): measured 9 presses and 9 releases, and every `$BUT,0,0`
came after its burst's last `$ALCD` (0-60 ms after). PASS: no release is lost or out of order. Reading: the AR's
gaps inside a hold alternate 90/120 ms (mean about 105 against `fire_ms` 100), most likely BLE connection-event
quantisation.

**Block 3.2, fire intervals (F308; Balance rules table rows 6, 11, 12).** Frames from `compile.resolve`, sent
into slot 0; Tony pulled by hand, as fast as he could, about every 150 ms.
- Shotgun (`fire_ms` 700): measured 5 gaps, all 720 ms. PASS.
- Desert Eagle (`fire_ms` 700): measured gaps 649, 721, 720, 719, 721, 750 ms; median 720. PASS on the median;
  the strict shortest-gap rule misses by 1 ms. Reading: the 649 ms gap is the first report delivered late, and
  the 750 ms gap is a late pull, neither is the cadence.
- Burst Rifle (`fire_ms` 75, `t23` 550): one 3-round burst per pull; holding the trigger 4 s fired one burst
  only, Tony: "no full auto, it only shot once". Inside a burst: measured gaps 74-90 ms, PASS. Between bursts
  (last round to the next burst's first): measured 615, 721, 630, 646, 644, 644, 645, 660, 690, 660 ms; shortest
  615, median about 645. The hand pull's own cadence bounds this, so the true `t23` gap sits between 550 and
  615, a reading of 550 plus the same roughly 20 ms overhead seen on the Shotgun and Desert Eagle. INCONCLUSIVE
  against the strict `t23` = 550 rule: a hand pull cannot separate the gun's own gap from the roughly 150 ms
  human cadence. `bench-2026-09-24.md` 3.2's pass rule is corrected: judge the Shotgun and Desert Eagle on the
  median gap, not the shortest (the host timestamps carry BLE delivery jitter), and re-measure the Burst
  Rifle's between-burst gap with a fixed-rhythm rig pull, not a hand pull.
- Cross-weapon reading: the gun adds about 20 ms to a configured fire interval (700 reads as 720 on two
  weapons).

**Block 1.4, re-check the F293 fix (Tactix-FE30, Pixel 5 Android 14, 0.4.6, build `main` + `c63876ac`).** The
fix is `HEADSET_JOIN_MODE 'disconnect'` (brx5, `ffbc987c`). Build note, stated plainly: `c63876ac` is a local
patch, not on `main`, and it only changes the MC plugin's event threading; it does not touch BLE, so this run
reads on the merged F293 fix.
- A1 (app open): measured drop 16:23:33Z; "headset probe: no `$VERSION` reply in 2000 ms, counted as not
  joined" at 16:23:43; "link released, next try in 15000 ms (not a flap)"; reconnected 16:23:59, 26 s after the
  drop. Tony: the headset joined before the phone reconnected; the warnings cleared quickly. No loop.
- A2: measured drop 16:24:36Z, probe 16:24:46 (not joined, released), reconnected 16:25:03, 27 s after the
  drop. The HUD read "headset joining", then the headset connected, then the phone, and the warnings dropped.
  No loop.
- B1, B2 (app force-stopped, the control): measured, the headset joined at 15 s and 15 s (stopwatch).
- A3 (app open, stopwatch): measured, the headset joined at 12 s, the phone linked at 18 s, and the warnings
  cleared at once. No loop.
- Headset left OFF: measured 5 short probe connects about 15-17 s apart, each "`$VERSION` reads `?`", released,
  not a flap; at 16:30:00 the log read "headset not joined after 60 s: no more automatic tries", and the HUD
  read "HEADSET NOT JOINED · POWER-CYCLE THE HEADSET". Powering the headset on and tapping RECONNECT NOW linked
  it and cleared the warnings.
- Step 5 (headset power-cycle mid-match) did NOT run: it needs MC, so it moves to Block 4.
- Verdict: PASS against 1.4's rule (no loop in 3 A runs; A's 12 s headset join is within 3 s of the B median of
  15 s; the warnings clear at relink). Reading, not a verdict on the mid-match case: the phone's settle wait
  costs about 8 s of relink time (26-27 s against about 17 s); `HEADSET_SETTLE_MS` could be tuned down later.
  F293 stays open until step 5 runs.

**Block 3.3, pickup slots and the overshield (S58; Tactix-FE30, laptop MCP session).** Arm: `armgen.py 1 5 ar
volume=65` plus `$WEAP` shotgun in slot 2 and deagle in slot 3 (from `compile.resolve`), each with an
`$AMMO,<slot>,<mag>,<reserve>,1,*` sent before `$SPAWN`. PSET `45,70,70,50` (shield max 70).
1. Slots 2 and 3 armed before `$SPAWN`: measured, `$ALCD` reported both, each fired on its own magazine and its
   own `$AMMO` reserve (slot 2 6/24, slot 3 7/48). PASS.
2. Slots 4 and 5: measured, `$WEAP,4,…` and `$WEAP,5,…` take (`$ALCD` slot 4 7/24, slot 5 6/12), and fire from
   buttons bound `$BMAP,4,4` and `$BMAP,5,5`, each counting its own magazine. So the gun has SIX usable weapon
   slots (0-5). Reading: slot 4 is also the default gyro melee slot (`$BMAP,8,4`), so loading a weapon there
   replaces melee. PASS.
3. Measured: `$BMAP,1,100,0,1,2,99,*` cycles ALT 0→1→2→0. A mid-life `$BMAP,1,100,0,1,99,99,*` cycles 0↔1 only,
   and slot 2 never comes up. PASS. Readings: a new ALT map restarts the cycle at the list start; ALT keeps its
   own cycle position; an EMPTY slot still counts in the cycle (ALT went 1 → 2, empty, clicks → 0, needing two
   ALT presses to get past it).
4. Measured, button ids: left 4, select 3, right 5 (select is 3 in our parser, not 5 as in Jay's).
   `$BMAP,<btn>,<slot>,,,,,*` (the function is the slot) makes the button FIRE that slot on each press: with the
   trigger on the Deagle (slot 1), select bound to slot 2 fired the Shotgun and the next trigger pull fired the
   Deagle again; the same for slots 3, 4 and 5 (the trigger stayed on the AR). `$BMAP,<btn>,100,<slot>,99,99,99,*`
   on a small button does NOT select that slot (it landed on 0, 3 and 1 in three tries). With the trigger blocked
   (`$BMAP,0,98`), select still fired its slot every press, so there is no switch-without-fire button this way.
   OPEN, unexplained reading: once, after the right button (slot 2) fired with the current weapon empty, the
   reload handle reloaded slot 2, either a switch-when-empty or reload targeting the last slot fired; needs a
   disassembly read.
5. Measured: an empty item slot (`$AMMO,2,0,0,1,*`) cannot fire, select bound to it gives no `$ALCD`. PASS.
   Reading: after that write the trigger still fired the current weapon (slot 1), so an `$AMMO` write alone does
   not change the current weapon.
6. Measured, across a death (`$BUMP,-9999,1,1,1,,*`, then `$SPAWN,,*` about 8 s later): every slot refills, item
   slots included (slot 2 came back 6/24 from 6/15). Reading: the phone must re-empty item slots after each spawn.
7. Overshield, measured: `$LIFE,45,70,75,2,*` echoes 75 but `$HP` reads back 70 within 0.75 s and stays there; 60
   holds at 60; 75 clamps to 70 again (A/B/A). So the shield clamps to the `$PSET` max. A mid-life `$PSET`
   re-send raising the shield max from 70 to 145 (nothing else changed) then lets `$LIFE,45,70,145,2,*` hold at
   145 for over 6 min; the gun still fires and ALT still cycles. A death clears it (145 → 0; the respawn gives
   45/70/0). NOT tested: hits draining the shield first, and a mode-2 grant on an already-dead gun (no rig).
   `docs/spec/powerups.md`'s Bench gate item 7 and its "Overshield mechanics" section both asserted the raw
   write sticks; both are corrected to the measured clamp-and-`$PSET`-re-send behaviour.
8. Confounded and unexplained: a `$BMAP,0,0` write once seemed to reset the current weapon to slot 0, but
   another `$BMAP` write did not reset it; left as an unexplained reading. Reading: the session started on slot
   3 after its `$WEAP` was the last one sent, which a `$WEAP` write may select, though slots 4 and 5 did not
   support that (the trigger stayed on the AR after arming them).

**Block 3.3 continued, the heavy-pickup auto-equip (S58; Tactix-FE30, laptop MCP session).** Tony's decision:
a heavy pickup (Rocket Launcher, Rail Gun) must equip straight onto the trigger. Arm: the `armgen.py` default
with the stock AR and pistol, no heavies.
1. Control: measured, the trigger fired the AR (slot 0).
2. Measured: a mid-life `$WEAP,2,…` rocket_launcher, then `$AMMO,2,2,2,1,*`; the next trigger pull fired the
   rocket (slot 2, 2→1). So a heavy pickup auto-equips.
3. Measured: a mid-life `$WEAP,3,…` rail_gun ONLY, with no `$AMMO`; the trigger fired the rail (slot 3). So the
   `$WEAP` write alone equips.
4. Reading: the rail is a charge weapon, one shot 30 ms after release, another 1.23 s into a held pull, about a
   1.2 s charge.
5. Measured: `$AMMO,0,29,32768,1,*` with the empty rail current fired nothing on the trigger. `$AMMO` does not
   switch weapons.
6. Measured: ALT from the empty rail went to slot 1 (the pistol), not slot 0. Reading: ALT continues from its
   own cycle position.
7. Measured, the switch-back lever: re-send `$WEAP,0,…` (the AR; `$ALCD` showed a refill to 32), then
   `$AMMO,0,29,32768,1,*` (`$ALCD` 29); the trigger then fired the AR 29→28. So the phone restores the primary
   by re-sending its `$WEAP` and then its saved `$AMMO`.

Not tested: whether a mid-life `$WEAP` re-send resets other per-weapon state (heat, swap delay).

**Melee, K4 (Tactix-FE30, team 1, player 5, `armgen.py 1 5 ar volume=65`; Tactix-9498, team 2, player 6,
`armgen.py 2 6 ar volume=65`; both with the shipped melee row `$WEAP,4,1,90,13,1,90,…,M92,…` and the three
`$SIR,13,*` rows).**
1. Measured: `$MELEE,255,*` and `$XYZZY,255,*` both gave NO reply, so the old `$MELEE` → `$BUT,4,0` note did not
   reproduce. Reading: `$MELEE` is not a command; the old reply was a coincidence.
2. Measured, swing detection on A: with melee in slot 7 and `$BMAP,8,7`, each of 4 swings gave `$BUT,8,1` /
   `$BUT,8,0` plus `$ALCD,1,100,7,0,0` (the magazine stays at 1), and the gun played the melee sound. With melee
   in slot 4 and `$BMAP,8,4` (the shipped frames): 3 swings, the same pattern for slot 4, and the sound played.
3. A hit on B: Tony swung A at B's headset twice. Measured: B logged `$HIR,4,13,5,1,90,0,1,*` then `$HP,25,0,0`
   (90 damage, armour 70 → 0 and HP 45 → 25), then a second `$HIR,4,13,5,1,90,0,1,*` and `$HP,0,0,0` (died).
   Each was matched to a swing on A (`$BUT,8`, `$ALCD` slot 4). **So melee WORKS in our compiled game on v4.32
   with the shipped frames. K4 CLOSED.** Why it failed on 2026-08-26 is unknown, a reading only (the swing
   technique or the target's arm, then).
4. Reading: melee at 90 damage kills a full Standard 115 pool in two swings.

**Emitter A/B, addendum (B respawned at 45/70/0).** Measured: with the barrel covered by Tony's hand, one swing,
B logged `$HIR,4,13,5,1,90,0,1,*` and `$HP,25,0,0`, the hit landed. With A's headset domes covered and the gun
aimed at B, two swings, B logged NO `$HIR` and no HP change. So the melee shot leaves from the SHOOTER'S
HEADSET, not the barrel (small n: 1 hit barrel-covered, 0 of 2 headset-covered), matching the `$WEAP` t1 = 1
"headset only" code reading. Caveat: A was not BLE-connected during these swings, so they are from Tony's
report, not from `$BUT,8` on the wire.

`docs/spec/*` carries no melee claim to correct; `docs/manual/dev.md`'s "headset emission" note and
`docs/bench-firmware-levers-2026-09-19.md` §2 are both corrected to the measured facts above, and
`docs/archive/followups-closed.md` carries the dated K4 closure.

**Assassinations, addendum (Tactix-9498, PSET 45/70/0, melee 90 damage, the shooter's headset emitter, K4's
arm).** Tony believes a back melee one-shots "like Halo" in native mode.
- A back-of-headset hit: measured, `$HIR,4,13,5,1,90,0,1` and `$HP,25,0,0`, not a kill.
- A front hit: measured, `$HIR,4,13,5,1,90,0,1` and HP 25, identical. The melee `$HIR` reports sensor 4 for
  front and back alike (measured, n=1 each), so the wire gives no back/front signal for melee.
- B re-armed with `$GSET` t7 (`criticalShotModifier`) = 50 instead of 0, nothing else changed; a back hit gave
  measured `$HIR,4,13,5,1,90,0,1` (crit token 0) and HP 25, not a kill.

So there is no one-hit back kill on our compiled arm, with crit 0 or 50 (measured). Whether native mode has one
is not tested, it is Tony's recollection. If Open BRX wants assassinations, the phone cannot detect "from
behind" from the `$HIR` sensor field for melee. Filed as **F336**, an open design question, not folded into
K4's closure.

**F336 addendum, the sensor field by dome (Tactix-9498 as the target, Tactix-FE30 shooting, Tony covering
sensor domes by hand).**
- Melee with ONLY the back dome open: measured, 2 hits, both `$HIR,4,13,5,1,90,0,1`.
- Melee with ONLY the front dome open: measured, 1 hit, `$HIR,4,13,…`.
- So the melee `$HIR` token 1 (sensor) read 4 in all 7 melee hits today, whichever dome was open. Sensor 4 is
  the GUN BODY (`protocol/brx-protocol.md` §4.1's sensor map: 0 front dome, 1 back dome, 2/3 the other two
  domes, 4 gun body, isolated 2026-08-26), which lay right beside the target's headset. Reading: at melee
  range, point-blank IR floods every receiver and the first to decode reports (protocol.md's own warning), so
  the gun-body sensor wins whichever dome is actually uncovered. This is a proximity artefact, not a fixed
  melee value.
- Control, the AR (fn 0, 24 damage), front dome only: measured, 2 hits, both `$HIR,0,0,5,1,24,…` (sensor 0).
  Back dome only: measured, 2 hits, `$HIR,0,…` then `$HIR,1,…` (sensors 0 and 1). This fits the map: front only
  gave sensor 0 twice, back only gave sensor 0 then sensor 1 (the back, with the first shot probably leaking to
  the front sensor).
- So for gunfire the sensor field does vary (0, 1 today; 2 and 4 in earlier logs), but hand-covering still did
  not give a clean dome-to-sensor map. A dome map needs a proper cover, or the IR rig at each dome.

Conclusion for F336, sharpened: at melee range the target's gun-body sensor wins, so the melee hit carries no
front/back signal. F336 stays open, a design question, not closed.

**F336 final addendum, aim versus dome (Tactix-9498 target, its gun moved out of line of sight, all four
headset domes uncovered, Tony hand-aiming at the target's headset).**
- Back aim: measured, an AR shot gave `$HIR,1,0,…,24` (sensor 1 = back, correct), then a melee swing gave
  `$HIR,0,13,…,90` (sensor 0 = front, WRONG dome).
- Front aim: measured, an AR shot gave `$HIR,0,0,…,24` (sensor 0 = front, correct), then a melee swing gave
  `$HIR,0,13,…,90` (sensor 0 = front, correct); a second swing did not register.

With the gun out of the way, melee now registers on a HEADSET dome rather than the gun body, confirming the
earlier reading that sensor 4 was the nearby gun body winning by proximity. The AR follows the dome correctly
in both rounds (n=2); the single back-aimed melee reported the front dome instead (n=1), an unexplained miss,
not yet a pattern. Back-melee repeats are running to test whether that miss is real or noise. So a front/back
("assassination") signal from `$HIR` at melee range is not yet established either way with hand-held aiming
geometry. F336 stays open; its next step is a fixed-geometry test (the headset on a head or stand, a fixed
distance, the IR rig).

**F336, back-melee repeats (target's gun still out of sight, no shooting).**
- Back melee, all domes open: measured, `$HIR,0,13,…,90` (sensor 0).
- Back melee with the FRONT dome covered by hand, back and sides open: measured, `$HIR,0,13,…,90` (sensor 0)
  again.

Tally: a melee landing on the headset reported sensor 0 in 4 of 4 (back ×3, one with the front covered; front
×1). The AR in the same setup tracked the dome (back → 1, front → 0). Reading: the headset labels a melee hit
as sensor 0 whichever dome receives it, and the gun body labels it 4 when the gun is near, so a melee `$HIR`
carries NO direction, and an assassination cannot come from the hit report. Caveat: hand covering can leak
(small n, hand-held). F336 stays open only as a design question: a backstab would need a non-IR signal, not a
fixed-geometry re-test of the `$HIR` field.

## 2026-09-24 (bench, evening): Block 4 setup, the F293 flap, F340, the AR ladder, S57's 7.11, the audio FIFO root cause, and a P0 frame corruption

Kit: 0.4.11+f366156e; a green Pixel 5 on Android 14 with Tactix-FE30; a grey Pixel 5 on Android 11 with
Tactix-9498; a black Pixel 5 as a phone respawn station on 0.4.10; a StickS3 respawn station; MC from `main`
`7026744b` with `--powerups`.

**1. A headset flap under load (F293).** Measured: Tactix-FE30's headset lost the gun 4-12 s after each of
three relinks (20:24:01, :11, :23Z), then the app went to its quiet period; the headset blinked green. brx5's
read of 0.4.11: each relink's probe reads `hds.N`, and a joined reading is silent in 0.4.11, so this is NOT the
F293 relink-before-join shape that is already fixed. Context: the Stick and the phone station were scanning,
and the grey Pixel ran SCAN AGAIN (its 15 s scan ended 20:23:25Z, before the first drop) while Tactix-FE30 was
connecting.

**2. Recovery and control.** Measured: both player apps force-stopped, the Stick off, the station app closed;
a double headset power-cycle, then the gun joined; the app then linked and held.

**3. A/B adding the scanners back (measured, n=1 each).** The phone station scanning, 60+ s: no drop on either
phone. Plus the Stick scanning, 60 s: no drop, no green blink. With all scanners on, a power-cycle of
Tactix-FE30 joined normally (Tony: "solid, like the earlier test"). So a steady link and a join both survive
the scanners; the trigger of the loop is still unknown. Reading: a second phone's SCAN AGAIN during a join.
Follow-up bench step: `bench-2026-09-24.md` Block 1 step 1.5 (power-cycle with and without a second phone's
SCAN AGAIN mid-join, A/B/A).

**4. Location OFF means an empty gun scan (F340, confirmed).** Measured, on the grey Pixel (Android 11):
`location_mode` = 0 with the location permission granted; the picker found no guns (twice, and after a
Bluetooth toggle) while the gun advertised at -71 dBm. Turning Location on made SCAN AGAIN find it at once. The
app must detect location off on Android ≤11; sent to brx1 to route.

**Also noted and sent to brx1:** Tony's decision to auto-join MC when found; 0.4.11 logs "tap JOIN (it is no
longer joined automatically)".

**5. 1.4's step 5 PASSES (headset power-cycle mid-match, F293).** Measured: a headset power-cycle mid-match;
the green phone logged a drop at 21:07:31 and "reconnected" at 21:07:33 (2 s), a reconcile, no loop. Tony:
"worked great". Step 1.4 is now fully done; F293 stays open only for the new load-triggered flap shape above.

**6. 4.3 PASSES against the spec, but too harsh (F308).** Measured: degraded -30 after round 6, heavy -60
after round 7, 0 about 690 ms after the last `$ALCD`; `$ALCD` accuracy walked 100→70→40→100. Tony: too harsh
at point blank. DECISION: the AR ladder becomes 100/70/55, sent to brx1's balance lane.

**7. 7.11 PASSES, and finds a real-play collision (S57).** Measured: `$IRTX,100,15,2,0,22,…` ×10 at about 2 m,
all received as 22. The victim-name word 26 registered 5/5 alone, but is lost in play: it is sent 186 ms after
the 22, inside the headset's 199 ms rate guard. brx4 is fixing it in 0.4.12 (300 ms, measured from the actual
send). Tonight's match saw one 22→28 sequence, which stays unexplained. Reading: a collision in play.

**8. Station thresholds, a second walk (F345).** Tony walked both stations. Measured, at 3 m: a phone station
reads -63 to -68 dBm, a StickS3 -53 to -58 (the same range F345 already records). Decision stated tonight:
phone station -70, StickS3 -57, which does not match the -66/-60 already fixed on `station-revives`. Flagged
for brx1 to reconcile.

**9. The Stick fixes, by brx4 (F344, F333).** Fixed: a periodic fn-15 IR hill beacon on an MC-assigned Stick,
removed; an advert republish missing the game-byte change (game 32 vs 33), fixed. With both landed, the S2
revive test PASSES: a gun respawned at station 1 (20:51:00Z) and MC counted it this time. Still open: the
Stick's own screen does not update its revive count (MC shows 1, the screen does not follow). Tony wants a
green "redeploy" flash on a revive, not just a number.

**10. The P0 frame corruption (F341, already filed by brx1).** After a status-201 write error, "a chunk of
frame 1 of 8 was lost, sending again from that frame" left Tactix-FE30 at `$HP,4545,7070` (unkillable);
evidence captured to a local JSON, held outside the repo. brx1 has an agent fixing it.

**11. Audio root cause, measured A/B/A, laptop MCP, no phone (F347).** While shield > 0 the gun plays a
continuous native shield hum that blocks the audio FIFO indefinitely: `VAA` played 9 s late or never; shield
0, `VAA` played at once. `$PLAYX,0` stops the hum and releases the queue, but the hum resumes by itself. Also
measured: clips are FIFO (`VA6Y` then `VAA`, `VAA` waits); `$PLAYX,0` stops only the current clip (one
`$PLAYX` on a 4-deep queue lets the rest play, four `$PLAYX` writes fragment playback then `VAA`); a regen
`$LIFE,0,0,10` grant is silent; `$HLOOP` is LED-only. This explains tonight's late or missing kill/shield
cues. DECISION (Tony): remove the "Shields Online" callout. Fix: brx4's 0.4.12 adds phone-side audio-FIFO
modelling and sends a must-hear cue's `$PLAYX,0` before it; a simulator and scenario suite for this is being
built by brx2.

**12. Other findings, routed to brx1, not filed here:** Location off on Android ≤11 means a silent empty gun
scan (grey Pixel: `location_mode` 0, F340); Tony's decision to auto-join MC; a Shields spawn starts at shield
0 every spawn, including the first; kill confirms and lead callouts overlap; the kill confirm leaves too fast;
the shield-hit sound varies (sometimes geiger, a pool?); a chunky shield HUD fill; the phone station counts no
revives while still on 0.4.10.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): S59 the Halo shield meter

Tony asked for a Halo-style shield HUD. Three variants went to a static gallery; he picked C, the Visor, and then
refined it in four notes: no words or timers (DOWN means waiting for respawn), no shield number, no second health
display, a bar between 11 and 21 px (16), and no armour number or bar in a game without armour. The overshield is a
lime layer by day that only hits drain (no fade), a brighter double rail at night. The gun LED stays one teal pool.
Three polish rounds: an always-running sweep, reduced motion missing pseudo-elements, a fresh life shown as broken,
armour granted mid-match never showing, and an invisible night creep. Desk only; the recharge timing is read from
the engine (6.5 s, then +10 per 300 ms).

## 2026-09-24 (bench): the Stick alone, firmware 96fb1868 (button hold, force restart, side button, mode screens)

Stick only, no gun, no BLE use, USB on COM10, firmware `96fb1868` plus a temporary serial log of button DOWN/HOLD/UP
edges with the hold threshold. Flash and boot passed: `STATUS` showed `locked=0 lock_s=0`, a boot count and an
uptime. `SELFTEST` failed again: it sent 25 bits (`1111000000100000100000010`) and decoded 23
(`00100000010000100000010`), the first mark at 2069 us then distorted, the same close-range distortion as F314.

A single click opened DIAGNOSTICS (standalone); 20 s idle returned home. **CONFIRMED.**

The `setHoldThresh` fix from round 1 (`25ed6096`) is proven: serial logged B's DOWN at 702.432 s and HOLD at
704.432 s, exactly 2.000 s against `thresh=2000`; A's threshold read 1000. **CONFIRMED.** An earlier run that looked
like B fired at 1 s is now explained: it was confounded, Tony's "1 s" presses ran about 3.5 s, and a 30 s no-touch
control logged nothing, so that reading proved nothing either way.

A+B joint holds released at 2.5 s and 4.5 s produced no restart: the single-button holds fired inside the library,
but neither RESET nor MODE resulted, so the joint-hold suppression works. **CONFIRMED.** A+B held 10 s showed a
countdown ("counted down") and the log line `FORCE RESTART (A + B held 7 s)`; the Stick was ready again 1.25 s
later and the boot count rose. **CONFIRMED.** New finding: the buttons still held at boot produced a "BTN A hold"
log line on the fresh boot; harmless, and already being fixed.

The side button (the small power button, green LED), on USB power: a single click restarts the Stick (screen off
then on), a double click powers it off even on USB power, and a single click after that powers it back on; boot
count rose from 4 to 9 across these presses. So F332's lock must block both gestures, not just one. F332 stays
open (the PM1 register write is still unconfirmed); this session's observed behaviour is folded into its row.

Gap found: in standalone bench mode, HILL and BRIDGE show the same home screen. Serial logged `MODE HILL`, but the
screen did not change to match. Filed as F333's mode-kind case, since F333 already tracks unwired station screens.

Tony's decision from this session: persist the last `station_config` in NVS so a restart comes back as the same
station; the operator lock (`lock_s`) stays RAM-only by design (a restart is meant to clear a lock). Being built
now.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): F293 fix, the headset probe on every connect

From brx2's A/B/A reading (an early phone connect while the headset is joining can stop the join, then the gun
drops the phone every 6-10 s). Every connect now runs a short probe inside `brxlink.js` before the app or engine
hears the link is up: `$PHONE`, `$VERSION` (plus `$STOP` on the first connect only; a relink never sends `$STOP`,
which would disarm IR mid-match). `hds.N` links as before. `?` or no reply releases the link and waits 15 s
(`HEADSET_SETTLE_MS`), which is not a flap. After 60 s the HUD says HEADSET NOT JOINED · POWER-CYCLE THE HEADSET and
automatic tries stop, except in armed or live, where they continue every 15 s. The headset warnings clear on the
first `hds.N`. `HEADSET_JOIN_MODE = 'hold'` keeps the link and polls instead, for a bench comparison. Two polish
rounds found a capped first pick that never started the MC link, stale probes across attempts, a 15 s mid-match gap
on one missed reply, and a RELINK button stuck disabled. Desk only: bench step 1.4 re-measures the A/B/A.

## 2026-09-24 (bench, afternoon): M5StickS3 IR range, the USB-host TX bug, and the backlight noise fix

Stick on COM10, rig receiver (VS1838B) on COM7, rig emitter on COM8 (the older `ir_emit.ino`, which misreads
`--gap`). No gun was connected over BLE; Tony fired a gun by hand.

**Transmit range: CONFIRMED.** The Stick's HILL beacon (proto 15, team 2, magnitude 8, every 5 s) decoded on the
rig receiver at 5 cm, 30 cm, 1 m and 3 m. At 3 m on battery, with no USB host attached, 3 of 4 beacons decoded
clean and 1 was two bits short.

**Bug found and fixed: beacons need a live USB host (`fc8c3db4`).** Standalone HILL beacons went out only while a
PC held the Stick's USB serial port open. An A/B/A/B test proved it: port closed, no beacons; port open, beacons;
closed again, none; open again, beacons resumed. Cause: the ESP32-S3's HWCDC blocks `loop()` on a TX timeout when
no host is attached to the USB CDC port. Fix: `Serial.setTxTimeoutMs(0)`. Retest with the port closed: beacons
continued every 5 s. Every field station was affected, since none carries a permanently attached host. Filed and
closed the same day as **F335**.

**Bench safety lesson.** A shot word sent by the Stick (`TXN`, 5 copies of a real gun's word, player 2, team 1,
magnitude 22) hit Tony's gun and did damage. Do not transmit a shot word on the bench near a gun; use the silent
proto-15 hill word instead. Folded into `bench-sticks3-2026-09-23.md`.

**Receive: still fails everywhere tried.** The Stick never decoded a gun shot or an emitter word, in five separate
setups: a gun at 1 m (the rig beside it decoded every shot; one run was confounded because the rig itself heard no
shots that time); a gun at 30 cm aimed at the window; a gun at 30 cm after a fresh boot straight into BRIDGE with
no transmit at all (rules out TX breaking RX: 0 frames seen); the rig emitter at exactly 38 kHz at 30 cm (one
75 us blip only); and the rig emitter at 5 cm (nothing), while the rig's own receiver in that same 5 cm spot
decoded 3 of 3. The Stick's own receiver does decode its own transmitter at millimetre range (the SELFTEST
loopback, a 2015 us sync), and the receive channel re-arms correctly (the STATUS RX line moves). The morning
build (`96fb1868`) also failed SELFTEST, so today's session broke nothing new. The IR window was clear, no film.
**Conclusion: this unit's (or this design's) receiver hardware is the limit, not our firmware and not the gun's
carrier.** F314 stays open with this evidence. Next: try the second Stick with the emitter at 5 cm, and order an
external 38 kHz receiver (a Seeed Grove IR receiver or an M5 Unit IR); Tony's existing Seeed Grove IR unit is
transmit-only.

**Noise source found and fixed: the backlight PWM (`2a836bce`).** The intermittent "noise" on the Stick's
receiver, F314's ranked cause 3, is the screen backlight's PWM at the idle dim level, not ambient IR. An A/B/A
test: backlight 25 and 0 gave about 20 noise bursts per 15 s; backlight 60, 120 and 255 gave none. The pulse gaps
are multiples of about 1.52 ms (about 660 Hz), matching the PWM period. Fixed by raising the idle dim level
(`BACKLIGHT_DIM 60`) and adding a `BL <n>` serial command for bench control.

**Two retractions.** A direct pin read (a `PINSCAN` command, since removed) had reported the receiver pin
floating with no transitions; M5's own documentation says this receiver can only be read through RMT, so that
reading was invalid and is withdrawn. A claimed decoder bug (noise fusing to the front of a word) is also
withdrawn: `fold_glitches` already handles it, and a new host test now pins the behaviour.

Captures: `C:\Users\Tony\.brx-mcp\ir-captures\20260924-*.log` (Windows).

### 2026-09-24 · brx3 · A58 MC half and the 0.4.7-0.4.9 cuts

The desk lane published three release-signed APKs, 0.4.7, 0.4.8 and 0.4.9. Each went out after the full UI gate
and a green GitHub CI run on the pushed release commit. A58, the station tamper lock, has its MC half built with
brx4, whose Stick half is `7f6765b9`:
- MC sends `lock_s` at LOAD, re-sends it at START, and sends 0 when a match stops.
- MC flags a station that rebooted inside the lock window, dated by the station's own `uptime_s`, so a muster
  Stick's reboot shows when it rejoins after the match.

Two polish rounds found six real gaps; all are fixed, with tests:
- a new session, a KIT re-push and KIT's auto-advance back to LOBBY did not lock correctly;
- an abort followed by a new START lost a muster station's restart window;
- the unlock confirm never expired;
- the unlock button showed for a lock that had already expired.

The Lows are F337.

## 2026-09-24 (bench, late afternoon, after `383885ee`): the onboard receiver cannot hear BRX IR; an external VS1838B on Grove G9 can

Stick on COM10, rig receiver (VS1838B) on COM7, rig emitter on COM8.

**Optical control: the onboard receiver works, just not on BRX-style IR.** A Sony TV remote at 1 m decoded on
the Stick's onboard receiver (G42): 5 frames, each with the expected 2.29-2.46 ms header. Same spot, same
30 cm distance, A/B against BRX sources: the remote gave 2 headers from 3 presses; the 38 kHz rig emitter gave
0 syncs from 3 words. So the onboard receiver's optics and RMT path are sound; it does not pick up BRX's IR
specifically, gun or emitter. Web research backs this: M5's own receive example matches our setup (RMT on G42,
1 us ticks, 128-symbol buffer, 20 ms idle, speaker off, EXT_5V on), an M5 forum post from April 2026 reports
"the sending works, but I can't receive the signal" on the same hardware, and M5's own docs warn reception is
abnormal under 30 cm.

**External receiver: a shared VS1838B first went dead, then worked once fixed.** The rig's VS1838B (3.3 V from
the rig board) was wired to the Stick's Grove G9 (yellow) with a shared ground, so both boards read the same
line. First try: the line went dead whenever the Stick's pin was live. A/B/A with the remote: the rig heard 0
bursts with the jumper connecting the Stick in, 8 bursts with it disconnected, 0 again with it reconnected.
Cause: the Stick's G9 pin state was dragging the VS1838B's weakly-pulled-up output low. Fix: enable an internal
pull-up on the Grove receive pin. With the pull-up, both boards decoded the remote cleanly (rig 12 bursts, Stick
13 bursts with textbook 2392/608/593/1193 us timings), and the Stick then decoded 3 of 3 real gun shots at 1 m
as complete 25-bit words (player 4, team 1, magazine 22, parity OK) -- cleaner than the rig, which stitched the
same shots from fragments.

**Firmware (`0f50605e`).** `RXPIN 42|9|10` picks the receive pin, persisted, refuses to double as the TX pin;
the internal pull-up is enabled whenever the pin is not the onboard receiver; the `STATUS` RX line now names the
active pin. Earlier in the session: `BL <n>` (screen brightness, bench-only) and the `BACKLIGHT_DIM 60` fix
already landed (`2a836bce`, prior entry).

**Conclusion: F314's root cause is the onboard receiver hardware, and a workaround is proven.** An external
VS1838B on a Grove pin decodes BRX gun shots that the onboard G42 receiver never did, at the same 1 m distance.
Not yet built: a standalone wiring that does not depend on borrowing the rig's receiver or its power. Proposed
next (not built): power a VS1838B's VCC from G10 driven HIGH (3.3 V; the part draws about 1 mA and runs on
2.7-5.5 V), OUT to G9 with the pull-up, GND to the Grove black wire, leaving the Grove red 5 V wire unused so
nothing on a signal pin ever sees more than 3.3 V. Longer term, an M5 Unit IR is an option. **Warning:** never
power a VS1838B from the Grove port's 5 V rail with its OUT pin on a Stick GPIO; that puts 5 V on a 3.3 V pin.

Next: build the standalone G10-powered wiring, then a HILL capture test with real shots on a standalone Stick
(no shared rig receiver) before closing F314.

## 2026-09-24 (desk, evening, no gun): Stick MVP scope, and `presence.h` ports the phone station core

Tony's decisions for the StickS3 MVP: stations are Bluetooth-only (hill, pickup, respawn); Stick IR
receive (F314's workaround) is post-MVP; grenade hill support on a Stick is post-MVP. KOTH's stock hill
defaults `station_source` to `phone`, which brx3 already built (`9ac01a5d`) alongside `a4d2a1ee`'s SETUP
warning naming OBJECTIVE SOURCE when a CONTROL station is assigned under a grenade or IR-station objective.

**`e65aea17` ports the phone's station core to the Stick.** `presence.h` is a header-only C++17 port of
three pieces of phone code, each named against its JS original so a Stick and a phone at the same spot
give the same answer: `PlayerPresence` (`app/src/beacon.js` `Presence`: smoothed RSSI, dwell, hysteresis,
expiry), `BleControlPoint` (`app/src/control.js` `ControlPoint`: the kind-5 hill), and `ReviveCounter`
(`app/src/utility.js` `tick()`: an alive bit going 0 to 1 counts as a revive at that station). The numbers
follow the phone station where the two JS files disagree (0.8 s dwell and -74 dBm from `utility.js`
DEFAULTS, not `beacon.js`'s 2 s / -62). 6,000 random ticks of JS and C++ agreed on every advert byte
(`test/test_presence.cpp`). One passive scan (50/100 duty, gated on the persisted assignment) feeds
presence for `control` and `respawn` plus the pickup ClaimGate, which is unchanged. An MC-armed control
station uses the BLE point, not the IR one, and still sends the S57 IR capture word on a capture.

Two bugs the port surfaced and fixed: respawn now advertises state 1 (at state 0, every phone read a
Stick respawn as disabled, a live bug since the station was first built); and the hill owner and hold
time now persist in NVS, written only on an owner change and tagged by MC session, game and id, so a
Stick restart cannot wipe an enemy's hold (RESET and release still clear it). Presence and the advertised
byte 14 default to -74 dBm, matching the phone station's value.

**mDNS was never working.** `MDNS.queryService` blocked `loop()` for its whole 3 s query on every retry,
overflowing the sighting ring; the fix moves to the ESP-IDF mdns component's async query, polled each
loop. In doing so, the session found `MDNS.begin` (`mdns_init()`) had never been called anywhere in the
Stick firmware, so a Stick's `_openbrx._tcp` browse for MC could not have found anything on real hardware
before this commit (H8's auto-discovery gap, noted there).

Screens: `station_screen.h`/`station_render.h` gained hill capturing/contested and respawn idle/owned,
narrowing F333's five-screen gap.

**Found in review, not fixed here:** MC armed a station keyed by its `game_no` byte, while a player phone
scoped presence by a `config_id` hash, so every MC-armed station was invisible to every phone. brx5 owns
the fix, landing in the 0.4.11 hotfix.

**Filed H9:** Stick BLE stations (hill, pickup, respawn) are unproven over the air: the 6,000-tick host
test proves C++/JS parity, not a real Stick against a real phone. Gate: Block 9 in
[`bench-2026-09-24.md`](../bench-2026-09-24.md) (`dd6a66b2`), MUST for the Stick MVP, to run after the
0.4.11 hotfix ships. F332 (PM1 side-button registers) and F333 (remaining station screens) are untouched.
F314 stays open but is now post-MVP: no further Stick IR receive work until the MVP ships over BLE.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): S58 reworked after bench 3.3, the trigger pickup and the protected overshield

Bench 3.3 (brx2) overturned two mechanisms: a shield set past the `$PSET` max clamps back within 0.75 s, and a
`$BMAP` slot button fires its slot instead of selecting it. Tony chose the new shapes. A heavy goes straight onto the
trigger: the phone saves the current weapon's counts, re-sends the pickup slot's head `$WEAP` and `$AMMO`, SELECT
toggles back and forth (the phone does the equip), and an empty heavy or a death switches back with the saved counts.
ALT keeps its job, so Easy Reload players get pickups. The overshield is one burst: spawn protection on, `$PSET` with
the shield max raised, an absolute `$LIFE` from the pre-grant pools (hits during the grant are ignored, Tony's Halo
rule), protection off after 1 s, the preset `$PSET` back on drain or death. Three polish rounds found a reconcile that
destroyed a held heavy, a lost protection-off leaving a player unhittable, a lethal hit revived by the grant, lost
switch-back and restore writes, and stale trigger tracking after ALT. Desk only: bench steps 3.4 and 3.5 gate the flag.

### 2026-09-24 · brx3 · A60, phones rejoin Mission Control with no tap

Tony's two phones needed a JOIN tap on 0.4.11. The logs showed two causes:
- **Grey was a genuine first contact.** An uninstall had wiped its saved MC.
- **Green hit F203.** Its first launch after the update came while MC was restarting. The remembered dial missed its 10 s welcome, and one miss deleted the address for good.

A60 has three parts:
- **F203 is fixed.** A remembered MC is never forgotten on one timeout.
- **MC has an identity proof.** A persisted install secret gives each phone a trust key, issued once on request. An automatic dial withholds every secret and checks an HMAC proof before it processes anything.
- **Rejoin needs no tap.** A phone with exactly one proven candidate rejoins after an app restart, an app update, or an MC restart at the same or a new address.

A first contact still needs one tap (F346, Tony's call). Three polish rounds found six Mediums (enrolment fail-open and flooding, a user-named join being overridden, a trusted RECONNECT to an unproven host, the several-found count, row overflow), and all six are fixed. The relay residual is stated in node.md 3.19.

## 2026-09-24 (desk): F341, the 4545/7070 unkillable player, and F342, the station-game scan flood

Field evidence: a live match, app 0.4.11+f366156e, a Pixel 5, Tactix-FE30. At 20:37:42 the phone wrote the spawn burst
(8 frames, the first a `$PSET` scream take). At 20:37:43 one chunk of frame 1 failed with "status code 201", and
brxlink sent the burst again from frame 1. From then on every `$LIFE,0,0,0` read answered `$HP,4545,7070,0`, a hit
(`$HIR` token 5 = 15) left the pools unchanged, and the reconcile after a drop logged "live at hp 4545".

Root cause, verified against the code and the plugin source. Status 201 is Android's `ERROR_GATT_WRITE_REQUEST_BUSY`
(the plugin's API 33 path rejects when `writeCharacteristic` returns it): the stack did NOT send that chunk. It was the
`$PSET`'s last chunk, the one with the `*`. brxlink then re-sent the `$PSET` from its first byte, and the gun's parser
keeps tokens 1..59 across a `$` (transport-hardening.md §1.3), so it read hp "45"+"45" and armour "70"+"70". brx2's
reading had the append right and the delivery wrong: a delivered chunk leaves a complete frame and a harmless second
copy. A byte-level parser model (app/test/pool-readback.test.mjs, FakeTagger `feed_bytes`) reproduces 4545/7070 exactly.

Fixes: brxlink writes `$*` before the next frame to a gun after any chunk error or link drop, and a late error that lands
during a later batch marks that batch's current frame for a re-send. The node checks every `$HP`/`$LCD` against the armed
`$PSET` ceilings and repairs a pool above them (`$*`, the life's `$PSET`, `$TID`, `$LIFE,<pools>,1,*`), reads back, and
after two failed repairs or an unanswered read-back shows `pool_wrong` on MC. `$*` is a v4.32 code read: screamers A4
and the new Block 2 step 6 in [`bench-2026-09-24.md`](../bench-2026-09-24.md) are its bench proof.

F342: the flood was the beacon scan in a station game (60 results/s at arm time, 78 at scanMode 0 later). A respawn-only
station game now scans only while the player is down, and an in-match reconnect keeps the guard's lowered mode.

## 2026-09-24 (desk and bench, evening and overnight): brx4 — Stick MVP scope, the side-button lock, a host screen simulator, player-sim, and F353's desk half

All on `main` up to `03c21273`.

**Decisions (Tony).** Stick stations are Bluetooth-only for the MVP: hill, pickup and respawn. Stick IR receive and
the grenade hill stay post-MVP. KOTH's default `station_source` is `phone`. The Stick's default pickup/respawn
threshold is -57 dBm; a phone station keeps -70 dBm. Revive counting and the REDEPLOY animation are also post-MVP:
`REVIVE_FEEDBACK_ENABLED` is off, so a respawn Stick only advertises, and the phone's bit6 signal stays parked on
branch `revive-bit`. Pickups are the priority, and they must work with no Wi-Fi.

**Built.** `e65aea17` ports the phone's Bluetooth hill and respawn logic (the presence port). `f41f4b0f` fixes three
bugs: MC refused a Stick hello with no clock, so the Stick now borrows MC's clock for its envelope's `t` field; a
Wi-Fi retry flood; and lost serial bytes. `cce30581` stops an assigned station sending periodic IR (a respawn Stick
had beaconed IR into a live match). `97166982` republishes the advert on a game/id/kind/threshold change (phones had
ignored a Stick that kept advertising an old game). `8a8bcae9` plus `e17efa66` build F332's side-button lock: the
M5PM1 0x49 bit 0 and 0x4A bit 0 registers, from the M5PM1 manual v1.9, with a read-modify-write, a read-back, a
retry, an early-boot clear, and a 20 s loop watchdog while locked. Bit 7 (`DL_LOCK`) is never written. `e879b9db`
removes the claim RSSI floor for a pickup, matching the phone station (`4cbb416a`). `edef75c9` plus `e17efa66` build
a host screen simulator: it drives the real `station_render.h` inside an `LGFX_Sprite` through 46 scenarios and the
real state machines, found 14 wrong screens, and all 14 are fixed. `03c21273` adds `hardware/player-sim`, an ESP32
sketch that simulates a player advert and times the SCAN advert timer. `e17efa66`'s change set went through 3 polish
rounds (two review lenses in round 1).

**Proven unattended** (Stick firmware `e17efa66`; MC run natively on Windows, no Wi-Fi laptop bridge):

- mDNS discovery, status reporting (uptime, boot count, association), and the `RESTARTED` flag.
- The A58 lock round trip on the real PM1 registers: locked reads 2b/01, unlocked reads 2a/00, and a reboot
  mid-lock still reads 2a/00 at boot; 64 s locked ran with no reset.
- An offline pickup award against a simulated player, including a dead player's claim refused.
- F353's desk half: the Stick's own advert gaps measure a median of 106 ms and a max of 953 ms while scanning
  (hill at 50%), and a max of 488 ms idle; none exceeded 1 s. This used a continuous ESP32 scanner. A laptop's
  Windows scanner is not a usable instrument for this: it showed 3.8 s gaps on a plain 100 ms advertiser. Reading:
  the phones' "left" flips seen earlier are phone-side, not the Stick's advert.
- The Stick hears the simulated player at -36 dBm on the desk, so its receiver is fine; the phones' earlier -80s
  were distance and phone TX, not the Stick.

**Rig.** The rig receiver (CH343 5C93045958, COM7) was flashed to `player-sim`, then restored to `ir_capture`, and
verified against the Stick's silent words after the restore. The Stick itself is left on `e17efa66`, Wi-Fi set,
unlocked, and assigned as powerup id 5 by a throwaway MC, which will be stopped; after that it looks for MC by
mDNS.

Tony's decisions that shape it: the range edit is allowed during play and under the A58 match lock, entered by holding A for 5 s on the STATS page; every edit is flagged to MC in `range_edits`; and STRENGTH (the advertising TX power) steps ultra_low -18 dBm, low -9, medium 0, high +9 (the default).

The A67 contract and its two addenda are brx3's. The key rule is the absent-age guard: MC re-sends the same `station_config` as the A58 lock carrier and on every reconnect, and that re-send must not undo an operator's on-station edit. Without an age on MC's value, an edit is kept only when the re-sent value is unchanged from the last one MC sent; a genuinely changed value from MC still wins. A new game alone keeps the edit (the station and its placement have not changed); a new station kind or id resets it. `seq` (the edit log's counter) never goes down, so a release, a new session or a stale NVS copy cannot make MC re-announce an edit it already has.

Polish: three rounds (round 1 ran two lenses).

Machine-verified: MC pushing threshold -60 reached the Stick (status reports `-60` with `src mc`, and the advert's byte 14 reads `c4`); the defaults are unchanged where MC sends nothing (-57 dBm, TX power high).

NOT verified (Tony's list, all bench, none run yet): the 5 s hold gesture and the RANGE screens lit on a real device; whether `BLEDevice::setPower(..., ESP_BLE_PWR_TYPE_ADV)` changes only the advert's power or the whole controller's (and so the player scan's own transmissions); and what a STRENGTH change actually does to the range a phone sees.

brx3's MC half of A67 landed later the same day (1420057b). End to end with it (machine, native Windows MC): MC keeps the Stick's threshold/tx_power and their source; an assignment with no threshold made the Stick apply and report -57 (src mc); MC's -63 reached the Stick (status -63, advert byte 14 = c1). Still Tony's: the on-station gesture and a strength A/B.
